# Session Notes

Working notes carried between Claude Code sessions. Short-lived by design — delete or rotate entries once the work they describe has landed.

## Last commit

`1a4f5f2 feat(001-browser-os-v1): POSIX-perfect Vfs::resolve_nofollow (follow intermediate symlinks, leave final at its own ino) — pre-slice Vfs::resolve_nofollow was a literal don't-follow-anything walker that short-circuited on any SymLink component, so /linkdir/file with /linkdir → /realdir errored with NotADirectory. Post-slice it delegates to the existing resolve_inner helper with follow_last=false, matching POSIX lstat / O_NOFOLLOW semantics: intermediate symlinks always dereferenced, final component left at its own ino when it's a symlink. Body rewrite — signature unchanged. Callers automatically upgraded: Vfs::stat returns target's metadata for /linkdir/file; Vfs::readlink works through symlinked intermediate dirs; Kernel::path_open(lookup_flags=0) succeeds on paths with symlinked intermediate dirs; handle_path_filestat_get(lookup_flags=0) also gets POSIX-correct lstat semantics automatically. Bonus: resolve_inner's SYMLOOP_MAX-bounded loop detection now applies to resolve_nofollow too — intermediate-symlink loops surface as SymLoop → ELOOP (pre-slice there was no loop protection on resolve_nofollow at all). Semantics pinned: resolve_nofollow('/link') with /link → /target → /link's own ino (unchanged; lstat-on-final-symlink preserved); resolve_nofollow('/linkdir/file') with /linkdir → /realdir → /realdir/file's ino (new intermediate-follow behaviour); intermediate-symlink loop → SymLoop → ELOOP. 3 new Rust tests in tests/vfs.rs (intermediate-symlink-follow via resolve_nofollow, intermediate-loop → SymLoop, stat() returning target filetype + size for symlinked intermediate) + 1 new Rust test in tests/sys.rs (path_open_nofollow_traverses_intermediate_symlink companion to slice 6's follow-set variant; updates slice 6 test comment block since "must pass follow=1" is no longer true) + 1 new TS test end-to-end through kernel.wasm (PATH_FILESTAT_GET on /linkdir/file with lookup_flags=0 → FILETYPE.REGULAR_FILE; was -ENOTDIR pre-slice). All existing 52 vfs + 70 sys + other slice tests continue to pass — resolve_nofollow's behaviour on direct symlinks is unchanged. No Platform trait additions; no Filesystem trait additions; no Vfs API additions (resolve_nofollow signature unchanged, only body rewritten); no FdObject / NodeType / Socket variant additions; no new KernelError / FsError variants (reuses slice 1's SymLoop); no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no public-method signature changes. Unopened fd is EBADF. Kernel-side: handle_fd_tell lives between fd_seek and fd_close in syscall/wasi.rs; reuses args_u32 + kerr_to_errno. Handler is ten lines: look up fd (EBADF on miss), match entry.object on Vnode vs non-Vnode, return entry.offset wrapped in Response::ok. No Vfs query needed (unlike fd_seek's SeekEnd branch). No heap touched. No new errnos. TS shim: fd_tell in user-wasm-runtime.ts between fd_seek and path_open. Signature (lowered) (fd: i32, offset_ptr: i32) -> errno. Dispatches FD_TELL with arg0=fd (single u32, fits the arg0 path, no Uint8Array needed unlike fd_seek's 16-byte args packing); on success writes response.value as u64 LE at offset_ptr via setBigInt64. Shared constants: OP_WASI.FD_TELL: 0x0033 added between FD_SEEK and FD_READDIR (FD_READDIR stays as the "still unhandled" probe — known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys in tests/syscall.rs + the kernel-wasm-{host,entry}.test.ts ENOSYS probes all still point at FD_READDIR, unchanged).`

- `0437093` — PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES WASI shim + utimensat-style semantics (write-side sibling of the two filestat-get slices; directly unblocked by the 98765ee real-vnode-timestamps slice). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs`: new `fstflags` module with the four u16 bitfield constants `SET_ATIM=0x1`, `SET_ATIM_NOW=0x2`, `SET_MTIM=0x4`, `SET_MTIM_NOW=0x8`. The PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES opcode (0x0042) was already declared in the `--- path ---` block from prior scaffolding. `crates/kernel/src/vfs/mod.rs`: new Filesystem trait method `set_times(&mut self, ino: Ino, atime_ns: Option<NanosSinceEpoch>, mtime_ns: Option<NanosSinceEpoch>) -> Result<(), FsError>` with a default impl returning `FsError::ReadOnly` — splitting the fstflags decode in the dispatcher means the trait method takes already-materialised Option values (None = "leave this field unchanged") without the filesystem needing to see the WASI bitfield semantics. New Vfs::set_times wrapper at the public API: resolves the path first (so a missing path surfaces ENOENT even when both Options are None), then short-circuits to Ok(()) for the zero-effect case, otherwise dispatches to Filesystem::set_times. `crates/kernel/src/fs/tmpfs.rs`: overrides `set_times` — writes the Option values to the TmpEntry's atime_ns + mtime_ns fields directly, bumps ctime_ns = now() unconditionally (any actual touch is a metadata change). `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mod.rs`: overrides `set_times` — reads the inode from disk, updates the Option-pass fields on the in-memory copy, bumps ctime = now(), routes through `stage_inode_write` + Transaction + `commit_and_apply` so the new timestamps persist atomically with the journaled write. devfs + procfs don't override and inherit the trait's default EROFS response — consistent with how they reject create/mkdir/unlink (devfs is immutable; procfs content is synthesised per-read). `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains `op::PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES => handle_path_filestat_set_times(kernel, pid, req, heap)` between PATH_FILESTAT_GET and FD_PRESTAT_GET. New `handle_path_filestat_set_times` handler (90 lines): reads dir_fd (ignored) + lookup_flags (ignored) + fstflags from args; validates the mutually-exclusive pairs (SET_ATIM + SET_ATIM_NOW → EINVAL; same for mtim) BEFORE the heap read (invalid flag pairs short-circuit without needing a well-formed heap); reads atim + mtim + path from heap[0..16]+[16..] (len < 16 → EINVAL; non-UTF-8 path → EINVAL); materialises the Option<NanosSinceEpoch> values based on the fstflags bits — for SET_*_NOW the kernel substitutes `platform::current().now_realtime_ns()` at this layer, not at the filesystem; calls Vfs::set_times and maps the Result to Response::ok/err. 35-line doc block documents the wire layout + ctime-always-bumped contract + the heap-layout rationale (putting the two u64 timestamps in heap instead of args[8..] leaves room for fstflags + future growth while keeping dir_fd + lookup_flags at the same offsets as PATH_FILESTAT_GET). `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI.PATH_FILESTAT_SET_TIMES: 0x0042` added between PATH_FILESTAT_GET and PATH_OPEN with a 6-line doc block; new FSTFLAGS shared constant table mirrors abi::wasi::fstflags with the same four bit values; `ERRNO.EROFS: 69` added (already in the kernel errno.rs + kerr_to_errno table, just not previously mirrored for TS callers). `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: new path_filestat_set_times shim with the WASI-lowered signature `(dirfd, flags, path_ptr, path_len, atim, mtim, fstflags) -> errno`. Reads the path bytes from user memory (like path_filestat_get + path_open do), builds a 16+path_len Uint8Array heap buffer with setBigUint64(0, atim) + setBigUint64(8, mtim) + path at offset 16, packs dir_fd + lookup_flags + fstflags into the 16-byte args window, dispatches with no heap-out roundtrip. 22-line doc block documents the lowered signature + the single-roundtrip contract. Tests: 8 new Rust kernel-side dispatch tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` after the path_filestat_get block (request IDs 800..807): `path_filestat_set_times_sets_atim_and_mtim_on_tmpfs` (happy path with explicit values + asserts ctime bumps to "now" which is > the 3-digit-million test values we chose), `path_filestat_set_times_set_atim_now_stamps_current_wall_clock` (1ms sleep + SET_ATIM_NOW + asserts atim advanced past create-time + mtim stayed at create-time), `path_filestat_set_times_with_zero_flags_is_noop_success` (the permission-probe pattern — atim/mtim/ctime all unchanged), the two mutually-exclusive-pair EINVAL tests, ENOENT on missing path, EROFS on /dev/console (pins devfs's trait-default ReadOnly), `path_filestat_set_times_with_short_heap_returns_einval` (pins the heap_len >= 16 guard). New test helpers `path_filestat_set_times_args(dir_fd, lookup_flags, fstflags)` + `path_filestat_set_times_heap(atim, mtim, path)`. 6 new TS-unit dispatch tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` between PATH_FILESTAT_GET and FD_SEEK (request IDs 810..815): ENOENT, EROFS on /dev/console, both exclusive-pair EINVALs, short-heap EINVAL, zero-flags no-op success on /proc/version (the permission-probe pattern). The happy-path "atim actually landed" test is NOT duplicated on the TS side because the Rust tests pin that exhaustively against tmpfs + the TS dispatch surface can't stat a tmpfs file without a multi-step pipeline that would mostly re-prove what the kernel-side tests already pin. New test helpers `encodeSetTimesHeap(atim, mtim, path)` + `encodeSetTimesArgs(dirfd, lookup_flags, fstflags)`. `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probes (Rust + TS) unchanged — FD_READDIR is still unhandled. `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (+59 net lines; shim + opcode entry). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` gets +3 lines — ERRNO.EROFS was added to the shared table so both bundles pick it up, but kernel-worker doesn't dispatch the shim so only the errno entry differs. `dist/manifest.json` unchanged at 29 entries. Files: `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` (+12; fstflags module + 4 constants + 5-line doc block), `crates/kernel/src/vfs/mod.rs` (+40; Filesystem trait method + Vfs wrapper + doc blocks), `crates/kernel/src/fs/tmpfs.rs` (+19; set_times override), `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mod.rs` (+25; set_times override routing through journal), `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs` (+104; handler + dispatch arm + 35-line doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (+293; 8 tests + 2 helpers + 31-line doc block), `web/src/shared/syscall.ts` (+23; opcode + FSTFLAGS + EROFS + doc blocks), `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (+59; shim + 22-line doc block), `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` (+179; describe block with 6 tests + 2 helpers + 20-line doc block), `dist/assets/user-worker.js` (+59 net), `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` (+3 net; EROFS entry). Test deltas: Rust 912 → 920 (+8), TS-unit 336 → 342 (+6), TS-integration 2 → 2 unchanged (the kernel-side Rust tests pin every errno branch + the TS-unit tests pin the wire layout end-to-end through kernel.wasm; no new browser-specific signal). Total 1250 → 1264 (+14) passing, 0 failing across all layers. Both filestat-get + filestat-set-times opcodes now share a symmetric interface: get returns FileStat with real a/m/ctime; set updates a/mtim via Option lanes with ctime always bumped. Recommended next target: the genuinely larger WASI-surface **`poll_oneoff`** slice (subscription decoding + clock + fd-readable/writable + "at least one ready" contract; biggest-payoff single candidate but needs its own session). Alternative smaller WASI-surface slice: **`fd_renumber`** (dup2-style fd duplication; FdTable::renumber surface; small). Alternative Vfs-expansion slice: **`path_rename` / `path_unlink_file`** (filesystem-mutation opcodes; need Vfs::rename + Vfs::unlink additions).
- `98765ee` — real vnode timestamps via Platform::now_realtime_ns (cross-cutting vfs slice; touches every filesystem impl). Pre-slice, every vfs entry reported `atime_ns = mtime_ns = ctime_ns = 0` regardless of whether it had just been created or hadn't been touched in a week — the three fields on `FileStat` existed but were hard-coded. This slice fills them in with wall-clock values sourced from `Platform::now_realtime_ns()`, threaded through via direct `platform::current().now_realtime_ns()` calls inside each filesystem impl (no new Filesystem trait method; no new Platform trait method — both surfaces stay byte-identical). Touch-on-write semantics (relaxed POSIX + noatime): create/mkdir stamp all three times with now(); write/truncate update mtime + ctime; read is deliberately not a touch in v1 — the write-amplification tradeoff isn't worth it until a later slice opts into relatime. Per-filesystem: `crates/kernel/src/fs/tmpfs.rs` gets three new `NanosSinceEpoch` fields on `TmpEntry` (atime_ns, mtime_ns, ctime_ns), stamped at both `TmpFs::new()` for the root dir and at create/mkdir for every new entry, updated in-place on write/truncate + stat reports them directly; `crates/kernel/src/fs/devfs.rs` gets a single `created_at_ns` field on `DevFs` captured at `DevFs::new()` that every entry (including the /dev root) reports for all three times — devfs is immutable at runtime so timestamps are stable across calls (pinned by a `devfs_stat_is_stable_across_calls` test that sleeps 1ms between stats and asserts equal mtime); `crates/kernel/src/fs/procfs.rs` re-reads now() per stat() call so the timestamp matches the freshness of the synthesised content (uptime/loadavg/meminfo all change per-call anyway); `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mod.rs` already had atime_ns/mtime_ns/ctime_ns slots on the on-disk inode (layout.rs predated this slice) — create/mkdir previously hard-coded 0 and write/truncate left them untouched, now each lane stamps with now() and the updates route through `stage_inode_write` → journal → apply so the timestamp persists atomically with the payload it stamps across the commit/remount cycle. `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mkfs.rs` also seeds the root inode with now() at mkfs time so a freshly-made opfs image reads back a non-zero stat for `/`. Tiny `now_ns()` helper in tmpfs.rs + opfs/mod.rs pulls the full `platform::current().now_realtime_ns()` chain into one line at call sites (5 call sites in tmpfs, 3 in opfs). No Platform trait changes; no Filesystem trait changes; no FileStat field additions (the slots were already there, just never populated); no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (the two filestat-get shims already threaded atime_ns/mtime_ns/ctime_ns through to the WASI 64-byte filestat_t wire format verbatim; the slice is purely semantic fill-in of already-existing slots). Tests: 7 new in `crates/kernel/tests/vfs.rs` covering tmpfs create-nonzero + tmpfs mkdir-nonzero + tmpfs write-advances-mtime-ctime-leaves-atime (with a 1ms `std::thread::sleep` to ensure the realtime clock advances past create-time) + tmpfs truncate-advances-mtime-ctime-leaves-atime + devfs-nonzero-and-self-consistent (atime=mtime=ctime since all three share the mount-time snapshot) + devfs-stable-across-calls (the immutable-vs-procfs contract pin) + procfs-nonzero. 5 new in `crates/kernel/tests/opfs.rs` covering opfs create-nonzero + opfs mkdir-nonzero + opfs write-advances-mtime-ctime-leaves-atime + opfs truncate-advances-mtime-ctime + the critical `opfs_timestamps_survive_remount` pin (mkfs → create → write → stat → sync → into_device → mount → stat, asserting timestamps byte-for-byte match — catches any slice that forgets to route the timestamp update through `stage_inode_write` or the journal). One existing test updated in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: `path_filestat_get_on_tmpfs_regular_file_returns_filetype_and_size` now asserts `atim/mtim/ctim > 0` instead of `== 0` (10-line explanatory comment about why native-platform realtime is always nonzero). `fd_filestat_get_on_char_device_fd_returns_filetype_char_device` still asserts times=0 unchanged — CharDevice fds are non-Vnode, their filestat is synthesised by handle_fd_filestat_get directly (not via Vfs::stat_ino), so the 0 hard-codes stay. `dist/*` byte-identical (kernel-only slice; no TS shim changes, no new opcodes, no manifest changes). Files: `crates/kernel/src/fs/tmpfs.rs` (+51/-7; 3 new fields + 5 call sites touching timestamps + now_ns helper), `crates/kernel/src/fs/devfs.rs` (+28/-8; created_at_ns field + stat population), `crates/kernel/src/fs/procfs.rs` (+26/-6; now() per stat call), `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mod.rs` (+31/-12; 3 call sites touching timestamps + now_ns helper), `crates/kernel/src/fs/opfs/mkfs.rs` (+4/-3; root seed), `crates/kernel/tests/vfs.rs` (+116; 7 tests + 22-line doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/opfs.rs` (+84; 5 tests + 8-line doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (+11/-3; one test rewritten). Test deltas: Rust 900 → 912 (+12; 7 vfs + 5 opfs), TS-unit 336 → 336 unchanged (kernel-only slice; the filestat shim already pipes fields through — the only TS test that pinned a specific timestamp was for a CharDevice fd which this slice deliberately doesn't touch), TS-integration 2 → 2 unchanged (timestamp-reporting is kernel-synthesised; the existing Playwright flow verifies the end-to-end boot + display flow but doesn't stat a file for timestamps). Total 1238 → 1250 (+12) passing, 0 failing across all layers. Unblocks `path_filestat_set_times` (the next slice — writing real timestamps via utimensat-style calls is cheap now that the read side has real values to ratchet from). Recommended next target: the narrow WASI-surface **`path_filestat_set_times`** slice (write-side sibling; set atim/mtim via `utimensat` family; unblocked by this slice and small — one handler + one TS shim). Alternative single-larger-WASI-opcode slice: **poll_oneoff**. Alternative narrower WASI-surface slice: **fd_renumber**.
- `17e5985` — pending-Connecting-client cleanup on listener drop (kernel-correctness polish; narrow IPC-state-machine slice). `crates/kernel/src/ipc/mod.rs`: `IpcTable::close_socket` gains a backlog-drain step — when the closing socket is in `SocketState::Listening`, its `backlog: VecDeque<SocketId>` is drained into a local `Vec<SocketId>` and each pending client is transitioned to `SocketState::Closed` (reusing the previously-unused enum variant) with `peer` left as None. The drain happens under a scoped mut borrow (snapshotted into `drained: Vec<SocketId>`) so the subsequent per-client state transitions can take fresh mut borrows without the borrow checker complaining. `send_on_socket` gains a new guard at the top of the peer-lookup block: `if sender.state == SocketState::Closed { return Err(IpcError::ConnectionRefused) }` fires BEFORE the existing `state != Connected → InvalidState` check, so drained clients now surface `ConnectionRefused` (→ ECONNREFUSED via the existing `KernelError::ConnectionRefused → ECONNREFUSED` entry in `kerr_to_errno`) rather than `InvalidState` → EINVAL. The existing `Connecting → InvalidState` branch is untouched, which preserves the accept-race retry window display-client-demo's EINVAL-retry loop walks during the normal handshake. `recv_on_socket` is unchanged — fd_read on a drained client still returns `InvalidState`/EINVAL, consistent with the other non-Connected states; the slice only covers the fd_write path the user handoff called out. Semantics rationale: the listener-drop case is semantically "the server you tried to reach is gone" (→ ECONNREFUSED), distinct from the accept-race case "the server is alive but hasn't accepted yet" (→ EAGAIN-or-EINVAL retry window). Before this slice the two cases were both EINVAL; after, only the accept-race is EINVAL and the listener-drop is ECONNREFUSED. 22-line doc block added on `close_socket` documents the drain + state=Closed + ECONNREFUSED contract. No new errno (ECONNREFUSED already in the errno table + already mapped via `KernelError::ConnectionRefused` from the `ipc_connect` path), no SocketState additions (`Closed` was already declared in the enum, just never transitioned into pre-slice), no Platform trait changes, no FdEntry/FdTable surface changes, no wire-format changes. `crates/kernel/tests/ipc.rs`: 5 new tests in a new "Listener drop with pending Connecting clients" block between `socket_close_unbinds_the_path` and the `paired()` helper: `socket_close_listener_drains_pending_connecting_client_to_closed_state` (pins the single-client drain → state=Closed + peer=None contract), `socket_close_listener_drains_all_pending_connecting_clients` (three concurrent pending clients, verifies every one gets drained), `socket_send_on_drained_client_returns_connection_refused` (pins the new send_on_socket branch), `socket_send_on_connecting_client_with_live_listener_still_invalid_state` (the accept-race invariant this slice must NOT disturb — explicitly asserts that sending on a Connecting client with a live listener still returns InvalidState, so the display-client-demo retry loop is not broken), `socket_close_listener_with_empty_backlog_is_unchanged` (pins the "no change in the empty-backlog path" contract). `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: 1 new end-to-end test in the IPC section after the `ipc_round_trip` test: `fd_write_on_pending_client_after_listener_drop_returns_econnrefused` (full flow through the dispatcher: server IPC_SOCKET + IPC_BIND + IPC_LISTEN; client IPC_SOCKET + IPC_CONNECT; pre-drop FD_WRITE asserts EINVAL; server FD_CLOSE on listener fd; post-drop FD_WRITE asserts ECONNREFUSED). Request IDs 300..307. 22-line doc block documents the kernel-correctness guarantee display-client-demo now relies on. `display-client-demo/src/main.rs` unchanged — the EINVAL retry loop stays in place because it still handles the accept-race (state=Connecting with live listener); my slice changes the listener-drop case from EINVAL to ECONNREFUSED, which makes the retry loop exit cleanly via the `std::process::exit(11)` path rather than spinning to MAX_POLLS. Eliminating the retry loop entirely would require accept-write race-freedom in the kernel — a larger slice outside this one's scope. `dist/*` byte-identical (kernel-only slice; no TS shim changes, no new opcodes, no new manifest entries). No `crates/abi` changes, no `web/` changes. Files: `crates/kernel/src/ipc/mod.rs` (+40/-2; drain step + state=Closed branch in send + 22-line close_socket doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/ipc.rs` (+122; 5 tests + 16-line doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (+160; end-to-end test + 22-line doc block). Test deltas: Rust 894 → 900 (+6; 5 in ipc.rs + 1 in syscall.rs), TS-unit 336 → 336 (no change; this is a kernel-only slice — the IPC state machine lives entirely in Rust and no TS shim needed updating), TS-integration 2 → 2 (the existing concurrent Playwright flow always accepts before exit, so the new drain path isn't exercised; kernel-side tests pin the semantic exhaustively). Total 1232 → 1238 (+6) passing, 0 failing across all layers. Recommended next target per the runway: the cross-cutting **real vnode timestamps** slice (touches tmpfs + devfs + procfs + opfs; threads `Platform::now_realtime_ns` into vnode metadata via a vfs side-channel with touch-on-write semantics so both filestat-get variants stop reporting zeros + unblocks path_filestat_set_times as a follow-up). Alternative WASI-surface candidate: **poll_oneoff** (biggest-payoff single opcode but genuinely needs its own session). Alternative narrower: **fd_renumber** (dup2-style fd duplication; small).
- `a98ce64` — fd-state opcodes bundle (fd_advise + fd_allocate + fd_sync + fd_datasync; four related WASI fd-state opcodes in one slice). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` unchanged — all four opcodes (FD_ADVISE=0x0020, FD_ALLOCATE=0x0021, FD_DATASYNC=0x0023, FD_SYNC=0x0032) were already declared alongside FD_SEEK + FD_TELL from prior scaffolding. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains four arms (`op::FD_ADVISE => handle_fd_advise(kernel, pid, req)`, `op::FD_ALLOCATE => handle_fd_allocate(...)`, `op::FD_SYNC => handle_fd_sync(...)`, `op::FD_DATASYNC => handle_fd_datasync(...)`) between FD_TELL and FD_CLOSE (no `heap` parameter — these are no-heap-roundtrip opcodes like fd_tell; responses carry 0 or errno, no value payload). Four new handlers model exactly on handle_fd_tell's shape: read fd via `args_u32(req, 0)`, look up via `kernel.fds(pid)?.get(fd)?` (EBADF on miss), match on `entry.object` — Vnode → `Response::ok(req.request_id, 0)` for advise/sync/datasync or `Response::err(req.request_id, ENOTSUP)` for allocate; every other FdObject variant → EINVAL via the `_ =>` arm. Ten-line handlers each + one shared 22-line doc block covering the four opcodes' collapsed semantics. `ENOTSUP` import added to the errno use-list (EBADF + EINVAL were already there). No Vfs query (unlike fd_seek's SeekEnd branch), no heap touched, no FdEntry/FdTable changes, no new errnos (ENOTSUP already in the kerr_to_errno table). No Platform-trait changes. Semantics rationale: v1's tmpfs-backed VFS has no advisor (fd_advise is a hint WASI permits ignoring), no write-buffer (fd_sync/fd_datasync have nothing to flush — tmpfs/devfs/procfs are in-memory; opfs flushes per-write at the block-driver layer), and no preallocation primitive (fd_allocate returns ENOTSUP because a success response would lie about reserved space). The EINVAL non-Vnode guard fires before the ENOTSUP in fd_allocate's case — a char device has no preallocation meaning at all, so EINVAL is tighter than ENOTSUP. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI` gains `FD_ADVISE: 0x0020`, `FD_ALLOCATE: 0x0021`, `FD_SYNC: 0x0032`, `FD_DATASYNC: 0x0023` with a shared 5-line doc block documenting the wire-format identity + collapsed-semantics contract. `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: four new shims between fd_tell and path_open. All four use the arg0-single-u32 dispatch path (mirroring clock_res_get + fd_tell — no Uint8Array packing needed). fd_advise signature is `(fd, offset, len, advice) -> errno` with all four non-fd args prefixed `_` (ignored); fd_allocate is `(fd, offset, len) -> errno` with offset + len prefixed; fd_sync + fd_datasync are `(fd) -> errno`. Shared 14-line doc block + per-shim 8-line doc blocks. Tests: 12 new Rust kernel-side dispatch tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` after the fd_tell block (request IDs 760..771): per opcode, three tests covering Vnode-success-or-ENOTSUP + non-Vnode-EINVAL (CharDevice via install_fd + DEV_CONSOLE) + unopened-fd EBADF. New `fd_advise_args(fd)` test helper packs the 16-byte inline args window (fd at 0..4, zeros elsewhere — the offset/len/advice bytes are semantically ignored in v1). The Vnode-success tests for fd_advise + fd_allocate also pin the read-only-on-offset contract via `k.fds(pid).unwrap().offset(fd).unwrap() == 0` after dispatch. 10 new TS-unit dispatch tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` between FD_TELL and PROC_SPAWN (request IDs 790..799): `dispatch: FD_ADVISE` block (3 tests) + `dispatch: FD_ALLOCATE` block (3 tests) covering all three paths each via openProcVersion + installConsoleFd; `dispatch: FD_SYNC` + `dispatch: FD_DATASYNC` blocks (2 tests each; Vnode-success + non-Vnode-EINVAL; EBADF-path is identical to advise/allocate and omitted to avoid signal duplication). Reuses `openProcVersion` helper from the FD_SEEK block verbatim — no new helpers needed. The `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe in tests/syscall.rs + the kernel-wasm-host.test.ts + kernel-wasm-entry.test.ts ENOSYS probes all still point at FD_READDIR (unchanged — FD_READDIR is genuinely still unhandled, next natural sentinel). `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (+95 net lines; four new shims + four new OP_WASI entries bundled). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` byte-identical (kernel-worker runs kernel.wasm + doesn't dispatch shims — only user-worker does; same dead-code-elimination pattern prior WASI-shim slices documented). `dist/manifest.json` entry count unchanged at 29 (no new shipped assets; kernel.wasm regenerates but is not tracked). Files: `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs` (+86; 4 handlers + 4 dispatch arms + 22-line shared doc block + ENOTSUP import), `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (+270; 12 tests + fd_advise_args helper + 20-line shared doc block), `web/src/shared/syscall.ts` (+9; 4 OP_WASI entries + shared doc), `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (+100; 4 shims + shared + per-shim doc blocks), `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` (+167; 4 describe blocks with 10 tests + 14-line shared doc block), `dist/assets/user-worker.js` (+95 net; 4 shims + 4 opcodes bundled). Test deltas: Rust 882 → 894 (+12 native tests; 3 per opcode covering Vnode + non-Vnode + EBADF), TS-unit 326 → 336 (+10 dispatch tests; advise + allocate get full 3-path coverage, sync + datasync get 2-path coverage since the EBADF path is identical to advise/allocate and adds no signal), TS-integration 2 → 2 unchanged (fd-state is kernel-synthesised, identical in vitest/node + Chromium — a Playwright assertion would duplicate signal without adding browser-specific coverage). Total 1211 → 1232 (+21) passing, 0 failing across all layers. Textbook mirror of the six prior WASI-surface slices' three-layer shape + TDD cadence; the seven-slice cadence CLOCK_TIME_GET + CLOCK_RES_GET + FD_FILESTAT_GET + PATH_FILESTAT_GET + FD_SEEK + FD_TELL + {fd_advise,fd_allocate,fd_sync,fd_datasync} now covers the full seekable-file + metadata + clock + fd-state surface area std binaries + libc-style C callers reach for. Recommended next target per the runway: pick a WASI-surface slice from {poll_oneoff (the genuinely larger one; unblocks mio-based userland + std::net-like patterns), path_rename / path_unlink_file (filesystem-mutation opcodes — need Vfs::rename + Vfs::unlink additions)} OR the cross-cutting **real vnode timestamps** slice (unblocks path_filestat_set_times after) OR the kernel-correctness **pending-Connecting-client cleanup on listener drop** slice.
- `6d429d9` — FD_TELL WASI shim + read-only seek-position query (the trivial Cur-0 fold of fd_seek as its own opcode; tightest WASI-surface slice to date, strictly smaller than fd_seek was). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` unchanged — `FD_TELL: 0x0033` was already declared alongside `FD_SEEK: 0x0031` from prior scaffolding. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains `op::FD_TELL => handle_fd_tell(kernel, pid, req)` between `FD_SEEK` and `FD_CLOSE` (no `heap` parameter — fd_tell is a no-heap-roundtrip opcode like fd_seek + clock_time_get; the u64 offset travels back in `response.value`). New `handle_fd_tell` handler: reads fd via `args_u32(req, 0)`, looks up the fd via `kernel.fds(pid)?.get(fd)?` (EBADF on miss), matches on `entry.object` — Vnode → `Response::ok(req.request_id, entry.offset as i64)`; every other FdObject variant → EINVAL via the `_ =>` arm of a one-arm match. Ten-line handler + twenty-line doc block documenting the read-only contract + the "strictly cheaper than fd_seek(Cur 0)" rationale + the non-Vnode-EINVAL guard (shared with fd_seek). No Vfs query (unlike fd_seek's SeekEnd branch). No heap touched. No FdEntry/FdTable changes. No new errnos (EBADF + EINVAL already in the kerr_to_errno table). No Platform-trait changes. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI.FD_TELL: 0x0033` added between `FD_SEEK` and `FD_READDIR`; six-line doc block mirrors FD_SEEK's wire-format identity + documents the "read-only sibling of fd_seek, Cur-0 fold, arg0 single u32" contract. `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: new fd_tell shim between fd_seek and path_open. Signature `(fd: i32, offset_ptr: i32) -> errno: i32`. Dispatches FD_TELL with `arg0=fd` (single u32 fits the arg0 path — no Uint8Array packing like fd_seek's 16-byte args; mirrors clock_res_get's arg0 path verbatim); on success writes the response i64 to user memory at offset_ptr via `setBigInt64(offsetPtr, response.value, true)` (little-endian, bit-exact through the i64↔u64 reinterpretation — same trick clock_time_get + clock_res_get + fd_seek all use for their u64 results). Fifteen-line shim counting the doc block. Tests: 4 new Rust kernel-side dispatch tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` after the fd_seek block (request IDs 750..753): `fd_tell_on_fresh_file_returns_initial_offset_zero` (id 750 — opens a tmpfs Vnode fd via make_proc_with_file_fd, asserts response.value == 0 + read-only contract: offset unchanged after the call), `fd_tell_after_fd_seek_returns_the_sought_position` (750/751 — the integration check that fd_tell sees what fd_seek wrote; SeekSet 5 then fd_tell returns 5; reuses the fd_seek_args + make_proc_with_file_fd helpers from the fd_seek block), `fd_tell_on_char_device_fd_returns_einval` (752 — installs CharDevice(DEV_CONSOLE) via install_fd, pins the non-Vnode rejection branch), `fd_tell_on_invalid_fd_returns_ebadf` (753). 3 new TS-unit dispatch tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` between FD_SEEK and PROC_SPAWN (request IDs 780..783): `returns 0 initially for a freshly-opened /proc/version Vnode fd` (780 — opens /proc/version via PATH_OPEN through the dispatch surface alone, using the `openProcVersion` helper lifted from the fd_seek block), `returns the seek'd offset after a SeekSet via FD_SEEK` (781/782 — the integration check: SeekSet 6 then FD_TELL returns 6; reuses `encodeFdSeekArgs` from the fd_seek block), `returns -EINVAL for a /dev/console fd` (783 — non-Vnode rejection). No new helpers needed — the describe block reuses openProcVersion + encodeFdSeekArgs verbatim. The `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probes in tests/syscall.rs + kernel-wasm-{host,entry}.test.ts stay on FD_READDIR (still genuinely unhandled; the lineage comment already notes the probe will swap when a slice claims FD_READDIR). `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (+38 net lines; FD_TELL opcode entry + fd_tell shim). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` byte-identical (the FD_TELL opcode is dead-code-eliminated from the kernel bundle since kernel-worker runs kernel.wasm + doesn't dispatch the shim — only user-worker does; same pattern the prior WASI-shim slices documented). `dist/manifest.json` entry count unchanged at 29 (no new shipped assets; kernel.wasm regenerates but is not tracked). Files: `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs` (+38; handler + dispatch arm + 20-line doc block), `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (+114; 4 tests + 16-line doc block), `web/src/shared/syscall.ts` (+6; OP_WASI entry + doc), `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (+33; shim + 15-line doc block), `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` (+63; describe block with 3 tests + 7-line doc block), `dist/assets/user-worker.js` (+38 net; shim bundled). Test deltas: Rust 878 → 882 (+4 native tests covering initial-offset + after-seek + non-Vnode-EINVAL + EBADF), TS-unit 323 → 326 (+3 dispatch tests covering initial + after-seek + non-Vnode), TS-integration 2 → 2 unchanged (fd_tell is kernel-synthesised, identical in vitest/node + Chromium — a Playwright assertion would duplicate the vitest signal without adding browser-specific coverage). Total 1203 → 1211 (+7) passing, 0 failing across all layers. Textbook mirror of the five prior WASI-surface slices' three-layer shape + TDD cadence; the six-slice cadence CLOCK_TIME_GET + CLOCK_RES_GET + FD_FILESTAT_GET + PATH_FILESTAT_GET + FD_SEEK + FD_TELL now covers the entire seekable-file + metadata + clock surface area std binaries reach for. `std::fs::File::stream_position` still lowers through fd_seek(Cur 0) (rustc's std doesn't call fd_tell directly — WASI libc-style ftell() would, but std has always folded through fd_seek), so the slice's payoff is the strictly-cheaper kernel path for C-FFI libc callers + the WASI-opcode-completeness win rather than a new userland capability. Recommended next target per the runway: pick another remaining WASI-surface slice from {fd_advise / fd_allocate / fd_sync / fd_datasync (related fd-state opcodes, 4 handlers in one session), poll_oneoff (the genuinely larger one), path_rename / path_unlink_file (filesystem-mutation opcodes — need Vfs additions but small on the WASI side)} OR the cross-cutting **real vnode timestamps** slice OR the kernel-correctness **pending-Connecting-client cleanup on listener drop** slice.
- `b486b87` — FD_SEEK WASI shim + per-fd seek-position mutation (file-position mutation half of the WASI seekable-file pair; same three-layer shape as the prior CLOCK_TIME_GET + CLOCK_RES_GET + FD_FILESTAT_GET + PATH_FILESTAT_GET cadence). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` unchanged — `FD_SEEK: 0x0031` + `Whence` enum (Set=0, Cur=1, End=2) + `FD_READDIR: 0x002F` were all already there. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains `op::FD_SEEK => handle_fd_seek(kernel, pid, req)` between `FD_READ` and `FD_CLOSE` (no `heap` parameter — fd_seek is a no-heap-roundtrip opcode like clock_time_get; the new offset travels back in `response.value` rather than via heap_out_mut). New `handle_fd_seek` handler reads fd from `args_u32(req, 0)`, whence from `(args_u32(req, 4) & 0xff) as u8`, offset from `args_u64(req, 8) as i64` (the only new args-decoding pattern in this slice — args_u64 already existed for PROC_SPAWN's CapSet, just first WASI use). Looks up the fd via `kernel.fds(pid)?.get(fd)?` (EBADF on miss); rejects non-Vnode FdObject variants with EINVAL via the `_ =>` arm of a one-arm `match entry.object`. For Vnode, branches on whence_byte against the Whence enum's u8 discriminants (no unsafe transmute — pattern arms are `x if x == abi::wasi::Whence::Set as u8 =>` etc.; future enum variants force a matching arm here): Set returns offset as u64 (EINVAL if offset < 0); Cur returns `entry.offset.checked_add_signed(offset)` or EINVAL on underflow past 0; End queries `kernel.vfs.stat_ino(mount_id, ino)?.size.checked_add_signed(offset)` with the same EINVAL underflow rule; any other whence value is EINVAL. Then `kernel.fds_mut(pid)?.set_offset(fd, new_offset)` and `Response::ok(req.request_id, new_offset as i64)`. 32-line doc block documents the wire layout + the per-fd-not-per-vnode contract + the seek-past-EOF-allowed rule + the fd_tell idiom (Cur 0 returns current position unchanged) + the response-value vs heap-roundtrip choice (mirrors clock_time_get for the same reason — one u64 result, no heap setup needed). DO NOT touch fd_read / fd_write — they stay unchanged; the only interaction is that after fd_seek lands, fd_read(fd, len) starts reading from the seek'd position, which is what FdEntry.offset semantically meant all along. No new errnos (EBADF + EINVAL already in the kerr_to_errno table). No new state on FdEntry, no Platform-trait changes. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: OP_WASI.FD_SEEK comment updated from "still unhandled probe" to "wire-format identity for fd_seek"; OP_WASI.FD_READDIR added as the new "still unhandled" probe (the comment migrates verbatim); WHENCE constant table added mirroring abi::wasi::Whence with SET=0/CUR=1/END=2. `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: new fd_seek shim between fd_read and path_open. Signature `(fd: i32, offset: i64, whence: i32, new_offset_ptr: i32) -> errno: i32` (whence comes AFTER offset in the WASI lowered signature; the kernel-side wire packs them differently — whence at args[4..8] before offset at args[8..16] — because the wire layout is u32-aligned-friendly and the shim absorbs the order swap). Builds a 16-byte Uint8Array via `args` (not arg0 — whence + offset don't fit a single u32) with `setUint32(0, fd)` + `setUint32(4, whence)` + `setBigInt64(8, offset)`; dispatches with no heap; on success writes the response i64 to user memory at new_offset_ptr via `setBigInt64(newOffsetPtr, response.value, true)` (the i64 bit pattern is identical to the u64 absolute offset — bit-exact through the i64↔u64 reinterpretation, same trick clock_time_get + clock_res_get use for their u64 ns timestamps). 22-line doc block documents the lowered signature + the whence/offset packing order + the bit-exactness note. Tests: 10 new Rust kernel-side dispatch tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` between path_filestat_get + fd_prestat_get (request IDs 730..740): `fd_seek_set_advances_to_absolute_offset` (id 730), `fd_seek_set_with_negative_offset_returns_einval` (731), `fd_seek_cur_with_zero_returns_current_position` (732 — pre-seeds offset 4 then asserts response.value=4 via Cur 0), `fd_seek_cur_with_negative_offset_back_past_zero_returns_einval` (733 — pre-seeds offset 2 then Cur -5 underflows), `fd_seek_end_with_zero_returns_file_size` (734), `fd_seek_end_with_negative_offset_seeks_into_file` (735 — End -3 on a 10-byte file = offset 7), `fd_seek_with_invalid_whence_returns_einval` (736 — whence=99), `fd_seek_on_invalid_fd_returns_ebadf` (737), `fd_seek_on_char_device_fd_returns_einval` (738 — pins the non-Vnode rejection branch), `fd_seek_then_fd_read_reads_from_new_position` (739/740 — the integration test that pins the actual reason fd_seek exists; SeekSet 4 then fd_read 4 returns "efgh" not "abcd"). New `fd_seek_args(fd, whence, offset)` test helper packs the inline args window. New `make_proc_with_file_fd(k, name, path, bytes)` test helper sets up a Vnode fd against a fresh tmpfs file (used by 7 of the 10 tests; the EBADF + char-device + invalid-whence-on-vnode cases use it via Default::default()-style minimal setup). 5 new TS-unit dispatch tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` between PATH_FILESTAT_GET + PROC_SPAWN (request IDs 770..776): `returns -EBADF for an unopened fd` (770), `returns -EINVAL for a /dev/console fd` (771 — non-Vnode rejection), `SeekSet on a /proc/version Vnode fd returns the new absolute offset` (772 — opens /proc/version via PATH_OPEN through the dispatch surface alone, no fixture pipeline), `SeekCur 0 reports the current offset (the fd_tell idiom)` (773/774 — chains SeekSet 4 then SeekCur 0 then asserts response.value=4), `SeekEnd 0 returns the file size of /proc/version` (775/776 — cross-checks via FD_FILESTAT_GET so the test isn't hardcoding StaticProcFsSource.version() length). New `encodeFdSeekArgs(fd, whence, offset)` + `openProcVersion(host, pid)` test helpers at module scope. The `known_wasi_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` probe in tests/syscall.rs + the `returns -ENOSYS for a known WASI opcode without a handler` probes in kernel-wasm-host.test.ts + kernel-wasm-entry.test.ts all swap from FD_SEEK to FD_READDIR (the new natural "still unhandled" sentinel; the `(This probe was FD_SEEK before that handler landed; before that, CLOCK_TIME_GET. ...)` comment threads the lineage). Test counts: Rust 868 → 878 (+10), TS-unit 318 → 323 (+5), TS-integration 2 → 2 (no change), total 1188 → 1203 (+15). Recommended next target per the runway: pick another WASI-surface slice from {fd_tell (the trivial Cur 0 fold — own opcode 0x0033, own kernel handler + test block), fd_advise / fd_allocate / fd_sync (related fd-state opcodes), poll_oneoff (the genuinely larger one)} OR the cross-cutting **real vnode timestamps** slice OR the kernel-correctness **pending-Connecting-client cleanup on listener drop** slice.
- `7fcdec2` — PATH_FILESTAT_GET WASI shim + path-based filestat_t synthesis (path-based sibling to 23f378a; unblocks `std::fs::metadata(path)` for userland std binaries — the fd variant already covered `File::metadata` but a lot of std code lowers through the path variant directly). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs` unchanged — the `PATH_FILESTAT_GET: 0x0041` constant + `filestat` offsets + `filetype` byte namespace were all already there from the 23f378a slice. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains `op::PATH_FILESTAT_GET => handle_path_filestat_get(kernel, pid, req, heap)` between `FD_FILESTAT_GET` and `FD_PRESTAT_GET`. New `handle_path_filestat_get` handler mirrors `handle_fd_filestat_get`'s filestat_t output shape + reuses the `filetype_from_nodetype(st.ty)` helper verbatim. Input side mirrors `handle_path_open`'s path decoding: `heap_in(req, heap)` → `core::str::from_utf8(path_bytes)` (EINVAL on decode failure); then `kernel.vfs.resolve(path)` → `(mount_id, ino)` with `FsError::NotFound` mapping to ENOENT via `kerr_to_errno(KernelError::Fs(..))`; then `kernel.vfs.stat_ino(mount_id, ino)` → `FileStat` (so dev=mount_id.0 as u64 matches the fd-variant's Vnode branch instead of hardcoding 0 like it would if we'd called `Vfs::stat(path)` directly, which throws away the mount id). Writes the 64 bytes into `heap_out_mut(req, heap, fs_off::SIZE)` via the same eight copy_from_slice calls fd_filestat_get uses at the pinned offsets; returns `extra_len: fs_off::SIZE as u32`. 41-line doc block documents the wire layout + the input-and-output-share-the-heap-ptr convention + the "dir_fd + flags are ignored in v1" contract. `dir_fd` (args[0..4]) + lookup flags (args[4..8]) are part of the documented wire layout but ignored in v1: PMos has no preopens (every path is absolute) and the v1 VFS doesn't follow symlinks either way, so the `LOOKUP_SYMLINK_FOLLOW=0x1` bit is a no-op regardless of its value. Kernel reads both u32s with underscore-prefixed names (`_dir_fd`, `_lookup_flags`) to pin the layout without unused-variable warnings; a future slice that wires preopens or symlink following decodes them without a wire-format break. `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: 6 new tests in a new "path_filestat_get" block between the fd_filestat_get + fd_prestat_get blocks, with a `path_filestat_args(dir_fd, flags)` helper that packs two u32s into the 16-byte args window. Reuses the `filestat_u64` helper from the 23f378a slice verbatim. `path_filestat_get_on_tmpfs_regular_file_returns_filetype_and_size` (vfs.create("/probe.txt") + vfs.write(24-byte payload) + resolve → filetype=4, dev=mount_id.0, ino=vnode's ino, size=24, nlink=1, times=0, + byte-by-byte check that heap[17..24] is all zero alignment padding), `path_filestat_get_on_tmpfs_directory_returns_filetype_directory` (vfs.mkdir("/adir") + resolve → filetype=3, size=0), `path_filestat_get_on_dev_console_returns_filetype_char_device` (resolve "/dev/console" via the devfs mount → filetype=2, dev=devfs-mount-id, size=0, nlink=1), `path_filestat_get_on_missing_path_returns_enoent` ("/nonexistent/path" → -ENOENT, extra_len=0, + explicit assertion that the heap bytes at [0..path.len()] are untouched after the error response — pins the "no write on error" contract), `path_filestat_get_on_root_returns_filetype_directory_and_root_mount_id` ("/" → filetype=3, dev=root-mount-id, ino=root-ino), `path_filestat_get_ignores_dir_fd_and_lookup_flags` (passes 0xFFFF_FFFF dir_fd + 0xDEAD_BEEF flags + valid path → still succeeds with filetype=2; pins the "ignored in v1" contract so drift surfaces as a hard test failure). Request IDs 720..725. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI` gains `PATH_FILESTAT_GET: 0x0041` between `FD_WRITE` and `PATH_OPEN` (alphanumeric-ish placement within the file/path block). `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: WASI shim gains `path_filestat_get(dirfd, flags, path_ptr, path_len, buf_ptr) -> errno`. Reads the path bytes from user memory at path_ptr (new pattern for this shim — previous shims only wrote TO user memory; this one reads from user memory to stage the path AND writes to user memory at buf_ptr), copies them into an owned Uint8Array, dispatches `PATH_FILESTAT_GET` with `heapPtr=0, heapLen=pathLen` + `pathCopy` as heapIn + `arg0=0` for the ignored dir_fd; on success copies the 64 bytes of returned filestat_t from heapOut to user memory at bufPtr via `writeBuf.set(heapOut.subarray(0, 64), bufPtr)`. Same shape as the path_open shim for the input side + the fd_filestat_get shim for the output side. 37-line doc block documents the WASI signature + the input/output-share-the-heap-ptr convention + the dir_fd/flags ignored status. `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`: `decodeFilestat` helper lifted from inside the FD_FILESTAT_GET describe block to module scope so both describe blocks share it (cleaner than duplication). New `describe("dispatch: PATH_FILESTAT_GET", ...)` block with 3 tests: `returns filetype=CHARACTER_DEVICE for /dev/console` (filetype=2, nlink=1, size=0), `returns -ENOENT for a path that does not resolve` ("/nonexistent/path" → -ERRNO.ENOENT, extraLen=0), `returns filetype=DIRECTORY for the root path /` (filetype=3, size=0). - `23f378a` — FD_FILESTAT_GET WASI shim + kernel-side filestat_t synthesis (first slice stretching the WASI surface to cover the file-metadata pair; unblocks `std::fs::File::metadata` for every userland std binary). `crates/abi/src/wasi.rs`: new `pub mod filestat` with `SIZE: usize = 64` + eight `OFF_*` offsets pinning the 64-byte WASI preview 1 `filestat_t` wire layout (dev @ 0, ino @ 8, filetype @ 16, nlink @ 24, size @ 32, atim @ 40, mtim @ 48, ctim @ 56 — all little-endian u64 except filetype's single byte at offset 16 followed by 7 bytes of C-ABI struct-alignment padding before nlink). New `pub mod filetype` with the eight WASI filetype constants (`UNKNOWN=0`, `BLOCK_DEVICE=1`, `CHARACTER_DEVICE=2`, `DIRECTORY=3`, `REGULAR_FILE=4`, `SOCKET_DGRAM=5`, `SOCKET_STREAM=6`, `SYMBOLIC_LINK=7`) mirroring `__wasi_filetype_t` from the C header. `crates/kernel/src/vfs/mod.rs`: new `Vfs::stat_ino(mount_id, ino) -> Result<FileStat, FsError>` public method mirroring the existing `read_ino` / `write_ino` pattern — the syscall layer already carries `(mount_id, ino)` on a Vnode fd, so `fd_filestat_get` skips path resolution by calling this instead of `Vfs::stat(abs_path)`. 13-line addition; exposes what was already reachable through `Vfs::stat` + `resolve`. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: dispatch match gains `op::FD_FILESTAT_GET => handle_fd_filestat_get(kernel, pid, req, heap)` between `FD_FDSTAT_GET` and `FD_PRESTAT_GET`. New `filetype_from_nodetype(ty: NodeType) -> u8` helper mapping the vfs's concrete NodeType (RegularFile/Directory/CharDevice/Socket/SymLink/Fifo) to the WASI filetype byte — Fifo → UNKNOWN because WASI has no FIFO filetype. Pre-existing file-scoped `FILETYPE_*` constants replaced with uses of the new `abi::wasi::filetype` module (`ft::CHARACTER_DEVICE` etc.) — single source of truth. New `handle_fd_filestat_get` handler: looks up `entry` in the caller's fd table (EBADF on miss), then matches on `entry.object` — Vnode branch calls `kernel.vfs.stat_ino(mount_id, ino)` + derives filetype from the NodeType + propagates `st.ino / st.size / st.nlink as u64 / st.atime_ns / st.mtime_ns / st.ctime_ns` + uses `mount_id.0 as u64` for dev; CharDevice branch returns `(CHARACTER_DEVICE, dev=0, ino=devnum as u64, size=0, nlink=1, times=0)`; Socket/DisplayConn → `(SOCKET_STREAM, 0, id as u64, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0)`; Pipe variants → `(UNKNOWN, 0, id as u64, ...)`; SignalChannel → `(UNKNOWN, 0, 0, ...)`. Then writes the 64 bytes into `heap_out_mut(req, heap, fs_off::SIZE)` via `buf[..SIZE].copy_from_slice(&[0u8; SIZE])` (zero the alignment-pad bytes 17..24 + any oversized window) + eight `copy_from_slice(&<field>.to_le_bytes())` calls at the pinned offsets; returns `extra_len: fs_off::SIZE as u32`. Inline 25-line doc block documents the wire layout + the "Vnode-queries-vfs, others-synthesise" split + notes real mtime/atime/ctime is a future vfs-side-channel slice. `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: 7 new tests in a new "fd_filestat_get" block between the existing fd_fdstat_get + fd_prestat_get blocks, with a shared `filestat_u64(heap, base, off)` helper that decodes the 8-byte LE u64 at the given field offset. `fd_filestat_get_on_char_device_fd_returns_filetype_char_device` (install_fd(CharDevice(DEV_CONSOLE)) → filetype=2, dev=0, ino=DEV_CONSOLE, size=0, nlink=1, padding bytes 17..24 all zero), `fd_filestat_get_on_socket_fd_returns_filetype_socket_stream` (k.ipc_socket(pid, SocketType::Stream) → filetype=6, size=0), `fd_filestat_get_on_regular_file_vnode_returns_filetype_and_size` (vfs.create + vfs.write(22-byte payload) + resolve + install_fd(Vnode) → filetype=4, dev=mount_id.0, ino=vnode's ino, size=22), `fd_filestat_get_on_directory_vnode_returns_filetype_directory` (vfs.mkdir + resolve + install_fd(Vnode) → filetype=3, size=0), `fd_filestat_get_on_pipe_read_fd_returns_filetype_unknown` (install_fd(PipeRead(7)) → filetype=0, ino=7), `fd_filestat_get_on_signal_channel_fd_returns_filetype_unknown` (install_fd(SignalChannel) → filetype=0, dev=0, ino=0), `fd_filestat_get_on_invalid_fd_returns_ebadf` (bad fd 99 → -EBADF, heap untouched). Request IDs 700..706. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI` gains `FD_FILESTAT_GET: 0x0027` between `FD_FDSTAT_GET` + `FD_PRESTAT_GET`. New `FILETYPE` namespace (all eight WASI bytes) mirroring `abi::wasi::filetype`. `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: WASI shim gains `fd_filestat_get(fd: i32, buf_ptr: i32) -> errno`. Dispatches `FD_FILESTAT_GET` with `heapPtr=0, heapLen=64`, copies the 64 bytes of the returned filestat_t from `heapOut` to user memory at `buf_ptr` via `writeBuf.set(heapOut.subarray(0, 64), bufPtr)` — same shape as the pre-existing fd_fdstat_get shim, just 64-over-24 on the buffer size. On kernel error returns positive errno (WASI convention — negated back from the already-negated Response.status). `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`: new `describe("dispatch: FD_FILESTAT_GET", ...)` block with 4 tests + a shared `decodeFilestat(heapOut): { dev, ino, filetype, nlink, size, atim, mtim, ctim }` helper that DataView-reads the 8 fields at their WASI offsets. Request IDs 710..714. `returns filetype=CHARACTER_DEVICE for a /dev/console fd` (installConsoleFd + markRunning → filetype=2, nlink=1, size=0, dev=0, + byte-by-byte check that heapOut[17..24] is all zero — the struct-alignment padding), `returns filetype=SOCKET_STREAM for an IPC socket fd` (IPC_SOCKET dispatch to get a fresh fd → filetype=6), `returns -EBADF for an unopened fd` (fd 99 → -ERRNO.EBADF, extraLen=0), `reports the heap-out layout with times and size defaulting to 0 for non-Vnode fds` (extra pinning test over the full layout: atim/mtim/ctim all 0n; filetype=CHARACTER_DEVICE). New `handle_clock_res_get` handler mirrors `handle_clock_time_get`'s four-branch shape but returns a constant per clock id rather than reading Platform: `CLOCKID_MONOTONIC | CLOCKID_REALTIME → 1` (ns; PMos's Platform clock is nanosecond-granular on every supported host), `CLOCKID_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID | CLOCKID_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID → -ENOTSUP` (same split the time handler uses — "known clock id, not implemented here"), unknown clock id → -EINVAL. Inline doc block notes the handler doesn't touch Platform because the answer is a compile-time constant per clock id; centralising that constant in the handler (rather than pre-computing 1 ns userland-wide) is deliberate so a future host with coarser resolution — e.g. a wasm32 host that only exposes microseconds — has a single edit point. `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: 5 new tests after the CLOCK_TIME_GET block, mirroring its style + fresh request_id in the 90..=94 range. `clock_res_get_monotonic_returns_nanosecond_resolution` + `clock_res_get_realtime_returns_nanosecond_resolution` (both assert value == 1 ns, status 0), `clock_res_get_process_cputime_returns_enotsup` + `clock_res_get_thread_cputime_returns_enotsup` (both assert status -ENOTSUP, value 0), `clock_res_get_unknown_clock_returns_einval` (clock_id 99 → -EINVAL, value 0). `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `OP_WASI` table grows `CLOCK_RES_GET: 0x0010` mirroring `abi::wasi::CLOCK_RES_GET`. `CLOCKID` doc block updated to mention CLOCK_RES_GET alongside CLOCK_TIME_GET (both share the clock_id namespace + same MONOTONIC/REALTIME supported + same cputime ENOTSUP split). `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: WASI shim gains `clock_res_get(clock_id: i32, resolution_ptr: i32) -> errno: i32` handler. Packs `clock_id` into `arg0`, dispatches `CLOCK_RES_GET`, writes the returned i64 resolution value at `resolution_ptr` via `DataView.setBigInt64(..., true)` (little-endian). On kernel error returns positive errno (WASI convention — negated back from the already-negated `Response.status`). BigInt path kept for bit-exactness even though the resolution value is small today (1 ns) — matches the time shim style + guards against a future resolution value that exceeds 2^53. `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`: new `describe("dispatch: CLOCK_RES_GET", ...)` block with 5 tests — MONOTONIC → value 1n, REALTIME → value 1n, PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID + THREAD_CPUTIME_ID → -ERRNO.ENOTSUP, unknown clock_id 99 → -ERRNO.EINVAL (request_ids 60..=64). Tests use `freshHost()` with default clock callbacks since the handler doesn't read Platform. `crates/hello-clock/src/main.rs`: extended with a third probe that directly calls `clock_res_get(MONOTONIC)` + `clock_res_get(REALTIME)` via a hand-written `wasi_snapshot_preview1` extern block (std doesn't expose `clock_getres` at the std level — the FFI is declared inline in the probe block, `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`-gated so the native build stays clean). Both calls assert the returned resolution is exactly 1 ns. Trailing `println!("hello-clock res ok")` prints on success. Module doc updated to describe the three-probe shape + the FFI rationale. `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: hello-clock composition test extended in place — title updated to mention CLOCK_RES_GET, comment block rewritten to describe the three-probe flow + new failure modes (\"missing res ok\" = CLOCK_RES_GET broken), expected lines array grows `"hello-clock res ok"` as the third entry. `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (+clock_res_get WASI shim). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` byte-identical (the shared `CLOCK_RES_GET` constant is dead-code-eliminated from the kernel bundle since kernel-worker runs kernel.wasm + doesn't dispatch the shim — only user-worker does). `dist/assets/bin/hello-clock.wasm` + `dist/assets/bin/kernel.wasm` regenerate (not tracked). `dist/manifest.json` entry count unchanged at 29 (no new shipped assets). Total 1158 → 1168 passing, 0 failing. Textbook mirror of the dcab1e5 CLOCK_TIME_GET slice — same three-layer shape + same Rust-kernel-then-TS-shim TDD cadence + same shared-constants-mirror pattern. Strictly narrower than dcab1e5: no Platform trait changes (a compile-time constant per clock_id suffices for v1), no new errno additions (ENOTSUP + EINVAL already in the ERRNO table). - `2955d62` — socket cleanup on `proc_exit` + PROC_EXIT WASI shim round-trip (kernel-correctness slice closing the documented follow-up tech-debt from `cf20126`; the kernel now releases fd-table-owned resources — IPC socket bindings, pipe refcounts, and any release-hookable per-fd object — the instant a process exits, not lazily at the still-deferred parent-side `proc_wait` T075 reap). `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: new private helper `fn release_fd_table_resources(&mut self, pid: Pid)` drains the per-pid `FdTable` via the existing `drain_all()` surface and routes each entry through the pre-existing `release_object` dispatcher (which already knew how to drop pipe readers/writers and close sockets). `Kernel::proc_exit` grows a `self.release_fd_table_resources(pid)` call between `sched.remove(pid)` and `procs.exit(pid, status)` — the fd table itself stays in `self.fds` (now empty) so the existing `reap` contract is preserved. `Kernel::reap` re-expressed using the helper (contains_key probe for NoSuchPid, then helper drain, then map removal, then caps/inbox/procs sweep); byte-equivalent on every pre-existing test but clearer intent + load-bearing idempotence note ("the second call is a no-op on the already-empty table"). Inline docs rewritten on both methods to document the new contract: "exit is the single source of truth for 'this process no longer owns any kernel resources'; the subsequent reap walks an already-empty fd table and is effectively a pid-table + cap-table + signal-inbox sweep." The motivation for doing this at exit (not reap) is spelled out: `proc_wait` T075 is still deferred → nothing reaps in the current M1 substrate → orphan bindings would live forever if cleanup waited for reap. `crates/kernel/tests/sys.rs`: 6 new tests at the bottom of the display_bind/display_connect block under a new "proc_exit cleanup" section. `proc_exit_releases_display_server_socket_binding_immediately` binds `/run/display` from a DisplayServer pid, proc_exits, and asserts `k.ipc.lookup_binding(DISPLAY_SOCKET_PATH).is_none()` before any reap fires; companion assertion: an unrelated DisplayClient's `display_connect` returns `KernelError::ConnectionRefused`. `proc_exit_allows_another_process_to_rebind_the_freed_path` — after the first DisplayServer proc_exits, a second one can `display_bind` the same path (proves the path is genuinely reusable, not just flagged closed — before the slice, this raised `AddressInUse`). `proc_exit_releases_generic_ipc_bindings_too` — covers any `ipc_bind("/run/foo")`, not just the capability-gated `/run/display` alias (the cleanup is at `release_object` → `close_socket`, which walks every socket fd regardless of path). `proc_exit_drops_pipe_writer_refcount_before_reap` — init creates a pipe, holds both ends, spawns a child inheriting the writer (refcount 2); child proc_exits, asserts writer_count drops 2 → 1 immediately, NOT at the subsequent reap; idempotence check: post-reap refcount is still 1 (not 0), proving the helper is safe to run twice. `proc_exit_clears_the_exited_processs_fd_table_entries` — the zombie's fd table is open_count=0 after exit (gives procfs + a future `ps` a way to distinguish "mid-teardown" from "still holds resources"). `proc_exit_of_pending_client_leaves_listener_intact` — symmetry test: when a Connecting client exits, the server's listener + binding are NOT torn down (proves the cleanup is scoped to the exiter's own fd table, doesn't over-reach into resources owned by other pids). `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: WASI `proc_exit` shim now dispatches OP_WASI.PROC_EXIT to the kernel backend BEFORE throwing the `UserProcessExited` sentinel. Previously the shim only threw — the kernel never saw an exit, so `Kernel::proc_exit` (and therefore the new fd-table cleanup) never ran for any userland-initiated exit. The dispatch is `{ opcode: OP_WASI.PROC_EXIT, requestId: 0, arg0: rval >>> 0 }`; response status/value are intentionally ignored (exiting process has no way to observe them + kernel-side is contractually infallible from userland). Inline comment block rewritten to describe the two-step shim + the rationale for ignoring the response. `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `ERRNO` table grows `ECONNREFUSED: 14` (mirror of `abi::errno::ECONNREFUSED`), so TS tests + future shim work can reference the symbolic name. `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`: 2 new tests in the existing `serviceSab: PROC_EXIT` describe block. `releases the exiting process's /run/display binding so a later DISPLAY_CONNECT returns -ECONNREFUSED` drives kernel.wasm directly — a CAPSET_ALL pid dispatches DISPLAY_BIND then PROC_EXIT, then a sibling pid's DISPLAY_CONNECT asserts `response.status === -ERRNO.ECONNREFUSED`. Kernel-wasm equivalent of the native `proc_exit_releases_display_server_socket_binding_immediately` test; pins the full Request → opcode dispatch → fd-table drain → `release_object` → `close_socket` → `bindings.remove` chain through the SAB-ring wire format. `frees the display-server path so a second pid can rebind it` is the DISPLAY_BIND rebind analogue (kernel-wasm mirror of `proc_exit_allows_another_process_to_rebind_the_freed_path`). `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: the four-pid composition test's display-client-demo assertion flips `exitCode 11` → `10` (from `fd_write` EINVAL retry exhaustion against the orphan listener → `display_connect` ECONNREFUSED poll exhaustion against the now-empty path). Comment block rewritten to describe the new flow: display_connect returns -ECONNREFUSED immediately (binding is gone), poll loop treats ECONNREFUSED as "retry", bounded iteration count exhausts, binary exits 10 through display-client-demo's documented exit-code table. The `fd_write` retry loop (still load-bearing for the concurrent Playwright flow's Connecting→Connected race, where `display_connect` succeeds against a live server but `fd_write` can reach the dispatcher before `ipc_accept` promotes the client) is noted as "unreachable under sequential `runAllSpawns`" but kept in place for the real-Worker interleaving in Playwright. `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` + `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (both pick up the shared ERRNO.ECONNREFUSED addition; user-worker additionally picks up the `proc_exit` shim's new dispatch line). `dist/manifest.json` entry count unchanged (29 — no new wasm binaries; kernel.wasm regenerates but is not tracked). Files: `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs` (+64 net; helper + proc_exit + reap rewrites), `crates/kernel/tests/sys.rs` (+209; 6 new tests), `web/src/shared/syscall.ts` (+1 ERRNO entry), `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (+25; proc_exit shim dispatches PROC_EXIT), `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` (+67; 2 new tests), `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` (exitCode 11 → 10 + comment rewrite), `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` (+1 line), `dist/assets/user-worker.js` (+25 net). Test deltas: Rust 844 → 850 (+6 native-platform tests covering the fd-table drain + binding release + rebind + pipe refcount + fd-table-zero + peer-symmetry paths). TS-unit 304 → 306 (+2 kernel-wasm-host tests exercising the DISPLAY_BIND → PROC_EXIT → DISPLAY_CONNECT=ECONNREFUSED round-trip + DISPLAY_BIND rebind; the four-pid composition test was edited in place, not duplicated — its exit-code assertion flipped 11 → 10). TS-integration 2 → 2 unchanged (Playwright's four-pid concurrent-Worker test still passes: display-server is alive when display-client-demo connects under real-Worker interleaving, so the round-trip completes as before; the input round-trip test is likewise unaffected — it drove through the same PROC_EXIT path both before + after, just silently before and via-the-kernel now). Total 1150 → 1158 passing, 0 failing across all layers. First slice where resource cleanup lands eagerly at proc_exit: removes the cf20126-documented tech-debt + closes the "orphan listener" failure mode for good. Semantically this is a step toward proc_wait T075 — once T075 lands, reap's fd-table drain becomes a pure no-op for the standard proc_exit-then-reap flow (the safety-net branch still runs for pathological paths like a pid registered for bookkeeping and immediately reaped without ever executing). Does NOT change `ipc_accept`'s blocking semantics (still on the runway under long-running-accept-loop, still blocked on T075). Does NOT touch display-client-demo's `fd_write` EINVAL retry loop (still load-bearing for the Playwright concurrent-Worker race — the user's handoff note suggested removing it but the race it covers is independent of the orphan-listener failure mode this slice fixes, so the loop stays).
- `dcab1e5` — CLOCK_TIME_GET WASI shim + kernel-side clock_id branching (first slice stretching the WASI surface past fd_write/fd_read/path_open/proc_exit/args_*/environ_*/fd_fdstat_get/fd_prestat_get; unblocks `std::time::Instant::now()` + `std::time::SystemTime::now()` in every userland std binary). `crates/kernel/src/platform/mod.rs`: `Platform` trait gains `fn now_realtime_ns(&self) -> u64` alongside existing `now_ns`; docs note realtime is NOT required to be monotonic (NTP adjustment + manual clock change + leap-second smearing may step it backwards) and callers needing monotonicity use `now_ns`. Module doc header extended to describe both clocks with cross-references to `clock_time_get(MONOTONIC)` + `clock_time_get(REALTIME)`. `crates/kernel/src/platform/native.rs`: `NativePlatform::now_realtime_ns` uses `SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)` with saturating-to-0 on the pre-epoch case; no strict-monotonicity fudge — WASI contract permits step. `crates/kernel/src/platform/wasm.rs`: extern block grows `fn pmos_host_now_realtime_ns() -> u64`; `WasmPlatform::now_realtime_ns` calls it verbatim (same cfg-gated unsafe block pattern as `now_ns`). `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: `handle_clock_time_get` rewritten to branch on `clock_id` — `CLOCKID_MONOTONIC (1) → Platform::now_ns()`, `CLOCKID_REALTIME (0) → Platform::now_realtime_ns()`, `CLOCKID_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID (2) / CLOCKID_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID (3) → -ENOTSUP`, unknown id → -EINVAL. Old behaviour (ignored clock_id; always returned `now_ns`) replaced. Inline doc block rewritten to describe the four-branch handler + rationale for ENOTSUP vs EINVAL split (POSIX precedent). Errno import grows `ENOTSUP`. `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`: clock_time_get test coverage 1 → 5. Existing `clock_time_get_returns_strictly_increasing_nanoseconds` renamed to `clock_time_get_monotonic_returns_strictly_increasing_nanoseconds`. Four new tests: `clock_time_get_realtime_returns_wall_clock_nanoseconds` (asserts value > 2020-01-01 UTC ns, a stable lower bound proving wall-clock sourcing), `clock_time_get_process_cputime_returns_enotsup` (clock_id=2 → -ENOTSUP), `clock_time_get_thread_cputime_returns_enotsup` (clock_id=3 → -ENOTSUP), `clock_time_get_unknown_clock_returns_einval` (clock_id=99 → -EINVAL). `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`: `ERRNO` table grows `ENOTSUP: 58`; new `CLOCKID` namespace (`REALTIME: 0`, `MONOTONIC: 1`, `PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID: 2`, `THREAD_CPUTIME_ID: 3`) mirroring `abi::wasi::CLOCKID_*`. `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts`: `KernelWasmHostOptions` gains `nowRealtimeNs?: () => bigint` alongside the existing `nowNs` (default `BigInt(Date.now()) * 1_000_000n` — Date.now is ms → ns); docs note CLOCK_REALTIME contract permits stepping. `KernelWasmHost.create` wires `pmos_host_now_realtime_ns: (): bigint => nowRealtimeNs()` import alongside `pmos_host_now_ns`. `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`: WASI shim gains `clock_time_get(clock_id: i32, precision: i64, timestamp_ptr: i32) -> errno: i32`. Packs `clock_id` into `arg0`, dispatches `CLOCK_TIME_GET`, writes returned i64 ns at `timestamp_ptr` via `DataView.setBigInt64(..., true)` (little-endian, bit-exact). On kernel error returns positive errno (WASI convention — negated back from the already-negated `Response.status`). The `_precision` parameter ignored (kernel handler's Platform clock is already nanosecond-resolution). `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`: `FreshHostOptions` grows optional `nowNs` + `nowRealtimeNs` callbacks. New `describe("dispatch: CLOCK_TIME_GET", ...)` block with 5 tests: MONOTONIC returns `nowNs` host-import value (1_234_567_890_000n); REALTIME returns `nowRealtimeNs` host-import value (1_700_000_000_000_000_000n — chosen to exceed 2^53 so the BigInt path is load-bearing and a Number-based regression surfaces as precision loss); PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID + THREAD_CPUTIME_ID both return -ERRNO.ENOTSUP; unknown clock_id 99 → -ERRNO.EINVAL. Imports line grows `CLOCKID`. `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-entry.test.ts`: `imports.env` grows `pmos_host_now_realtime_ns: () => 0n` alongside the existing `pmos_host_now_ns` (kernel.wasm now imports both; test-level hosts bypassing `KernelWasmHost.create` must satisfy both). `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: top-level `helloClockWasmBytes: ArrayBuffer` + `beforeAll` loads `target/wasm32-wasip1/release/hello-clock.wasm` (bin target, dashes preserved) alongside existing 13 wasm paths. Test doubles as the shim's "missing import" guard — if the clock_time_get shim is absent, instantiation fails with LinkError before `runtime.run()` returns. NEW `crates/hello-clock/` bin crate (Cargo.toml + src/main.rs, 14 + 37 lines): `fn main` calls `Instant::now()` twice + asserts `b >= a` + prints "hello-clock monotonic ok"; then `SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)` + asserts `.as_secs() > 1_577_836_800` (2020-01-01 UTC lower bound) + prints "hello-clock realtime ok". Module doc documents how `Instant::now()` lowers to `clock_time_get(MONOTONIC, ...)` + `SystemTime::now()` lowers to `clock_time_get(REALTIME, ...)`. `Cargo.toml` workspace root adds `"crates/hello-clock"` member. `Cargo.lock` regenerated. `Justfile` appends `-p hello-clock` to the wasm32-wasip1 cargo build line. `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` appends `("hello-clock", "hello-clock")` to `USERLAND_BINS` (bin target, dashes preserved). `dist/manifest.json` entry count 28 → 29 (+`assets/bin/hello-clock.wasm`). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` rebuilt (+pmos_host_now_realtime_ns import + nowRealtimeNs default). `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt (+clock_time_get WASI shim). `dist/assets/bin/hello-clock.wasm` new, ~56 KiB LTOed std bin — not tracked per convention. `dist/assets/bin/kernel.wasm` regenerates (not tracked). Textbook opcode-handler expansion: Platform-trait method + kernel-side branching handler + kernel-wasm-host import closure + TS-side WASI shim + shared CLOCKID constants all land together, isolation-tested at every layer. Pattern carries forward for the next WASI-surface slice (fd_filestat_get / path_filestat_get / clock_res_get / poll_oneoff) — same three-layer shape + same Rust-kernel-then-TS-shim TDD cadence.
- `cf20126` — persistent accept loop in display-server + new display-client-demo crate (first slice where two separate WASM binaries in separate user Workers exchange bytes through a PMos IPC socket). `crates/display-server/src/main.rs` drops self-connect (drops `display_connect` import + `PIXELS` constant + client-side `fd_write`): extern block carries `display_bind` + `ipc_accept` only. `fn main` replaces the single-shot `ipc_accept` with a bounded EAGAIN poll loop (10_000 iterations; `rc >= 0 → break + server fd`, `rc == -EAGAIN (-6) → continue`, other neg → exit 12, exhaustion → exit 17) — same pattern `hello-input-echo`'s `fd_read` EAGAIN poll uses. `fd_read(server)` also grows a bounded EAGAIN poll (10_000 iter; `rc == 0 && nread > 0 → break`, `rc == 0 || rc == EAGAIN (+6) → continue`, other → exit 14, exhaustion → exit 18) covering the Playwright race where the server's fd_read reaches dispatch before the client's fd_write has landed. Remaining flow (path_open /dev/fb0 → exit 15; fd_write(fb_fd, recv_buf) → exit 16; trailing `println!("display-server fb blit ok")`) unchanged; `recv_buf` stays load-bearing so a regression breaking `fd_read` surfaces as wrong framebuffer bytes rather than a green test. Module doc rewritten to describe long-running-server shape + bounded-poll safety valve + companion `/bin/display-client-demo` binary. NEW `crates/display-client-demo/` bin crate (Cargo.toml + src/main.rs, 14 + 147 lines): `fn main` prints `"display-client-demo starting"`, enters bounded `display_connect` poll loop (10_000 iter; `rc == -ECONNREFUSED (-14) → continue`, other neg → exit 12, exhaustion → exit 10) covering the race where the client races ahead of display-server's bind; issues `fd_write(client, PIXELS)` with a bounded EINVAL retry loop (10_000 iter; `rc == EINVAL (+28) → continue`, other → exit 11, exhaustion → exit 11) covering the narrow race where `fd_write` reaches dispatch before `ipc_accept` has promoted client from `Connecting` to `Connected`; trailing `println!("display-client-demo sent pixels")` prints on success; native stub under `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]`. `Cargo.toml` workspace root adds `"crates/display-client-demo"` member. `Justfile` build recipe adds `-p display-client-demo` to the cargo wasm32-wasip1 line. `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("display-client-demo", "display-client-demo")` to `USERLAND_BINS` (bin target — dashes preserved in the output filename). `crates/init/src/main.rs` `fn main` grows a THIRD `proc_spawn` call for `/bin/display-client-demo` after the existing display-server spawn (same extern block — still one `proc_spawn` import — same unsafe-block + errno/pid println shape); module doc rewritten to describe three fire-and-forget demo children + spawn-order rationale (display-server's bind lands before display-client-demo's connect under normal Worker scheduling because pid-map iteration mirrors spawn order). `web/src/bootstrap.ts` deletes the `#display-server` URL hash branch + 4-line comment block; default boot's four-pid tree now covers display-server end-to-end via real IPC (strictly stronger signal than the old single-binary self-connect demo). `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: top-level `displayClientDemoWasmBytes: ArrayBuffer` + `beforeAll` loads `target/wasm32-wasip1/release/display-client-demo.wasm` (bin target, dashes preserved). Composition test renamed "init (std) spawns hello-std AND display-server AND display-client-demo via pmos_ext.proc_spawn, all three children run after init exits"; `binaryRegistry` grows `/bin/display-client-demo`, `history.toHaveLength` 3 → 4. Critical shape decision documented in-test: under sequential `runAllSpawns` children run one-at-a-time FIFO (hello-std, display-server, display-client-demo), so display-server's accept poll exhausts (no concurrent client) and exits 17; display-client-demo's `display_connect` then succeeds against the ORPHANED `/run/display` binding left behind by display-server's proc_exit (socket cleanup is a future kernel slice), so the client transitions to `Connecting` + queues on the orphaned backlog + `fd_write` returns EINVAL forever because nobody is there to `ipc_accept` + promote to `Connected` — EINVAL retry loop exhausts, exits 11. Neither "fb blit ok" nor "sent pixels" prints; `fbWrites.toHaveLength(0)` pins the fact that the sequential harness CANNOT drive the IPC round-trip. The three-binary round-trip is validated ONLY under Playwright where concurrent Workers in separate linear memories give the required interleaving. 7-line console assertion swapped for 8-line (4 init lines + 1 hello-std + 1 display-server "starting" only + 1 display-client-demo "starting" only). `web/tests/integration/real-kernel.spec.ts` first describe block strengthened end-to-end: top-of-file comment rewritten to describe the four-pid shape + protocol-ordered assertion (server's fb_blit_ok MUST come after client's sent_pixels — server's fd_read unblocks only after client's fd_write lands), title "runs init -> hello-std + display-server" → "runs init -> hello-std + display-server <-> display-client-demo", new findIndex vars `initSpawnDisplayClientIdx` + `displayClientStartIdx` + `displayClientSentIdx`, ordering strengthened (`initSpawnHelloStdIdx < initSpawnDisplayServerIdx < initSpawnDisplayClientIdx < initExitIdx`; `helloStdIdx` + `displayServerStartIdx` + `displayClientStartIdx` all `> initExitIdx`; within-pid monotonicity `displayServerBlitIdx > displayServerStartIdx` + `displayClientSentIdx > displayClientStartIdx`; load-bearing cross-pid `displayServerBlitIdx > displayClientSentIdx`), DOM `#pmos-real-console.innerText` assertions extended to cover 3 new display-client-demo-related lines + new `init spawned display-client-demo pid=\d+` line + the `indexOf(displayClientSentPixels) < indexOf(displayServerFbBlitOk)` protocol-ordering equivalent, `peakLiveWorkers >= 3 → >= 4` (four concurrent user Workers during the window where init has spawned all three children and none have exited yet; kernel Worker still not counted). Second test block `"display-server: std binary binds, accepts a client, relays pixels to /dev/fb0"` DELETED — `#display-server` hash gate is gone, default-boot test's four-pid round-trip is a strict superset. `dist/assets/bootstrap.js` rebuilt (28.5 KiB unchanged). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` + `dist/assets/user-worker.js` byte-identical (no TS source change). `dist/manifest.json` entry count 27 → 28 (+`assets/bin/display-client-demo.wasm`). `dist/assets/bin/display-client-demo.wasm` (new ~42 KiB, not tracked). `dist/assets/bin/display-server.wasm` + `init.wasm` regenerate (not tracked).
