## Conventions carried forward

- Commit author identity: `webos dev <dev@webos.local>`. Set per-command via `git -c user.name=... -c user.email=...`, never `--global`.
- Test count delta reported in every commit message.
- Deviation notes in `tasks.md` point at real file paths with a brief reason for the drift, so a future reader grepping for the originally-planned file path can find the code.
- Toolkit is a `std` crate (no `#![no_std]`), so `Box`, `String`, `Rc`, `Cell` are available.
- Canvas writes **RGBA** byte order. The term rasterizer writes **BGRA** — the known bug the migration slice fixes.

## Toolchain bootstrap (fresh sandbox)

Captured on 2026-04-15. The dev sandbox comes up with Rust but **without** `just` or the WASM targets on the initial PATH / rustup profile, so a brand-new Claude Code session has to do this once before `just build` / `just test-*` works:

- **Rust toolchain**: already installed under `~/.cargo/bin` (cargo 1.94.1, rustc 1.94.1). Not on the default `PATH` — prefix every command with `export PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"` (or set it once at the top of a Bash invocation).
- **WASM targets**: install both via `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown wasm32-wasip1`. `wasm32-unknown-unknown` is for the kernel (cdylib); `wasm32-wasip1` is for every userland crate. (The repo used to reference `wasm32-wasi`; upstream rustup renamed that target to `wasm32-wasip1`. The T029 resolution slice updated every reference.)
- **`just`**: not preinstalled. Run `cargo install just --locked` (takes ~45 s to build on a cold cache; lands in `~/.cargo/bin`). Verified with `just --version` → `just 1.49.0`.
- **Node + npm**: already at `/bin/node` and `/bin/npm`, on the default `PATH`. No action needed for `npx vitest run` / `npx playwright test`.

After the bootstrap, the following layer targets are green:

- `just build` — full pipeline: kernel cdylib + 14 userland crates + esbuild + `xtask assemble-dist` → `dist/`.
- `just test-kernel`, `just test-display-server`, `just test-toolkit`, `just test-drivers` — per-layer isolation tests.
- `cargo test --workspace` — the authoritative full Rust count (787 as of the T029 slice). This covers crates the `just test-*` targets don't touch (integration-tests, term, ring, display-proto, etc.).
- `(cd web && npx vitest run)` — TS unit tests (212).

**The full `just test` meta-target still fails**, but the residual gaps are now downstream, not toolchain:

- `just test-integration` needs Phase 3+ Playwright scaffolding (no `web/tests/integration/` directory exists and no `playwright.config.*`; T127 onward). A vitest/playwright dep conflict also surfaces when Playwright tries to run with no test files — addressing that is part of the same Phase 3 slice.
- `just test-perf` needs T220 (`crates/integration-tests/src/bin/input-latency.rs` — the p95 input-latency harness — does not exist yet).

So "the full test suite" in practice today is:

```sh
export PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"
cargo test --workspace          # all Rust crates, host target
(cd web && npx vitest run)      # TS unit tests
```

= 787 + 212 = **999 passing, 0 failing** as of the T029 resolution slice. These are the same numbers the `preferred_size` slice landed — T029 changed build plumbing, not test code.

## Append-only slice log

The chronological prose block below is an append-only journal of every slice that landed in this branch. Newer slices appear at the top of "Recent slice history" above as one-liners; the long-form notes here exist so that a future session looking for the *why* behind a particular kernel handler or TS shim can grep for keywords ("DISPLAY_BIND", "fd_prestat_get", "PROC_SPAWN") and read the full rationale that accompanied the change.

After the **T073 dispatcher first landing**: 809 + 212 = **1021 passing, 0 failing** (+22 new isolation tests at `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`).

After the **extern-C seam slice** on top of T073: 811 + 229 = **1040 passing, 0 failing**. Rust gained +2 `abi::ring` byte-roundtrip tests; TS gained +17 `kernel-wasm-entry.test.ts` integration tests that load `kernel.wasm` from disk and drive the exports. `kernel.wasm` itself went from 86 bytes to 112 KiB — the exports pinned the whole dispatcher + `Kernel` graph alive through LLVM's dead-code elimination. A pre-existing bump-allocator bug where the cursor started at 0 (clobbering the static data section) was fixed in the same slice; initial allocations now correctly start at `__heap_base`.

After the **KernelWasmHost slice** (T091 TS-side first landing): 811 + 246 = **1057 passing, 0 failing**. TS gained +17 `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` tests (Rust unchanged; only one new no-op Rust export for `kernel_inject_console_input`). `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts` + `web/src/shared/syscall.ts` are the new production modules; the class is not yet wired into `kernel-worker-entry.ts`, which still defaults to `MockKernel` for backwards compatibility. The syscall shared module is hand-maintained against `abi::wasi` / `abi::ext` / `abi::errno` / `abi::cap` / `platform::DevId` and should be promoted to xtask autogeneration (like `sab-layout.ts`) when the opcode set grows.

After the **Worker-spawn Platform-hook slice** (T074 progression + the PROC_SPAWN opcode): 816 + 250 = **1066 passing, 0 failing**. Rust +5 (new `PROC_SPAWN` tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` — happy path, platform-refusal rollback, missing-stdio EINVAL, cap-superset ENOTCAPABLE, invalid-UTF-8 EINVAL). TS +4 (new tests in `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` exercising the `onSpawnProcess` callback round trip). `kernel.wasm` grew from 112 KiB → 127 KiB because the PROC_SPAWN handler + `Kernel::proc_spawn`'s platform call + rollback path are now reachable from an export. New host import `pmos_host_spawn_process` on the Rust side; new `KernelWasmHostOptions.onSpawnProcess` callback + `encodeSpawnManifest` helper on the TS side. The single biggest unknown on the MVP path (the Worker-spawn seam) is now proven end-to-end in the same sense as T073 and T091: the callback fires, the pid is allocated, the platform hook rolls back cleanly on refusal. What remains is the ACTUAL `new Worker(...)` + SAB allocation + user-wasm loading + WASI-shim path on the TS side — a follow-up slice where `onSpawnProcess` stops being a spy and starts actually spawning a Worker. The kernel-side of PROC_SPAWN is done (for the minimal path + caps manifest shape).

After the **user-wasm runtime slice** (first real user wasm end-to-end): 816 + 252 = **1068 passing, 0 failing**. TS +2 (new tests in `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` — hello-wasi-min runs through a real `KernelWasmHost` and produces "hello from userland\n" on `/dev/console`; plus a synthetic exit-code-only wasm built at test time via hand-emitted binary to prove exit-code propagation). Rust test count unchanged; the new `crates/hello-wasi-min/` cdylib crate is `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`-gated everywhere so `cargo test --workspace` sees a native-target no-op and `cargo build --target wasm32-wasip1 -p hello-wasi-min` produces a 265-byte binary with exactly two imports (`wasi_snapshot_preview1.fd_write` + `.proc_exit`) and two exports (`memory` + `_start`). New production modules: `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (`UserWasmRuntime` class + `KernelBackend` interface + `KernelWasmHostBackend` adapter + `UserProcessExited` unwind sentinel + minimal WASI shim for `fd_write` + `proc_exit`). `just build` now copies the hello binary to `dist/assets/bin/hello_wasi_min.wasm` (manifest entry count: 16 → 17). This is the first slice where a REAL `wasm32-wasip1` user binary runs against a REAL kernel via REAL WASI imports with no mocks on any layer — proves end-to-end that a WASI syscall from userland lands on a kernel device write and the observable effect bubbles back through `KernelWasmHost.onConsoleWrite`.

After the **spawn-callback wiring slice**: 816 + 254 = **1070 passing, 0 failing**. TS +2 (the composition test: `PROC_SPAWN` dispatched on behalf of a virtual parent → default `onSpawnProcess` queues a pending spawn from `binaryRegistry` → `drainPendingSpawns` runs the child to completion; plus the rollback test: missing-binary path returns `-EIO` and does not queue). `KernelWasmHost` now accepts a `binaryRegistry: ReadonlyMap<string, BufferSource>` option; when set and no explicit `onSpawnProcess` is provided, the host installs a default callback that looks the path up and queues `{ pid, path, bytes }` into an internal `pendingSpawns` queue. New public method `kernel.drainPendingSpawns()` pops the queue and runs each entry via a `UserWasmRuntime` + `KernelWasmHostBackend` bound to the spawn's pid — sequential, wait-for-child semantics, reentrant (a child that queues more spawns gets picked up on subsequent loop iterations). The module graph stays acyclic because `user-wasm-runtime.ts` uses `import type` for `KernelWasmHost`. This slice closes the `PROC_SPAWN`-to-running-binary loop: for the first time, a kernel-created pid actually maps to a running wasm process.

After the **two-level composition slice** (reentrancy under real wasm): 816 + 255 = **1071 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (two-level composition test). New crate `crates/hello-wasi-spawner/` (322-byte cdylib) imports `wasi_snapshot_preview1.{fd_write,proc_exit}` AND `pmos_ext.proc_spawn`. The `UserWasmRuntime` WASI shim gains a `pmos_ext` import namespace alongside `wasi_snapshot_preview1`; the `pmos_ext.proc_spawn(path_ptr, path_len, caps: u64) -> i32` shim reads the path from user memory, builds a `PROC_SPAWN` manifest via `encodeSpawnManifest`, dispatches through the backend, and returns the new pid (positive) or negative errno. The reentrancy test: init spawns `spawner`, the drain loop runs `spawner` which writes `"spawner alive\n"` and then calls `pmos_ext.proc_spawn("/usr/bin/hello", ...)` mid-run, queuing `hello` into the same pendingSpawns list the drain loop is currently iterating over; spawner exits, the drain loop picks up `hello`, runs it, `hello` writes `"hello from userland\n"` and exits; drain returns. Asserts the capture is `["spawner alive\n", "hello from userland\n"]` in that exact order — the sequential ordering is load-bearing, not incidental, because it proves the spawner fully ran before the child took over. First proof that PMos extension syscalls (not just WASI) can flow from user wasm through the runtime shim into the kernel dispatcher, and the first proof that `drainPendingSpawns` is reentrancy-safe under real wasm execution (not just "in theory"). `just build` manifest entry count: 17 → 18.

After the **kernel opcode breadth slice #1**: 820 + 255 = **1075 passing, 0 failing**. Rust +4 tests; TS unchanged (two existing tests that probed CLOCK_TIME_GET for ENOSYS were swapped to probe FD_SEEK instead, which is still unhandled, plus a stub for `pmos_host_now_ns` was added to `kernel-wasm-entry.test.ts` since CLOCK_TIME_GET's handler pulled the import back into the live set). New kernel opcodes: `CLOCK_TIME_GET` (wraps `Platform::now_ns`), `RANDOM_GET` (wraps `Platform::random_bytes`, fills the heap scratch in place), `SCHED_YIELD` (trivial OK return — required because Rust std's wasi libc calls it from a few spin-wait loops and would panic on `-ENOSYS`). Dispatcher opcode count 10 → 13. This is the first expansion slice where the kernel gains capabilities that every "real Rust std binary" needs at startup — the next slice pairs these with WASI shim entries on the TS side and a real Rust std test binary that exercises all three.

After the **IPC + DISPLAY_CONNECT opcodes slice** (kernel opcode breadth #2): 830 + 255 = **1085 passing, 0 failing**. Rust +10 tests; TS unchanged. Six new opcode handlers on the kernel side: `IPC_SOCKET`, `IPC_BIND`, `IPC_LISTEN`, `IPC_CONNECT`, `IPC_ACCEPT`, `DISPLAY_CONNECT`. Dispatcher opcode count 13 → 19. Four new thin `Kernel` method wrappers (`ipc_socket`, `ipc_bind`, `ipc_listen`, `ipc_connect`) following the existing `display_bind`/`display_connect`/`accept_socket` pattern — each validates fd/pid/cap preconditions then calls into the pre-existing `IpcTable` primitives. Plus a private `Kernel::socket_id_from_fd` helper that centralises the "fd → SocketId" lookup the new wrappers share. `DISPLAY_CONNECT` is a trivial wrapper over the semantic `Kernel::display_connect` method (which already existed). `kernel.wasm` grew 131 KiB → 143 KiB as the IPC handler graph became reachable. The slice's crown jewel test (`ipc_round_trip_server_accepts_client_and_they_exchange_bytes`) runs two processes in one kernel — server binds + listens + accepts, client connects, both ends exchange bytes via the existing `FD_READ` / `FD_WRITE` opcodes (which already route `FdObject::Socket` to the right path). First proof that generic IPC works end-to-end through the opcode layer. What remains: the TS-side shim entries for each of the 6 new opcodes, and — as the real payoff — a no_std server/client pair of user wasm binaries that exchange bytes through a real `KernelWasmHost`. That's the follow-up slice where the display-server runway actually opens up.

After the **IPC-through-real-wasm slice** (TS shim + `ipc-self-test` binary + composition test): 830 + 256 = **1086 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (new composition test). Rust unchanged (the new crate is `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`-gated like the earlier `hello-wasi-*` crates, so `cargo test --workspace` sees a native-target no-op). New crate `crates/ipc-self-test/` (772-byte cdylib) imports 8 functions across two namespaces — `wasi_snapshot_preview1.{fd_write, fd_read, proc_exit}` + `pmos_ext.{ipc_socket, ipc_bind, ipc_listen, ipc_connect, ipc_accept}` — and plays both server and client via self-connection in a single `_start` pass. Self-connection works because the kernel's IPC state machine indexes sockets by `SocketId`, not by pid. TS shim additions: `wasi_snapshot_preview1.fd_read` (scatter-gather mirror of the existing `fd_write`, one kernel dispatch with total capacity, distribute bytes across iovecs); `pmos_ext.{ipc_socket, ipc_bind, ipc_listen, ipc_connect, ipc_accept}` (five new PMos-extension shims that translate each call into the corresponding opcode). `shared/syscall.ts` `OP_EXT` table now covers 12 opcodes (was 5). `just build` manifest entry count 18 → 19. First proof that every IPC opcode works through REAL user wasm — not just direct `KernelWasmHost.dispatch` calls from a Rust test. The binary's exit code is the step-by-step diagnostic (codes 10-18 for each failed step; 0 for green), and the test's observable — the received bytes echoed to `/dev/console` via the final `fd_write(1, ...)` — proves the bytes actually traversed the kernel's IPC state machine. This closes the "runway to display server" — what remains is: a no_std display-server-ish binary that binds `/run/display` via the now-working IPC opcodes, plus the framebuffer-driver TS-side wiring that routes `pmos_host_driver_call(Framebuffer, ...)` into `fb.ts`'s canvas blit.

After the **framebuffer pipeline slice** (path_open WASI shim + onFramebufferWrite callback + hello-framebuffer binary): 830 + 257 = **1087 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (framebuffer acceptance test). Rust unchanged. New crate `crates/hello-framebuffer/` (379-byte cdylib) imports exactly three WASI functions (`path_open`, `fd_write`, `proc_exit`) and writes 16 bytes of RGBA pixel data (red/green/blue/white) to `/dev/fb0`. The kernel routes `fd_write` on `FdObject::CharDevice(DEV_FB0)` through `DeviceDispatcher::framebuffer_write` → `Platform::driver_call(DevId::Framebuffer, ...)` → `pmos_host_driver_call` host import → `KernelWasmHost`'s routing closure → `options.onFramebufferWrite(bytes)` callback. TS shim additions: `wasi_snapshot_preview1.path_open` (9-arg WASI signature collapsed to a PMos PATH_OPEN dispatch with just `path` + `flags=0`; the other WASI args — dirfd, dirflags, oflags, rights_base/inheriting, fdflags — are ignored for now since the kernel's path_open semantics don't yet distinguish them). `KernelWasmHostOptions` gains `onFramebufferWrite`; the `pmos_host_driver_call` closure now routes `DEV.FRAMEBUFFER` as well as `DEV.CONSOLE` (input/block/net still unrouted but accept the call without error). `just build` manifest entry count 19 → 20. This is the first slice where user wasm sends bytes the TS host recognizes as "pixels destined for the framebuffer" — a Playwright-level test could take these bytes and actually blit to canvas; in vitest we only prove the bytes reach the callback intact. Combined with the IPC slice before it, the "pixels via the constitutional path" runway is now really short: what's left is a Rust display-server-ish binary that owns `/run/display` + receives client draw commands + composites + writes resulting pixels to `/dev/fb0`, then Playwright/browser-side wiring of `onFramebufferWrite` to an actual canvas blit.

After the **display-server-lite end-to-end slice** (DISPLAY_BIND opcode + display_server_lite.wasm): 833 + 258 = **1091 passing, 0 failing**. Rust +3 (new `DISPLAY_BIND` opcode tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` — happy path installs a listener at `/run/display`, missing `DISPLAY_SERVER` cap returns `-ENOTCAPABLE`, second caller against an already-bound path returns `-EADDRINUSE`). TS +1 (the end-to-end composition test). Dispatcher opcode count 19 → 20.

After the **kernel opcode breadth #3 slice** (args/environ/fdstat/prestat bootstrap quartet): 840 + 258 = **1098 passing, 0 failing**. Rust +7 tests; TS unchanged. Six new WASI opcode handlers: `ARGS_SIZES_GET`, `ARGS_GET`, `ENVIRON_SIZES_GET`, `ENVIRON_GET`, `FD_FDSTAT_GET`, `FD_PRESTAT_GET`. Dispatcher opcode count 20 → 26. The four args/environ handlers return the v1 "empty argv, empty envp" answer: *_SIZES_GET writes two zero u32s into the heap scratch, *_GET is a trivial noop-success. `FD_FDSTAT_GET` looks up the caller's fd via `Kernel::fds(pid)?.get(fd)`, maps the `FdObject` variant to a WASI filetype byte (`CharDevice → 2`, `Socket/DisplayConn → 6`, `Vnode → 4`, pipe/signal → 0 Unknown), and writes a 24-byte `fdstat_t` with fs_flags=0 and rights_base/rights_inheriting=u64::MAX (so userland doesn't reject ops on rights grounds before the kernel does; real rights tracking is a future slice). Invalid fd returns `-EBADF`. `FD_PRESTAT_GET` returns `-EBADF` unconditionally — PMos has no preopen directories, so the WASI preopen-discovery loop hits EBADF at the first probe and terminates cleanly. Rationale for the slice: these six opcodes are the minimum set any Rust `std` binary touches during libc init before `main` runs. Before this slice, a `std` binary would hit `-ENOSYS` on one of them and panic; after it, the init path completes and the binary reaches `main`. No user binary exercises these yet (all test cdylibs are `#![no_std]` with hand-written imports), so the proof is isolation tests only — each opcode has one positive path and one error path. Kernel.wasm unchanged in this slice since the TS shim hasn't picked the new opcodes up; that's a follow-up slice when we first want to run a `std` binary.

After the **WASI bootstrap quartet through real wasm slice**: 840 + 259 = **1099 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (new composition test); Rust unchanged (the new cdylib is `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`-gated like the rest). Six new TS WASI shims in `user-wasm-runtime.ts`: `args_sizes_get`, `args_get`, `environ_sizes_get`, `environ_get`, `fd_fdstat_get`, `fd_prestat_get`. `args_sizes_get` and `environ_sizes_get` share a private `sizes_get` helper that dispatches the opcode, reads the two u32s from the 8-byte heap-out window, and stores them at the user-memory out-pointers. `args_get` / `environ_get` are trivial noop-successes on v1 (argc=0 means nothing to write). `fd_fdstat_get` dispatches with a 24-byte heap-out window and copies the `fdstat_t` bytes into user memory at the caller's `buf_ptr`. `fd_prestat_get` ignores the out-pointer (the kernel always returns `-EBADF`) and just propagates the errno. `OP_WASI` gains `ARGS_GET`, `ARGS_SIZES_GET`, `ENVIRON_GET`, `ENVIRON_SIZES_GET`, `FD_FDSTAT_GET`, `FD_PRESTAT_GET` constants. New crate `crates/hello-wasi-bootstrap/` (824-byte cdylib) imports 8 functions (6 new + `fd_write` + `proc_exit`) and exercises each in order, with step-specific exit codes (10..16) on any mismatch. On full success writes `"bootstrap ok\n"` to `/dev/console` and exits 0. First slice where the new kernel handlers are actually live in the distributed `kernel.wasm` (size 143 KiB → 141 KiB — LTO dropped some debug symbols from earlier slices). `just build` manifest entry count 21 → 22. The composition test asserts the exit code is 0 AND the concatenated console output is exactly `"bootstrap ok\n"`, so a regression in any single opcode surfaces as a specific non-zero exit code. This closes the "can a Rust std binary boot?" question: the six opcodes touched during libc init all work end-to-end through real wasm.

After the **FramebufferDriver end-to-end slice** (OP_BLIT framed path reaches fb:blit messages): 840 + 260 = **1100 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (new composition test); Rust unchanged. New KernelWasmHost options `framebufferDriver?: Driver` + `onFramebufferMessage?: (msg: unknown) => void`. When `framebufferDriver` is set, the host calls `driver.init(driverHost)` once at construction — `driverHost.postToMain` forwards to `onFramebufferMessage`, `pushInputToKernel` is a no-op (fb is write-only). On every `driver_call(Framebuffer, ...)` the host reads the first byte of the payload as the driver op (e.g. `fb.OP_SET_MODE = 0x01`, `fb.OP_BLIT = 0x02`), passes `payload.subarray(1)` to `driver.call(op, ...)`, which decodes the typed message and posts it via the driver host. Both callbacks can coexist: `onFramebufferWrite` (raw bytes) fires first, then the framed driver call — callers pick by setting whichever one they want. New crate `crates/hello-fb-blit/` (512-byte cdylib) opens `/dev/fb0`, writes a 9-byte `[OP_SET_MODE, w=2, h=2]` framing, then a 25-byte `[OP_BLIT, w=2, h=2, ...16 RGBA bytes]` framing. Exit codes 10-12 encode per-step failure, 0 for success. The composition test wires a real `FramebufferDriver` into `KernelWasmHost`, captures the two posted messages (`fb:set-mode` then `fb:blit`) via `onFramebufferMessage`, and asserts: exit code 0; exactly two messages in order; dimensions (2, 2) on both; the blit's `rgba` array is the exact 16-byte RGBA sequence. This closes the gap between the raw-bytes `onFramebufferWrite` callback (what hello-framebuffer + display-server-lite use) and the typed `fb:set-mode` / `fb:blit` surface that `FbHost` + canvas blit already consume on the mock-kernel path. A future display-server binary can write OP_BLIT frames to `/dev/fb0` and the output reaches the same main-thread handler chain a MockKernel blit hits today — the canvas wire-up is now the same code path for both. `just build` manifest entry count 22 → 23. New kernel handler `handle_display_bind` wraps `Kernel::display_bind` (already existed from an earlier semantic slice; this slice just gives it an opcode). `DISPLAY_BIND` opcode constant is `0x1201` in both `abi::ext` (Rust) and `OP_EXT.DISPLAY_BIND` (TS). TS shim additions: `pmos_ext.display_bind` + `pmos_ext.display_connect` (no args, both return either a positive fd or a negative errno). New crate `crates/display-server-lite/` (761-byte cdylib) composes the full pipeline in one self-connecting binary: (1) `display_bind()` → listener fd on `/run/display`, (2) `display_connect()` → client fd, (3) `ipc_accept(listener)` → server fd, (4) `fd_write(client, PIXELS)` pushes 16 bytes of RGBA (red/green/blue/white) through the display socket, (5) `fd_read(server, buf)` reads them back, (6) `path_open("/dev/fb0")` → framebuffer fd, (7) `fd_write(fb_fd, buf)` forwards the received bytes — deliberately reading from `buf` not the `PIXELS` constant so the test proves bytes actually traversed the IPC socket rather than short-circuiting through memory. Self-connection works because the binary holds `CAPSET_ALL` and the IPC state machine indexes by `SocketId`. Exit codes 10-16 encode each step's failure point; 0 for success. `KernelWasmHost` gains a `spawnHistoryEntries` getter + `SpawnHistoryEntry` export type — `drainPendingSpawns` now records `{pid, path, exitCode}` for each finished child so tests can assert per-child outcomes (the prior shape threw exit codes away; this is a test-only affordance that's safe under the current sequential in-process model). `just build` manifest entry count 20 → 21. First slice where user wasm drives the **constitutional path** end-to-end: user bytes → display IPC socket → user bytes → `/dev/fb0` → kernel `driver_call` → TS host `onFramebufferWrite`. The remaining runway to "a desktop window" is the protocol layer (display-proto crate), a real multi-process display-server binary, and Playwright wiring to actually blit `onFramebufferWrite` bytes onto a canvas. Debugging note carried forward for next session: when adding new kernel opcodes, **rebuild `kernel.wasm` explicitly** via `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p kernel --no-default-features` — `cargo check` updates debug builds but not the release artefact the TS tests load, and a stale `kernel.wasm` surfaces as `-ENOSYS` from any unhandled opcode.

After the **input path end-to-end slice** (injected /dev/input_kbd bytes echo to /dev/console through real user wasm): 840 + 262 = **1102 passing, 0 failing**. TS +2 (new composition test + a split assertion). Rust unchanged. Two new `#[no_mangle] extern "C"` kernel exports in `wasm_entry.rs`: `kernel_inject_input_kbd(len: u32) -> i32` and `kernel_inject_input_mouse(len: u32) -> i32` — same shape as the existing `kernel_inject_console_input`, routing to `DeviceDispatcher::inject_kbd_event` / `inject_mouse_event` which already existed from the test harness. `KernelWasmHost.injectInput(devnum, bytes)` grows a routing map: `DEV.CONSOLE` → `kernel_inject_console_input`, `DEV.INPUT_KBD` → `kernel_inject_input_kbd`, `DEV.INPUT_MOUSE` → `kernel_inject_input_mouse`; other devnums throw a descriptive error. Pre-existing `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts` "non-console rejected" test was updated in-place — split into "three wired devs accepted" + "unrouted rejected" (`DEV.BLOCK` is the negative probe now). New crate `crates/hello-input-echo/` (516-byte cdylib) path-opens `/dev/input_kbd` (v1 devfs is FLAT — paths are `/dev/input_kbd`, not `/dev/input/kbd`; note for future binaries), does one `fd_read` into a 64-byte stack buffer, then `fd_write(1, ...)` echoes the bytes back to stdout. Exit codes 10-13 encode per-step failure. The composition test injects `"Hi!\n"` via `kernel.injectInput(DEV.INPUT_KBD, ...)` BEFORE `drainPendingSpawns` — the sequential in-process dispatcher doesn't block reads, so the input ring must be pre-populated. **Gotcha for the next input-style slice**: `/dev/console` writes are line-buffered and only flush to `onConsoleWrite` on `'\n'`, so test bytes must include a trailing newline or sit in the kernel's line buffer forever. Asserts exit code 0 AND exactly one `onConsoleWrite` call with the verbatim 4-byte `"Hi!\n"`. First proof that input → kernel → user wasm → console round-trips end-to-end through real wasm. `just build` manifest entry count 23 → 24. kernel.wasm 144480 → 144890 bytes (two new inject exports + reachable graph now live).

After the **first real Rust std binary slice** (hello-std: println! + fn main runs to completion): 840 + 263 = **1103 passing, 0 failing**. TS +1 (capstone composition test). Rust unchanged. New crate `crates/hello-std/` — a `bin` target (not cdylib) with the full Rust `std` linked in. Cargo produces `hello-std.wasm` at 40 KiB (vs. ~800 bytes for the no_std cdylibs). Note: bin targets preserve dashes in the output filename, so the xtask manifest entry uses `hello-std` as both crate name AND file stem (unlike the cdylibs which have `foo-bar` crate + `foo_bar.wasm` filename). Discovered WASI import surface by greping the compiled wasm: exactly four functions — `fd_write`, `proc_exit`, `environ_get`, `environ_sizes_get`. All four were already wired on both the kernel side (`syscall/wasi.rs` handlers from the earlier opcode-breadth slices) and the TS shim side (`user-wasm-runtime.ts` from the WASI-bootstrap-through-real-wasm slice). The composition test wires a binary registry → spawns `/bin/hello-std` via PROC_SPAWN → drains → asserts exit 0 AND the concatenated console output is exactly `"hello from std\n"`. This is the capstone to the three prior slices (kernel opcode breadth #3, WASI bootstrap TS shims, hello-wasi-bootstrap): a conventional Rust program with no PMos-specific awareness — just `fn main() { println!(...); }` — runs to completion through the real kernel. Every opcode the std startup path touches is proven. Manifest entry count 24 → 25. **Why this matters for the runway**: everything from here (display server, toolkit, bundled apps, desktop shell) can now be written as ordinary std Rust code. The WASI shim doesn't need any more bootstrap expansion unless a specific std API needs an opcode that isn't yet handled (e.g., filesystem stat, network, threads) — and when that happens, the failure mode is a clean `-ENOSYS` at the missing opcode, not a silent corruption or panic.

After the **KernelWasmHost-into-entry slice** (useRealKernel boot flag + onConsoleWrite postMessage routing + boot-binary auto-spawn through real wasm): 840 + 267 = **1107 passing, 0 failing**. TS +4. Rust unchanged. `BootConfig` gains `useRealKernel?: boolean` + `bootBinary?: string`; `installWorkerEntry(messaging, options?)` accepts a second arg with `kernelWasmBytes?: BufferSource` + `binaryRegistry?: ReadonlyMap<string, BufferSource>`. With `useRealKernel: true` the entry async-constructs a `KernelWasmHost` from the injected bytes (Worker-scope fetch lands in the next slice), wires `onConsoleWrite` → `console:write` postMessage so the existing `ConsoleHost` picks up real-kernel output without changes, and forwards `binaryRegistry` into the host's default `onSpawnProcess`. With `bootBinary` also set, the entry runs the choreography from `kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`'s spawn flow: register an `init` pid (CAPSET_ALL + console fd 0/1/2 + Running), dispatch `PROC_SPAWN(bootBinary)`, `drainPendingSpawns` until every transitive child exits, then resolve `whenReady`. The `WorkerEntry` handle exposes `realKernel: KernelWasmHost | undefined` for tests. Capstone test loads real `kernel.wasm` + real `hello-std.wasm` from disk, drives the full boot through the entry's `WorkerMessaging`, asserts the captured `console:write` payload is exactly `"hello from std\n"` — first proof that a real Rust `std` binary boots end-to-end through the production entry-point. Default flag-off path keeps every existing test green by construction.

After the **build-chain + Worker-scope fetch slice** (kernel-worker.js into dist + fetcher fallback for kernel.wasm and binary registry): 840 + 269 = **1109 passing, 0 failing**. TS +2. Rust unchanged. Build-chain half: Justfile's `just build` target now esbuilds `kernel-worker-entry.ts` into `build/assets/kernel-worker.js` and `xtask assemble-dist` copies it into `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js`. Before this slice the file was either stale or absent — the entry's `useRealKernel` path could never reach a real Worker without it. Manifest entry count 25 → 26. Worker-fetch half: `WorkerEntryOptions` gains `fetcher?: (url) => Promise<ArrayBuffer>` defaulting to `globalThis.fetch`. When `kernelWasmBytes` is absent, the entry calls `fetcher("/assets/kernel.wasm")`. When `binaryRegistry` is absent and a `bootBinary` is configured, the entry additionally fetches `/manifest.json`, walks every `assets/bin/*.wasm` entry, fetches each via `Promise.all`, and builds a `ReadonlyMap` keyed by `/bin/<stem>` (matching `KernelWasmHost.create`'s default `onSpawnProcess` lookup). Tests inject a fetcher mock; production hits the network from Worker scope. Two new tests: one boots with only `kernelWasmBytes` absent + a fetcher mock, asserts `["/assets/kernel.wasm"]` was the only URL pulled and `realKernel` is set. The other boots with both `kernelWasmBytes` AND `binaryRegistry` absent + a manifest mock, asserts the manifest was pulled, only the bin/*.wasm asset was followed up with a binary fetch (decoy `bootstrap.js` and `kernel.wasm` outside `bin/` are ignored), and the boot binary's console output reaches the messaging channel.

After the **bootstrap.ts real-kernel mode + first Playwright slice**: workspace at **1110 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 840 + TS-unit 269 + TS-integration 1). Rust + TS-unit unchanged; +1 Playwright integration test. `bootstrap.ts` gains a `runRealKernelMode()` branch at the top of `main()` triggered by `window.location.hash.includes("real-kernel")`. That function spawns the kernel Worker with `useRealKernel: true` + `bootBinary: "/bin/hello-std"`, wires a `ConsoleHost`, and forwards every flushed `/dev/console` line to `console.log` with a `[real-kernel]` prefix. Lifecycle events emit `[pmos-bootstrap] real kernel ready` / `... real kernel panic: <message>` so a Playwright test has both positive and negative observables. New `web/playwright.config.ts` configures a single chromium project with the xtask dev-server as the `webServer` (PATH-prefixed because the dev sandbox does not put cargo on the default PATH). Port 8081 to avoid the default 8080 colliding with `just dev`. New `web/vitest.config.ts` restricts unit-test discovery to `tests/unit/**/*.test.ts` so vitest stops failing on `tests/integration/*.spec.ts` (Playwright rejects `test()` calls outside its own runner). The first Playwright test (`real-kernel.spec.ts`) navigates to `/index.html#real-kernel`, scrapes `page.on('console', ...)`, asserts `[real-kernel] hello from std` appears, asserts `real kernel ready` appears, asserts no panic line. Cold-path on local dev-server is ~190 ms; test caps at 15 s for slow CI. **Gotcha carried forward**: when modifying `kernel-worker-entry.ts` or its imports, ALWAYS rerun `just build` before committing — the dist/assets/kernel-worker.js bundle is committed alongside source, and a missed rebuild lands a stale bundle that ships old behaviour. The earlier slice 4a+4b commit (`171c10a`) shipped a stale bundle for this reason; the slice 4c+4d commit re-bundled it correctly.

After the **DOM surface for real-kernel output slice**: workspace **1111 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (unchanged counts). Strengthened Playwright assertions; no new tests. `bootstrap.ts`'s `runRealKernelMode()` now mounts a `<pre id="pmos-real-console">` into the body and appends every `/dev/console` line into it, hiding the unused `#pmos-fb` boot-screen canvas via `style.display = "none"`. Panics flush to both the page console (as before) and the DOM with a `[panic] ` prefix. The Playwright spec reads `#pmos-real-console.innerText` and asserts every init + hello-std line appears AND `init starting` comes before `hello from std` in the rendered text (same ordering guarantee the page-console check already covered). Before this slice, `/index.html#real-kernel` produced a blank page with output only in dev tools. Styling is a minimal cream-on-dark monospace inlined on the element — no framework, no dependencies.

After the **real Rust std init binary slice** (init spawns hello-std via pmos_ext.proc_spawn): workspace at **1111 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 840 + TS-unit 270 + TS-integration 1). TS-unit +1 (composition test); Rust unchanged; TS-integration count unchanged but assertions strengthened. `crates/init/src/main.rs` is no longer a stub. It's now a real Rust `std` binary: `println!("init starting")`, `extern "C" { fn proc_spawn(...) -> i32; }` from `pmos_ext` gated on `#[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]`, call `proc_spawn(b"/bin/hello-std", u64::MAX)`, `println!` either `"init spawned hello-std pid=N"` on success or `"init: proc_spawn /bin/hello-std failed errno=N"` on failure, then `println!("init exiting")`. The native target gets an inert `unsafe fn proc_spawn(...) -> i32 { 0 }` stub so `cargo build --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` still link the bin target; only `cargo check --workspace` was passing without the stub because check skips linking. Vitest composition test in `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: loads init.wasm + hello-std.wasm + kernel.wasm, dispatches `PROC_SPAWN(/bin/init)` on behalf of a synthetic boot-loader pid, `drainPendingSpawns`, asserts the spawn history has exactly two entries (init → hello-std, both exit 0) AND the concatenated `/dev/console` output splits into exactly four lines in the exact order `["init starting", /^init spawned hello-std pid=\d+$/, "init exiting", "hello from std"]`. The ordering is load-bearing — it certifies that `drainPendingSpawns` is sequential in v1 (init fully runs and exits before hello-std starts); a concurrent drain would interleave the lines. bootstrap.ts's `runRealKernelMode()` flips its `bootBinary` from `/bin/hello-std` to `/bin/init`; the Playwright test updates to assert the same four ordered lines reach the page console via index comparisons on the captured `page.on('console', ...)` array. Cold-path ~180 ms, unchanged from the prior hello-std-only test because the extra work is a few hundred bytes of output + one drain-loop iteration. This is the capstone to the `useRealKernel` arc: a real Rust `std` binary → extension syscall → second real Rust `std` binary → browser console, all through production code paths with no mocks on any layer. The init crate's shape is a single-shot launcher (it exits after spawning once) because the sequential in-process drain can't support a looping parent; the looping/supervision behaviour a real OS init needs lands with the Worker-per-pid seam.

After the **PROC_RAISE WASI opcode slice** (WASI raise(3) self-signal): workspace **1613 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1155 → 1159 +4, TS-unit 449 → 452 +3, Playwright 2 → 2). New WASI opcode handler `handle_proc_raise` at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs` for opcode `0x0061` (was ENOSYS pre-slice; ENOSYS probe at FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS `0x0026` unchanged since PROC_RAISE was never the probe target). Wire layout: args[0..2] = signum u16 (no target_pid — raise(3) always targets the caller, so the layout is 14 bytes shorter than PROC_KILL's). Decode matches PROC_KILL's signum→Signal mapping (SIGINT=2 / SIGKILL=9 / SIGTERM=15; anything else → EINVAL before the kernel is touched) and delegates to `Kernel::proc_kill(pid, pid, signal)` — the self-signal cap path is the only one exercised, so raise(3) never reports ENOTCAPABLE regardless of the caller's CapSet. SIGKILL zombifies the caller immediately (response still posts back before the dispatch loop tears the Worker down on the next pass); SIGTERM/SIGINT queue on the caller's own SignalInbox and leave state Running. TS side: new `PROC_RAISE: 0x0061` constant in `web/src/shared/syscall.ts`'s `OP_WASI` table + a new `proc_raise(signum: i32) -> errno` shim in `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts`'s `wasi_snapshot_preview1` namespace (packs signum into a 16-byte args window at offset 0 as u16 LE, dispatches `OP_WASI.PROC_RAISE`, returns WASI-style positive errno on error / 0 on success — inverse sign convention from the adjacent `proc_kill` PMos-ext shim which returns negative errno directly, because proc_raise is a WASI syscall and WASI's errno convention is positive). 4 Rust tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (proc_raise_sigterm_queues_on_own_inbox, proc_raise_sigint_queues_on_own_inbox, proc_raise_sigkill_zombifies_caller, proc_raise_unknown_signum_returns_einval) + 3 TS tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`'s new "dispatch: PROC_RAISE" describe block (EINVAL on bad signum, self-SIGINT returns 0, self-SIGKILL returns 0). No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Kernel public-method / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes (PROC_RAISE was already declared in `abi::wasi` as `0x0061`); no wire-format changes to any existing opcode. Closes one of the last remaining single-slice-sized gaps in the v1 WASI surface; contract-side behaviour matches `specs/001-browser-os-v1/contracts/syscalls.md`'s `proc_raise` row exactly ("delivers signal-equivalent to self via the signal channel"). **Rebuild discipline carried forward**: `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p kernel --no-default-features` is required after kernel opcode additions — a stale `kernel.wasm` surfaces as `-ENOSYS` on the new opcode (caught the first TS test run; fixed by rebuild).

After the **SIGPIPE delivery slice** (POSIX-correct SIGPIPE posted alongside EPIPE on broken-pipe writes): workspace **1618 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1159 → 1164 +5, TS-unit 452 → 452, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only change — no TS shim, no new opcode, no ABI addition. New `Signal::Pipe = 13` variant in `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs` joins Kill/Term/Interrupt; `is_catchable` returns true for Pipe via the existing `!matches!(Kill)` default. `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: `Kernel::fd_write` refactored from match-with-`?` to match-to-`Result<usize, KernelError>` binding so the `Err(PipeBroken)` outcome can be observed before return (the prior `?` early-return bypassed any post-match work on the Socket arm — that was the initial test failure on the broken-socket case, fixed by the refactor). After the match, `matches!(result, Err(KernelError::PipeBroken))` triggers a new `Kernel::post_sigpipe(pid)` helper that posts `Signal::Pipe` to the target's SignalInbox (silent no-op if inbox missing — defensive for test-only pid-removal races). `Kernel::proc_kill`'s catchable-signal arm grows to cover `Signal::Pipe` in the same `inbox.post(...)` branch as Term/Interrupt so the variant is first-class for any future `kill(pid, SIGPIPE)` wire (the `handle_proc_kill` ext-opcode arm still accepts only signums 2/9/15 — userland cannot synthesize SIGPIPE through PROC_KILL in this slice; SIGPIPE remains kernel-generated-only, matching POSIX's typical usage). `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: `handle_sock_send`'s error arm bifurcates — if the mapped `KernelError` is `PipeBroken`, call `kernel.post_sigpipe(pid)` before encoding the errno into the Response; other IpcError variants (ConnectionRefused, InvalidState, etc.) pass through without touching the inbox. 5 new Rust tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (fd_write_on_broken_pipe_posts_sigpipe_alongside_epipe, fd_write_on_broken_socket_posts_sigpipe_alongside_epipe, sock_send_on_broken_socket_posts_sigpipe_alongside_epipe, successful_fd_write_on_pipe_does_not_queue_sigpipe, repeated_broken_writes_coalesce_to_single_sigpipe_entry — the last asserts that `SignalInbox::post`'s dedup logic applies at the syscall layer without needing any additional coalescing in the handlers). Closes the documented gap in `crates/kernel/src/ipc/pipe.rs`'s header ("conceptually SIGPIPE; the kernel translates this into the right errno at syscall-dispatch time") — the errno translation lived since ca5bb8a, the matching signal posting lands now. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket variant additions; one new catchable `Signal` variant (`Signal::Pipe = 13`, pre-declared in `abi::ext::sig::SIGPIPE = 13`); one new `Kernel` public helper (`post_sigpipe`, not a signature change to existing methods); no new KernelError variants (reuses PipeBroken); no new Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes. ENOSYS probes unchanged (WASI: FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS `0x0026`; ext: CAP_GRANT `0x1302`). Userland cannot yet see the SIGPIPE — there is no `FdObject::SignalChannel` read path in the ABI — so the inbox content is observable only via `Kernel::pending_signals` / `drain_signals` from test code; a future slice wiring signal-delivery-via-fd will promote SIGPIPE (and Term/Interrupt) to an fd-readable stream. Existing TS tests that drive broken-pipe writes through a real `kernel.wasm` continue to pass unmodified — the `-EPIPE` return is unchanged; only kernel-internal state grew.

After the **SignalChannel read path slice** (`fd_read` + `POLL_ONEOFF` on `FdObject::SignalChannel`): workspace **1627 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1164 → 1173 +9, TS-unit 452 → 452, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only change. The immediate follow-up to cbe8959's SIGPIPE delivery slice — post-SIGPIPE the signal was queued on the inbox but userland had no fd-based way to see it; this slice wires that path. `FdObject::SignalChannel` was pre-declared but every op on it returned `NotSupportedOnFd` / per-subscription `EINVAL`. Post-slice, `fd_read` on it drains the caller's `SignalInbox` as u16 LE pairs (2 bytes per signal, FIFO with dedup-coalescing preserved), and `POLL_ONEOFF FD_READ` reports ready with `nbytes = 2 * pending` when the inbox is non-empty. Empty inbox surfaces `-EAGAIN` on `fd_read` (matches the socket / pipe empty-read convention); `buf.len() < 2` surfaces a 0-byte success without consuming anything from the inbox (lossless partial-buffer semantics — the signal stays queued for the next read). `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs`: new `SignalInbox::drain_bounded(max)` drains at most `max` entries from the head leaving the tail intact in order. `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: new `Kernel::drain_signals_up_to(pid, max)` helper wrapping `drain_bounded`; `Kernel::fd_read`'s `SignalChannel` arm upgraded from `NotSupportedOnFd` to the drain-and-serialise flow; `PipeWrite` and `DisplayConn` remain `NotSupportedOnFd`. `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: new internal `signal_channel_readiness` helper wired into `fd_readiness` via a new arm; `fd_readiness` signature gains a `pid: Pid` parameter (syscall-internal function, no public-API change) — needed so the SignalChannel arm can look up the caller's inbox via `kernel.pending_signals(pid)`. 2 new `SignalInbox` inline tests (`drain_bounded_returns_prefix_in_order_and_leaves_remainder`, `drain_bounded_with_zero_returns_empty_and_preserves_queue`); 7 new / updated `syscall.rs` tests: `poll_oneoff_fd_read_on_empty_signal_channel_not_ready` (replaces the prior `..._emits_einval_event` — empty is not-ready not EINVAL post-slice), `poll_oneoff_fd_read_on_signal_channel_with_pending_signals_ready` (SIGTERM + SIGPIPE → `nbytes = 4`, inbox unchanged since poll doesn't drain), `poll_oneoff_fd_write_on_signal_channel_emits_einval` (read-only variant), `fd_read_on_signal_channel_with_empty_inbox_returns_eagain`, `fd_read_on_signal_channel_drains_pending_signals_as_u16_pairs` (FIFO order assert), `fd_read_on_signal_channel_with_one_byte_buffer_returns_zero_preserving_queue`, `fd_read_on_signal_channel_with_partial_buffer_drains_only_what_fits` (3 signals, 2-byte buffer → 1 drained, 2 stay, next read drains the rest), `fd_read_on_signal_channel_coalesces_repeated_same_signal` (10 SIGTERMs dedup to one 2-byte record). No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket variant additions; two new `Kernel` public helpers (`drain_signals_up_to`); no new `KernelError` variants; no new `Cap` variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no request-format changes (only the response-side payload shape on SignalChannel reads is new — bytes-in-heap-scratch for a variant that previously couldn't be read at all). Userland-side install of SignalChannel at fd 3 at proc_spawn time is NOT part of this slice — tests install via `k.install_fd(pid, 3, FdObject::SignalChannel, ...)` explicitly; a future auto-install slice at proc_spawn will promote the variant to "every process has fd 3 readable from birth" which completes the POSIX signalfd analogue. ENOSYS probes unchanged (WASI: FD_FDSTAT_SET_RIGHTS `0x0026`; ext: CAP_GRANT `0x1302`). No TS shim, no ABI addition, no TS test changes — this slice is purely kernel-internal activation of a pre-declared fd variant.

After the **SignalChannel auto-install slice** (`proc_spawn` installs `FdObject::SignalChannel` at fd 3 on every child): workspace **1629 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1173 → 1175 +2, TS-unit 452 → 452, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only change. Completes the POSIX signalfd analogue that 18c2466 started: `fd_read` / `POLL_ONEOFF` on SignalChannel exist, but users had to manually install the variant via `k.install_fd(pid, 3, ...)` before this slice — post-slice, every process built through `Kernel::proc_spawn` inherits a live signal channel at fd 3 from startup without any explicit install. `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: `Kernel::proc_spawn` gains one `install_at(3, FdEntry::new(FdObject::SignalChannel))` after the stdio 0/1/2 install loop. No `inherit_object` call is needed — `SignalChannel` carries no object-side reference count (signal inboxes are keyed by pid, not by fd). `Kernel::register_process` (the lower-level test primitive used by `make_running_proc`) deliberately does NOT auto-install — the 38 isolation tests in `syscall.rs` that manually install fd 3 with a different object all use that path and continue to work unchanged. One assertion added to `proc_spawn_creates_child_with_stdio_and_marks_ready` (sys.rs) and one to `proc_spawn_creates_child_and_records_platform_spawn_call` (syscall.rs — exercises the PROC_SPAWN opcode end-to-end, not just the Rust API). Two new `sys.rs` tests: `proc_spawn_signal_channel_fd_reads_signals_posted_by_parent` (parent → `proc_kill(child, SIGTERM)` → child `fd_read(3, buf)` returns 2 bytes u16 LE = 15), `proc_spawn_signal_channel_read_on_empty_inbox_returns_would_block` (fresh child, no pending signals → `KernelError::WouldBlock`). Impact on existing binaries: `display-server-lite`, `hello-framebuffer`, and every other proc_spawn'd wasm use whatever fd numbers WASI returns — they don't hard-code fd 3, so their fd allocations start at 4 post-slice instead of 3 without any observable change. `FD_PRESTAT_GET` still returns EBADF for all fds (PMos has no preopens), so WASI libc preopen loops terminate at fd 3 the same way they did pre-slice — the loop hits EBADF at the probe before ever dispatching against the installed SignalChannel entry. `FD_FDSTAT_GET` on SignalChannel already returns filetype=UNKNOWN + rights=u64::MAX from the opcode-breadth-#3 slice, so a libc probe gets a valid response. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Kernel public-method / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (the `SpawnManifest` layout is unchanged — callers don't supply fd 3; the kernel installs it unconditionally). ENOSYS probes unchanged. TS side: no shim changes, no test changes — existing TS tests use `host.registerProcess` which routes through `register_process` (no auto-install), and `PROC_SPAWN` composition tests don't inspect fd 3 directly.

After the **SIGCHLD delivery slice** (kernel-generated SIGCHLD to parent on child Zombie transition + SIGPIPE/SIGCHLD accepted by PROC_KILL/PROC_RAISE): workspace **1638 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1175 → 1184 +9, TS-unit 452 → 452, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only change. Completes the parent-supervision side of the signal-delivery trilogy (cbe8959 SIGPIPE → 18c2466 fd_read SignalChannel → 9fbe708 auto-install fd 3): the 9fbe708 slice made `fd 3 = SignalChannel` automatic, but nothing was generating SIGCHLD when a child died, so a parent polling fd 3 couldn't observe child-exit events. Post-slice, `Kernel::proc_exit` and `Kernel::proc_kill`'s `Signal::Kill` arm both post `Signal::Child = 17` to the exiting pid's parent, so a supervisor process that spawns children and polls fd 3 observes every exit exactly once — regardless of whether the child called `proc_exit` voluntarily or was zombified by SIGKILL from outside. Orphans (ppid == 0) and parents whose inbox has been reaped silently no-op via the new `Kernel::post_sigchld(ppid)` helper. `crates/kernel/src/proc/signal.rs`: new `Signal::Child = 17` variant; `is_catchable` returns true via the `!matches!(Kill)` default; module header updated to describe the catchable set {Term, Interrupt, Pipe, Child} + fd 3 drain path + kernel-generated origins of SIGPIPE (broken-pipe write) and SIGCHLD (child Zombie transition). `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs`: new `Kernel::post_sigchld` helper + one call from `proc_exit` (reads ppid before the exit call, transitions pid to Zombie, then posts SIGCHLD) + one call from `proc_kill`'s SIGKILL arm (reads target.ppid before the exit call, zombifies, posts SIGCHLD) so voluntary exit and SIGKILL paths deliver symmetrically. `proc_kill`'s catchable-signal arm grows to cover `Signal::Child` alongside Term/Interrupt/Pipe so userland-synthesised SIGCHLD queues on the target's own inbox (distinct from the kernel-generated path which targets the *parent*). `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs` + `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs`: `handle_proc_kill` + `handle_proc_raise` signum match arms grow by two each: `13 => Signal::Pipe`, `17 => Signal::Child`. Accepted set moves from {2, 9, 15} to {2, 9, 13, 15, 17}; unknown signums still reject EINVAL before the kernel is touched. Tests (+9 Rust): 5 in `crates/kernel/tests/sys.rs` (`proc_exit_posts_sigchld_to_parent_inbox`, `proc_kill_sigkill_posts_sigchld_to_parent_inbox`, `proc_kill_sigterm_does_not_post_sigchld_because_target_still_alive` — negative-case proof the post is strictly gated on exit, `orphan_process_exit_does_not_panic` — ppid == 0 defensive guard, `parent_fd_read_fd3_observes_sigchld_after_child_exit` — end-to-end capstone: init proc_spawns parent, parent proc_spawns grandchild, grandchild proc_exits, parent's fd 3 drains a 2-byte u16 LE = 17); 4 in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (`proc_raise_sigpipe_queues_on_own_inbox`, `proc_raise_sigchld_queues_on_own_inbox`, `proc_kill_sigpipe_queues_on_child_inbox`, `proc_kill_sigchld_queues_on_child_inbox`). Updated `proc_raise_unknown_signum_returns_einval` comment to note the post-slice accepted set. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket variant additions; one new catchable `Signal` variant (`Signal::Child = 17`, pre-declared in `abi::ext::sig::SIGCHLD = 17`); one new `Kernel` public helper (`post_sigchld`); no new `KernelError` / `Cap` variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (the signum field accepts a wider set — unknown signums still EINVAL, so no caller that previously got EINVAL on 13 or 17 depended on that specific errno). ENOSYS probes unchanged. TS side: no shim, no test changes — existing PROC_KILL / PROC_RAISE TS tests use signums from {2, 9, 15} which continue to behave identically; the new 13 / 17 arms are reachable from TS but not exercised yet. Completes the full signal-delivery trilogy for v1 (minus a future end-to-end wasm test that drives the SIGCHLD-through-fd-3 pipeline through a real user binary).

After the **TS composition test for signal drain via fd 3 slice**: workspace **1641 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1184 → 1184, TS-unit 452 → 455 +3, Playwright 2 → 2). TS-side proof that the signal-delivery pipeline's drain path (18c2466 fd_read on SignalChannel) is actually reachable in the distributed `kernel.wasm` — LTO could theoretically have stripped it even though Rust isolation tests passed, and only an end-to-end dispatch through the real wasm proves the code graph is intact. `crates/kernel/src/wasm_entry.rs`: new `#[no_mangle] extern "C" fn kernel_install_signal_channel_fd(pid, fd) -> i32` test-harness export that installs `FdObject::SignalChannel` at the requested fd in pid's fd table (companion to `kernel_install_console_fd`; returns 0 on success / -1 on any error). Production proc_spawn path auto-installs at fd 3 without this export; the export exists specifically for host-side tests that start from `register_process` (no auto-install) and want to exercise SignalChannel paths without routing through spawn. `web/src/kernel-wasm-host.ts`: new `KernelExports.kernel_install_signal_channel_fd` signature + new public `KernelWasmHost.installSignalChannelFd(pid, fd)` method mirroring `installConsoleFd`'s shape (throws on non-zero return). 3 new TS tests in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`'s new `describe("dispatch: FD_READ on SignalChannel")` block: `drains a self-raised SIGTERM as u16 LE 15` (PROC_RAISE(15) → FD_READ(3, 4) → value=2 + heapOut u16 LE 15), `drains a self-raised SIGPIPE as u16 LE 13` (same shape with signum 13 — exercises the accept arm added in 91c618f's SIGCHLD slice), `returns -EAGAIN when the signal inbox is empty` (FD_READ on empty-inbox SignalChannel → -EAGAIN). `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` rebuilt (committed alongside, since the bundle is tracked). No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; one new `kernel.wasm` export (`kernel_install_signal_channel_fd`, host-side test surface only — no opcode involvement); one new KernelWasmHost public method; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes. ENOSYS probes unchanged.

After the **hello-sigchld user wasm binary slice**: workspace **1642 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1184 → 1184, TS-unit 455 → 456 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). End-to-end proof of the signal-delivery pipeline through a REAL `wasm32-wasip1` user binary — no kernel / test-harness code touched, purely an end-to-end demonstration slice on top of the existing surface. New crate `crates/hello-sigchld/` (378-byte cdylib) imports three WASI functions (`fd_read`, `fd_write`, `proc_exit`; no `path_open` because fd 3 = SignalChannel is auto-installed by proc_spawn per 9fbe708) and loops `fd_read` on fd 3 with EAGAIN polling until a 2-byte u16 LE signum arrives, then writes the 2 bytes plus a trailing newline to fd 1 in one `fd_write` (the `/dev/console` driver is line-buffered — the newline forces a flush through `onConsoleWrite`). Exit codes 11/12/13/0 encode per-stage failure / success. Pattern mirrors hello-input-echo verbatim (same polling shape, same Ciovec/Iovec struct layout, same panic handler). New composition test in `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`: init proc_spawns `/bin/hello-sigchld` → child pid allocated with fd 3 auto-installed → init `PROC_KILL(child, 15)` queues SIGTERM on child's inbox via the parent-child cap path → `runAllSpawns` runs the child → first `fd_read(3, 4)` drains 2 bytes (no EAGAIN loop needed because the signal is pre-staged) → `fd_write(1, [0x0f, 0x00, 0x0a])` reaches `onConsoleWrite` → test decodes the first 2 bytes as u16 LE = 15. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-sigchld` to members; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-sigchld", "hello_sigchld")` to the USERLAND list so `just build` copies the release wasm to `dist/assets/bin/` (manifest entry count 29 → 30). No kernel, abi, or Platform code changes. No new opcodes, wire changes, or TS shim additions. ENOSYS probes unchanged. This closes the signal-delivery trilogy proof: every layer from kernel-generated SIGCHLD / SIGPIPE through SignalInbox queueing through fd 3 auto-installation through userland `fd_read` drain is exercised end-to-end by a real 378-byte wasm binary — the smallest possible user-space proof that the pipeline works through production code paths.

After the **SIGCHLD-via-fd3 end-to-end TS composition test slice**: workspace **1643 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1186 → 1186 via later slices, TS-unit 456 → 457 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). TS-only change. Closes the "parent observes child exit via fd 3" loop by running a REAL user wasm child (hello-wasi-min) and asserting init's fd 3 reads `Signal::Child = 17` as a u16 LE record after the child's `proc_exit`. init's fd 3 is staged via `kernel.installSignalChannelFd(init, 3)` (the 1c2a81c test-harness export) since `registerProcess` deliberately skips the proc_spawn auto-install path. The test also asserts that a second `fd_read` on fd 3 returns `-EAGAIN` post-drain — proves the `SignalInbox::post` dedup observable through the syscall layer. Test-only slice; one new `it(...)` block + one new `OP_WASI` import in `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts`. No kernel, abi, Cargo, or xtask changes. ENOSYS probes unchanged.

After the **SIGCHLD coalescing tests + ext.rs header refresh slice**: workspace **1645 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1184 → 1186 +2, TS-unit 457 → 457, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only slice. Two follow-on tests covering the POSIX coalesce + re-post contract for SIGCHLD delivery (already provided "for free" via `SignalInbox::post`'s dedup-on-identity policy; the tests pin the behaviour so a future refactor that splits the post/dedup paths doesn't silently regress the supervisor use case): `multiple_child_exits_coalesce_to_single_sigchld_in_parent_inbox` (three children exit; `pending_signals` stays at 1; drain returns `[Signal::Child]`) and `sigchld_reposts_after_drain_when_another_child_exits` (post-drain, a later child exit re-posts a fresh SIGCHLD — proves coalescing is per-still-pending-entry, not per-kernel-lifetime). Also refreshes the stale module header at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs`: removes the "only 5 of 15 ext opcodes are handled" claim (obsolete — 16 ext opcodes are now routed across IPC, PROC, DISPLAY, CAP families), adds a "Coverage" section listing every routed opcode, adds a "Remaining ENOSYS" section naming `IPC_SEND` / `IPC_RECV` (deferred with fd passing), `CAP_GRANT` (intentionally v2), `MOUNT` / `UMOUNT` (need fstype factory), `FS_WATCH` (needs event queue), `HOST_FILE_RECV` (needs drag-drop token plumbing), and pins CAP_GRANT as the canonical ext-range ENOSYS probe target per the existing `known_ext_opcode_without_handler_returns_enosys` test. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; no new Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes. ENOSYS probes unchanged.

After the **POSIX kill(pid, 0) existence probe slice**: workspace **1650 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1186 → 1191 +5, TS-unit 457 → 457, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only slice. Pre-slice, `PROC_KILL` with `signum == 0` rejected with EINVAL; post-slice, signum 0 is the POSIX existence + permission probe that runs every precondition `proc_kill` would run (target exists, not reaped, sender permitted via parent/self/ProcKillAny) but delivers no signal. Returns 0 on success, ESRCH if pid doesn't exist or has been reaped, ENOTCAPABLE on permission denied (PMos's capability-based POSIX-EPERM analogue). Unblocks the supervisor pattern "is this pid alive and could I signal it" without requiring userland to pick a surrogate signum whose side effect it then has to tolerate. Implementation: new `Kernel::proc_check_signal(sender, target)` helper in `crates/kernel/src/sys.rs` that shares the precondition surface with `Kernel::proc_kill` by design — a future slice that tightens cap rules for proc_kill should tighten both in lockstep. `handle_proc_kill` in `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs` routes signum 0 to `proc_check_signal` before the existing signum match. Accepted set moves from {2, 9, 13, 15, 17} to {0, 2, 9, 13, 15, 17}; unknown signums still reject EINVAL. 5 new Rust tests in `crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs` (on live child → OK with empty inbox; on self → OK; on nonexistent pid → ESRCH; on non-child without ProcKillAny → ENOTCAPABLE — proves permission check fires even without delivery; on reaped target → ESRCH). No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; one new Kernel public helper; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (signum accepts one more valid value). ENOSYS probes unchanged. TS side: no shim, no test changes — the new signum-0 arm is reachable from TS but not yet exercised through a real user binary.

After the **TS composition tests for PROC_KILL signum 0 slice**: workspace **1653 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 457 → 460 +3, Playwright 2 → 2). TS-only slice; commit 29b2daa. Direct follow-up to cab9dc5 — the signum-0 existence + permission probe arm landed by the prior slice was reachable from TS but only Rust isolation tests covered it. This slice closes that gap by dispatching three PROC_KILL signum-0 calls against the bundled `kernel.wasm` through `KernelWasmHost`, proving the path is live in the release build and not stripped by LTO. Tests added to the existing `describe("dispatch: PROC_KILL")` block in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`, placed between the self-SIGINT test and the close-brace so the signum-0 arm sits next to the other PROC_KILL coverage: (1) `"returns 0 for self-probe (POSIX kill(getpid(), 0))"` — sender == target with CAPSET_ALL, proves the self-permit path returns success without delivering a signal; (2) `"returns -ESRCH for an unknown pid with signum 0"` — target pid 9999, proves the existence check fires before the (no-op) delivery and surfaces ESRCH the same way a real signal would; (3) `"returns -ENOTCAPABLE when probing a non-child target without Cap::ProcKillAny"` — two CAPSET_ORDINARY_APP processes (DisplayClient only, no PROC_KILL_ANY), neither parent of the other, sender probes target with signum 0, proves the cap check fires even when no signal would be delivered. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; no Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim changes (the existing `proc_kill` shim already packs signum as u16 LE at args[4..6] which carries 0 losslessly); no kernel.wasm rebuild needed (cab9dc5 already shipped the signum-0 arm in the bundled release wasm). ENOSYS probes unchanged. The remaining single-slice runway candidate for the signum-0 arm is a `hello-kill-probe` user wasm binary (mirrors hello-sigchld's 378-byte shape) that calls PMos-ext `proc_kill(getpid(), 0)` and writes the return code to `/dev/console` — would close the end-to-end loop with a real `wasm32-wasip1` binary the same way 60165e7 did for SIGCHLD.

After the **hello-kill-probe user wasm binary slice**: workspace **1654 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 460 → 461 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 12a8b45. End-to-end proof of cab9dc5's POSIX kill(pid, 0) existence-probe arm through a real `wasm32-wasip1` user binary — no kernel / test-harness code touched. The companion to 60165e7's hello-sigchld: same shape (cdylib + a single `_start` that calls one PMos-ext syscall and writes the result to stdout), same xtask USERLAND wiring pattern, same composition-test pattern. New crate `crates/hello-kill-probe/` (331-byte cdylib — smaller than hello-sigchld's 378 because no fd_read poll loop) imports two WASI functions (`fd_write`, `proc_exit`) and one PMos-ext function (`proc_kill`). `_start` calls `proc_kill(9999, 0)` — signum 0 + a pid that's deliberately not allocated — packs the i32 LE return code plus a trailing newline into a 5-byte buffer, and writes it to fd 1 in one fd_write. The newline forces the line-buffered console to flush through `onConsoleWrite`. Exit codes 0/13/101 follow the hello-sigchld convention (success / fd_write failure / panic). Why pid 9999: a self-probe would need a `proc_self` shim (currently unimplemented in `pmos_ext` — only the kernel-side opcode handler exists at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs:99`); a parent-probe would need to know init's pid up front. Probing an unallocated pid only requires the (already-shipped) `proc_kill` shim and exercises the new arm meaningfully — pre-cab9dc5 returned `-EINVAL = -28`, post-cab9dc5 returns `-ESRCH = -71`. The distinct errno makes the test assertion sharp: a regression that reverted to pre-cab9dc5 behaviour would surface as a wrong byte sequence on console, not a silent pass. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-kill-probe` to members; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-kill-probe", "hello_kill_probe")` to the USERLAND_BINS list so `just build` copies the release wasm to `dist/assets/bin/`; manifest entry count 30 → 31. Composition test in `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts` (placed between hello-sigchld and the SIGCHLD-via-fd-3 end-to-end test so the kill-probe pair sits next to the other PROC_KILL-driven user-wasm coverage): init proc_spawns `/bin/hello-kill-probe` → child runs → `fd_write(1, [0xb9, 0xff, 0xff, 0xff, 0x0a])` reaches `onConsoleWrite` → test decodes the first 4 bytes as i32 LE and asserts `-ERRNO.ESRCH` (-71); asserts the 5th byte is `'\n'`; asserts the child's exit code is 0. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method, KernelError, or Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim changes (the existing `proc_kill` shim packs signum as u16 LE at args[4..6] which carries 0 losslessly; its docstring still says "Any other signum returns -EINVAL" which is now stale post-cab9dc5 + post-91c618f — a future docstring-cleanup slice can refresh both this and the analogous PROC_RAISE shim that omits SIGPIPE/SIGCHLD/0 from the accepted list). ENOSYS probes unchanged. The signum-0 surface is now end-to-end proven from kernel isolation (5 Rust tests in cab9dc5) → real-kernel.wasm dispatch (3 TS tests in 29b2daa) → real wasm32-wasip1 user binary (this slice). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) shim-docstring refresh slice (TS-only, sync `proc_kill` + `proc_raise` shim docstrings to the post-trilogy accepted set {0, 2, 9, 13, 15, 17}); (b) `proc_self` PMos-ext shim slice (one-line shim wrapping the existing PROC_SELF=0x1103 opcode handler, unblocks self-probe wasm tests + future supervisor binaries that need their own pid); (c) hello-kill-probe self-probe variant once `proc_self` lands.

After the **proc_self pmos_ext shim + hello-pid user wasm slice**: workspace **1655 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 461 → 462 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit c7d5c9b. The kernel-side `handle_proc_self` opcode handler at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs:99` has existed since the PROC_SELF opcode (0x1103) was first declared, but no PMos-ext shim wrapped it for user wasm — every wasm binary that wanted its own pid had to dispatch the raw opcode by hand. This slice adds the one-line shim and proves it through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary. Shim addition in `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (placed immediately before `proc_kill` so the process-introspection shims `proc_caps_get` / `proc_self` / `proc_kill` sit together): `proc_self(): i32` — no args, returns the caller's pid as a positive i32, the cheapest possible PMos-ext syscall (every piece of information the kernel needs is already in the dispatcher frame, and the kernel's `handle_proc_self` always returns Ok). Defensive negative-errno return path included for forward-compatibility with any future error mode the kernel might add. POSIX `getpid(2)` analogue. New crate `crates/hello-pid/` (small cdylib in the same shape as hello-kill-probe and hello-sigchld) imports two WASI functions (fd_write, proc_exit) and one PMos-ext function (proc_self). `_start` calls proc_self(), packs the returned i32 LE pid plus a trailing newline into a 5-byte buffer, and writes it to fd 1 in one fd_write. Exit codes 0/13/101 follow the now-established hello-* pattern (success / fd_write failure / panic). Composition test asserts the decoded i32 LE matches the captured childPid (via `onSpawnProcess`), the 5th byte is `'\n'`, and exit code is 0. ENOSYS probes unchanged. After the **hello-raise user wasm slice**: workspace **1659 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 465 → 466 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit d509364. End-to-end proof of the WASI proc_raise path (560d7af / cbe8959's self-signal arm) through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary — sister to 60165e7's hello-sigchld. Origin difference: hello-sigchld observes SIGTERM queued by its parent's PROC_KILL; hello-raise observes SIGTERM queued by its own proc_raise call. The fd 3 drain path is identical in both cases — the asymmetry is purely in how the signal lands in the inbox. Because proc_raise is synchronous (the signal is queued on the caller's inbox before the shim returns), no EAGAIN polling is required: the fd_read that follows always finds the pending signal on the first dispatch. New crate `crates/hello-raise/` (small cdylib) imports four WASI functions (fd_read, fd_write, proc_exit, proc_raise). Unlike earlier hello-* binaries, this one uses only the `wasi_snapshot_preview1` namespace — proc_raise is a WASI opcode (0x0061), not a PMos-ext syscall, so no pmos_ext import. `_start` self-raises SIGTERM, drains the resulting 2-byte record from fd 3 (auto-installed SignalChannel per 9fbe708), echoes the bytes plus a trailing newline to fd 1, exits 0. Exit codes 0/10/11/12/13/101 (success / proc_raise errno / fd_read errno / unexpected zero read / fd_write errno / panic). After the **hello-wait-noop user wasm slice**: workspace **1664 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 470 → 471 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit eddec6a. End-to-end proof of the proc_wait pmos_ext shim's error path (98a3341 ext opcode wiring) through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary. Until this slice, proc_wait had kernel-isolation tests plus a TS shim, but no user wasm exercising it through the bundled kernel.wasm. This binary closes that gap as the smallest possible end-to-end exercise of the proc_wait syscall (PROC_WAIT = 0x1101). The child has no children of its own — it's a leaf process — so proc_wait(-1) hits the kernel's `WaitOutcome::NoChildren -> ECHILD` arm at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs:563`. The binary writes the 4-byte i32 LE -9 plus a trailing newline = 5 bytes. ECHILD = 9, so -ECHILD = -9, distinct from EAGAIN (-6), EINVAL (-28), ESRCH (-71) — the byte sequence is sharp. New crate `crates/hello-wait-noop/` (small cdylib in the hello-* family shape) imports two WASI functions (fd_write, proc_exit) and one PMos-ext function (proc_wait). `_start` calls `proc_wait(-1, 0, 0)` — target_pid -1 = WaitTarget::Any, options 0 (no WNOHANG), status_out_ptr 0 (no buffer needed on the failure path) — packs the i32 LE return code plus a trailing newline into a 5-byte buffer, writes it to fd 1. Exit codes 0/13/101. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-wait-noop`; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-wait-noop", "hello_wait_noop")`; manifest entry count 36 → 37. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method, KernelError, or Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. ENOSYS probes unchanged. The proc-management quartet (proc_spawn / proc_self / proc_parent / proc_wait) now has full end-to-end real-wasm coverage: hello-wasi-spawner (proc_spawn), hello-pid (proc_self), hello-ppid (proc_parent), hello-wait-noop (proc_wait). The proc_wait SUCCESS arm — actually reaping a zombie child from a real wasm — remains unaddressed because v1's sequential drain in runAllSpawns means a supervisor binary can't observe its own spawned child's exit (the parent must return before runAllSpawns pops the child; once the child runs, the parent is gone). A future composition test could stage a synthetic zombie via `kernel.dispatch(supervisor, PROC_SPAWN(child))` followed by `runAllSpawns` to zombify the child, then re-enter the supervisor through a separate `runtime.run()` call — but that requires a re-entry seam that doesn't exist yet. Defer until needed. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) shim-docstring refresh for `proc_kill` + `proc_raise` (still say "Any other signum returns -EINVAL" / "v1 knows three" — accepted set is now {0, 2, 9, 13, 15, 17}); (b) hello-clock-gettime no_std variant — the existing hello-clock uses Rust std's `Instant::now()`, but a no_std binary calling `clock_time_get(CLOCK_MONOTONIC)` directly would prove the bare-WASI clock surface; (c) cap_check / cap_list user wasm proofs — both shims exist on the kernel-handler side but neither has a user-wasm exerciser; (d) hello-fd-prestat-probe — calls `fd_prestat_get(0..5)` and writes the per-fd EBADF/OK record, proves the WASI preopen-loop termination semantics through real wasm.

After the **cap_check pmos_ext shim + hello-cap-check user wasm slice**: workspace **1665 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 471 → 472 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit a18cbb1. The kernel-side `handle_cap_check` opcode handler at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/ext.rs:133` has existed since the CAP_CHECK opcode (0x1300) was first declared, but no PMos-ext shim wrapped it for user wasm — which was awkward because the existing proc_caps_get shim docstring already referenced cap_check ("the shim requires an output buffer; a caller that doesn't want the value should use CAP_CHECK instead") even though no shim existed for callers to actually use. This slice closes the gap. Shim addition in `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (placed at the head of the introspection group so the cap-related shims sit together in the order {cap_check, proc_caps_get, proc_self, proc_parent, proc_kill}): `cap_check(cap_id: i32): i32` — per-cap yes/no probe. Returns 1 if the caller holds the requested cap, 0 if not, -EINVAL if cap_id doesn't correspond to a known Cap discriminant (guards userland against typos and forward-compatibility probing), -ESRCH if the caller's pid has been removed from the process table (defensive). Companion to cap_list / proc_caps_get's bitset-returning shims for callers that only care about one specific cap and don't want to materialise the full bitset. New crate `crates/hello-cap-check/` (small cdylib in the hello-* family shape) imports two WASI functions (fd_write, proc_exit) and one PMos-ext function (cap_check). `_start` calls `cap_check(CAP_SHELL = 3)`, packs the i32 LE return value plus a trailing newline into a 5-byte buffer, and writes it to fd 1 in one fd_write. Composition test spawns with CAPSET_ALL, so the answer is always 1. Bytes `[0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x0a]`. Exit codes 0/13/101. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-cap-check`; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-cap-check", "hello_cap_check")`; manifest entry count 37 → 38. `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt by `just build`.
