After the **WASI random_get + sched_yield shims + hello-random user wasm slice**: workspace **1666 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 472 → 473 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 5dd1714. The kernel-side `handle_random_get` + `handle_sched_yield` opcode handlers at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs:688 + 719` have existed since the WASI surface was first wired, but no JS shims provided the matching imports in `wasi_snapshot_preview1`. Any wasm binary importing `random_get` or `sched_yield` would have failed at instantiation with "missing import." This slice closes both gaps and proves random_get end-to-end through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary. **The gap was acute** because Rust std's HashMap initialization calls random_get to seed the SipHash key — any future std binary that touches HashMap would have crashed at startup. hello-std doesn't use HashMap so this latent bug never fired, but a typical std binary would have hit it on the first allocation. Two new shims in `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (placed at the tail of the wasi_snapshot_preview1 namespace right before the closing brace): `random_get(buf_ptr: i32, buf_len: i32): errno` — fills buf_len bytes at buf_ptr in user memory with random bytes from `Platform::random_bytes` (the JS host's `crypto.getRandomValues` in production, a deterministic PRNG in tests); mirrors the input-output heap pattern of fd_filestat_get; short-circuits buf_len == 0 to 0 without dispatching. `sched_yield(): errno` — cooperative-scheduling hint; PMos's current scheduler is a single-threaded round-robin that runs each dispatch to completion, so yield has no behavioural effect; the shim exists because Rust std's WASI libc calls sched_yield in lock busy-wait loops and would panic on -ENOSYS. New crate `crates/hello-random/` (small cdylib in the hello-* family shape) imports three WASI functions (fd_write, proc_exit, random_get). `_start` calls `random_get(buf, 8)` twice into adjacent stack buffers, exits 12 if the two reads produced identical bytes (1 in 2^64 chance — a strong sanity check that catches stuck-zero / stuck-constant shims), then writes both 8-byte records plus a trailing newline = 17 bytes to fd 1. Exit codes 0/10/11/12/13/101. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-random`; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-random", "hello_random")`; manifest entry count 38 → 39. `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt by `just build`. Composition test asserts 17 bytes total, 17th byte is `'\n'`, and the two 8-byte halves differ (catches a no-op shim that returns zeros and a stuck-value shim that returns identical buffers); asserts exit code 0. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method, KernelError, or Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. ENOSYS probes unchanged. **Latent bug closed**: any future std binary that uses HashMap (most non-trivial std binaries) will now bootstrap cleanly. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) hello-yield-loop binary that calls sched_yield() N times and asserts each returns 0 — proves the sched_yield shim is reachable through real wasm (not just declared); (b) hello-cap-check-bad-id binary — cap_check(99) → -EINVAL, proves the EINVAL arm of a18cbb1's cap_check shim through real wasm; (c) hello-fd-prestat-probe — calls `fd_prestat_get(0..5)` and writes the per-fd EBADF/OK record, proves the WASI preopen-loop termination semantics through real wasm; (d) kernel-wasm-host TS test for RANDOM_GET (asserts the dispatcher path is reachable + fills the heap with non-constant bytes) — there's currently no TS test at the dispatcher level for this opcode, only the new user-wasm test.

After the **WASI fd_close shim + hello-fd-close-bad user wasm slice**: workspace **1667 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 473 → 474 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit f03cf74. The kernel-side `handle_fd_close` opcode handler at `crates/kernel/src/syscall/wasi.rs:433` has existed since the WASI surface was first wired, but no JS shim provided the matching fd_close import in `wasi_snapshot_preview1`. Any wasm binary importing fd_close would have failed at instantiation with "missing import." This slice closes that gap and proves the close-bad-fd path end-to-end through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary. PMos's kernel auto-closes every fd at process exit, so existing one-shot user wasms (hello-wasi-min, hello-pid, hello-sigchld, etc.) leak nothing — but long-running processes that path_open + fd_close many files (display server, term, future shell with file-tab management) need this shim to recycle fds instead of accumulating them. Shim addition in `web/src/user-wasm-runtime.ts` (placed immediately before random_get so the WASI tail-end shims sit together): `fd_close(fd: i32): errno` — releases the FdEntry at `fd`, decrementing any underlying pipe / socket reference and freeing the fd-table slot; returns 0 on success, positive errno on failure (EBADF if `fd` isn't open). Mirrors the WASI errno return convention (positive on failure) — distinct from PMos-ext shims which return negative errno. New crate `crates/hello-fd-close-bad/` (small cdylib in the hello-* family shape) imports three WASI functions (fd_close, fd_write, proc_exit). `_start` calls `fd_close(99)` on a deliberately unopened fd (PMos fd tables for spawned children start with 0/1/2 = console + 3 = SignalChannel; fd 99 is well beyond any allocation), packs the i32 LE return code (always 8 = EBADF in this case) plus a trailing newline into a 5-byte buffer, writes it to fd 1. Exit codes 0/13/101. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-fd-close-bad`; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-fd-close-bad", "hello_fd_close_bad")`; manifest entry count 39 → 40. `dist/assets/user-worker.js` rebuilt by `just build`. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) `cap_list` shim + hello-cap-list binary — closes the last "kernel handler with no shim" gap; (b) hello-fd-close-good binary — opens a file via path_open then closes it cleanly via fd_close, proves the success arm of the new shim; (c) hello-yield-loop binary that calls sched_yield() N times and asserts each returns 0 — proves the sched_yield shim from 5dd1714 is reachable through real wasm.

After the **hello-fd-close-good user wasm slice**: workspace **1685 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 491 → 492 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit aa1e38f. Companion to f03cf74's hello-fd-close-bad (EBADF arm). Closes the success-arm coverage gap for the fd_close shim through a real wasm32-wasip1 binary. The binary closes fd 2 (auto-installed `/dev/console` stderr), then immediately re-closes fd 2 to verify the slot was actually freed (a broken shim that returned 0 on every close would silently pass the first close but the re-close would also return 0 — the binary exits 14 on that invariant violation, surfacing as a non-zero exit code observable in the test). After the close + re-check the binary writes the FIRST close's rc (0) plus a newline to fd 1 (still open). The slot-released re-check is the load-bearing assertion: it proves the kernel's fd_close actually freed the table slot, not just no-op'd. Without it a regression that left fds half-released would have shown the binary as "passing" on the first close while leaking fd table slots in long-running processes. New crate `crates/hello-fd-close-good/` (small cdylib in the hello-* family shape) imports three WASI functions (fd_close, fd_write, proc_exit). Exit codes 0/13/14/101 (success / fd_write failure / re-close didn't EBADF / panic). Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds `crates/hello-fd-close-good`; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds `("hello-fd-close-good", "hello_fd_close_good")`; manifest entry count 41 → 42. Composition test asserts the decoded i32 LE = 0, the 5th byte is `'\n'`, and exit code is 0 (proves the slot-released invariant held). No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method, KernelError, or Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed.
