Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) hello-yield-loop binary that calls sched_yield() N times — proves 5dd1714's sched_yield shim through real wasm; the only sub-trio shim still missing a user-wasm proof (random_get + fd_close are both proven now); (b) long-running display-server accept loop (still the largest unaddressed runway item, 2+ sessions); (c) Rust kernel `proc_check_signal` symmetry slice — sync proc_kill's cap-check rules with proc_check_signal so a future tightening of one tightens both in lockstep (mentioned as a comment in cab9dc5 but no enforcement test).

After the **hello-yield-loop user wasm slice**: workspace **1686 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1191, TS-unit 492 → 493 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 9ef143a. Final user-wasm proof for this session's WASI shim trio (random_get + fd_close + sched_yield). The 5dd1714 sched_yield shim was previously proven only at the kernel-isolation layer (Rust tests) and the dispatcher layer (1cc4211); this slice adds the user-wasm proof. The binary calls `sched_yield()` four times in a loop, asserts each return value is 0, and writes the iteration count (always 4 — the binary exits 12 on any earlier yield failure) as an i32 LE plus a trailing newline. PMos's scheduler is a single-threaded round-robin so yield has no behavioural effect; the load-bearing assertion is purely "the shim is callable and returns 0", but the loop iterates four times so a regression that broke the shim on the second call would still be observable. New crate `crates/hello-yield-loop/` imports three WASI functions (fd_write, proc_exit, sched_yield). Exit codes 0/12/13/101. Workspace support: `Cargo.toml` adds the crate; `crates/xtask/src/assemble_dist.rs` adds the USERLAND_BINS entry; manifest entry count 42 → 43. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method, KernelError, or Cap variant additions; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. ENOSYS probes unchanged. **Three-layer coverage milestone closed.** Every kernel-side syscall opcode now has all three layers green: (1) kernel-isolation Rust tests, (2) TS dispatcher tests through KernelWasmHost, (3) real wasm32-wasip1 user binary exercising the production shim path. The "kernel handler with no user-wasm proof" gap survey is now empty for the opcodes covered by hello-* binaries this session. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) long-running display-server accept loop (still the largest unaddressed runway item, 2+ sessions); (b) Rust kernel `proc_check_signal` symmetry slice — sync proc_kill's cap-check rules with proc_check_signal so a future tightening of one tightens both in lockstep (mentioned as a comment in cab9dc5 but no enforcement test); (c) MockKernel replacement for live-terminal (3+ sessions); (d) term::Session → Canvas migration (3+ sessions — Known Deviation #4). The runway is now dominated by multi-session architectural work; the single-slice cadence from the past ~20 slices has exhausted the natural shim/dispatcher/user-wasm coverage surface.

After the **proc_kill <-> proc_check_signal cap-matrix symmetry test slice**: workspace **1687 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1191 → 1192 +1, TS-unit 493 → 493, Playwright 2 → 2). Rust-only slice; commit 211584c. cab9dc5's commit message stated: "Shares the precondition surface with proc_kill by design — a future slice that tightens cap rules for proc_kill should tighten both in lockstep." This invariant was previously enforced only by code review (the dispatcher routes signum 0 to proc_check_signal so they share the body for signum 0, but a hypothetical future cap check added inside `Kernel::proc_kill` itself for non-zero signums would not propagate to proc_check_signal — and there was no test asserting they agree). This slice pins the contract with a property-style test (`crates/kernel/tests/syscall.rs`, +1) that enumerates a representative set of (sender, target, expected_outcome) tuples covering every leg of the cap-check matrix: (init, child, Ok) parent; (init, init, Ok) self; (sib_a, sib_b, NotCapable) cross-sibling; (init, sib_a, Ok) parent w/ cap; (init, bogus, NoSuchPid) nonexistent; (init, reaped, NoSuchPid) Dead state. For each tuple the test asserts both `Kernel::proc_check_signal(sender, target)` AND the dispatcher `PROC_KILL(sender, target, signum=15)` produce the same classification. The proc_kill arm uses signum 15 (SIGTERM, catchable) rather than signum 0 because signum 0 simply forwards to proc_check_signal — testing them together would be tautological. Signum 15 exercises the proc_kill body's own cap-check path and the comparison against proc_check_signal's body proves they share a surface. Side-effect handling: proc_kill on the Ok arms queues SIGTERM on the target's inbox; the test drains the inbox after each iteration so subsequent cases that revisit the same target start clean. A regression that adds a new cap rule to one function but not the other (e.g. requiring CAP_PROC_INSPECT for some case in proc_kill but forgetting to mirror it in proc_check_signal) would surface as a mismatch on the relevant case and fail this test. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; no Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no TS shim or runtime changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. ENOSYS probes unchanged. The cap-matrix contract from cab9dc5 is now machine-enforced rather than code-review-enforced — the first non-coverage slice in this session, and a fitting close-out for the single-slice cadence batch since the runway is now dominated by multi-session architectural work. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) long-running display-server accept loop (still the largest unaddressed runway item, 2+ sessions); (b) MockKernel replacement for live-terminal (3+ sessions); (c) term::Session → Canvas migration (3+ sessions — Known Deviation #4). The single-slice batch from this session has run from 1650 → 1687 passing (+37 tests, 19 slices, 38 commits) and closed three major coverage milestones: shim completeness, three-layer dispatcher coverage, kernel cap-matrix symmetry.

After the **display-server multi-client outer accept loop slice**: workspace **1687 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1192 → 1192, TS-unit 493 → 493, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit b1449da. First slice in the four-slice "long-running display-server accept loop" arc (see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-display-server-multi-client-accept-loop-design.md` + `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-04-19-display-server-multi-client-accept-loop.md`). Reshapes `display-server` from a single-shot binary into a bounded outer accept loop (`MAX_CLIENTS = 4`). Each iteration accepts a client via the existing EAGAIN-polled `ipc_accept`, reads the 16-byte RGBA payload, relays it to `/dev/fb0`, calls `fd_close` on the client-side server fd (WASI shim pre-landed in f03cf74), and prints `display-server served client {i}`. Accept-poll exhaustion after at least one served client drains cleanly (exit 0); exhaustion on iteration 0 keeps the existing exit-17 semantic for the sequential vitest `runAllSpawns` harness. `path_open("/dev/fb0")` lifts out of the per-client body — opened once at startup, reused for every served client. New exit code 19 covers `fd_close` returning a non-zero errno. The `MAX_CLIENTS = 4` ceiling is a testing fiction (vitest composition helper is strictly sequential and would otherwise spin forever when no more clients arrive); slice 2 of the arc (kernel `ipc_accept` blocking + signal-driven exit via fd 3) removes it. Init fires a second `/bin/display-client-demo` via one additional `proc_spawn` so the outer loop has two concurrent siblings to serve under Playwright. Three-layer test coverage maintained via targeted updates (not additions): kernel isolation unchanged (the multi-accept-on-single-listener contract is already pinned by `crates/kernel/tests/sys.rs:1385 multiple_clients_accept_into_distinct_server_side_fds`); the `init (std) spawns hello-std AND display-server AND display-client-demo` vitest test at `web/tests/unit/user-wasm-runtime.test.ts:2118` now pins `history.length === 5`, `lines.length === 10`, and both display-client-demo exits at 10 under sequential `runAllSpawns`; the `real-kernel.spec.ts` Playwright test pins two `display-server served client {i}` lines (server-iteration indices 0 and 1), two `display-client-demo sent pixels` lines, two `init spawned display-client-demo pid=…` DOM lines, and peak live workers ≥ 5. No new Rust native tests, no new vitest tests, no new Playwright tests — the existing tests' assertions strengthen. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; no Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. ENOSYS probes unchanged. **Architectural milestone**: the single-shot display-server shape that's been in place since display-bind landed is now a multi-client outer loop — the precondition for every remaining slice in the runway item. First slice in the workspace to land per the superpowers brainstorming + writing-plans + executing-plans flow; the committed design spec and implementation plan under `docs/superpowers/` predate this commit on the same branch. Next single-slice runway candidates: (slice 2 of the arc) kernel `ipc_accept` blocking semantics — replace the `-EAGAIN` response with a per-socket wait queue on the listener and teach the scheduler to park the caller and wake on connect, removing the `MAX_POLLS` busy-poll from display-server's accept call; (slice 3) signal-driven server exit — poll fd 3 (SignalChannel) between accepts, exit cleanly on SIGTERM, remove the `MAX_CLIENTS` ceiling; (slice 4) init `proc_wait` supervision loop — reap children and respawn display-server on crash; (fallback) any of the remaining pure-coverage gaps that surface during slice-2 prep.

After the **ipc_accept blocking kernel park/wake slice (2a)**: workspace **1694 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1192 → 1197 +5, TS-unit 493 → 495 +2, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 516df81. Second slice in the long-running display-server accept-loop arc (see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-ipc-accept-blocking-slice-2a-design.md`). Closes the spec-vs-impl drift at `specs/001-browser-os-v1/contracts/syscalls.md:203-210` — the `flags: u16` parameter has been in the ABI spec since v1 but never honoured by the implementation — and implements the park-and-wake follow-up explicitly deferred by `multi-process-plan.md §5` ("Real park-and-wake on fd readiness is a follow-up"). `flags=0` now blocks the caller on an empty listener backlog; a peer's `ipc_connect` completes the parked accept inline (mints server-side fd in the acceptor's fd table, appends the response to `Kernel.pending_wakes`, transitions the acceptor Ready). `flags & accept_flags::NONBLOCK` preserves the historical `-EAGAIN` semantic. New `ServiceOutcome::{Done, Parked}` return type on `dispatch()` lets the handler signal "don't push a response" without out-of-band state; the `crates/kernel/src/syscall/mod.rs` test-compat wrapper unwraps `Done` back into a `Response` for the ~340 existing Rust dispatcher-test call sites that haven't been migrated to the typed outcome. New kernel WASM export `kernel_take_next_wake_for_pid(pid)` writes one queued Response into `RESP_SCRATCH` and returns 1 (vs 0 if nothing queued) — `kernel_dispatch` also gains the rc=1-means-parked signal. JS `KernelWasmHost.dispatch` returns `{ response?, heapOut, parked? }`; `drainWakesForPid(pid, sab)` loops the take export and pushes each response onto the pid's SAB response ring via the same `encodeResponse` + `RES_HEAD`-bump path `serviceSab` uses for the Done case; `startDispatchLoop` calls it before `serviceSab` on each round-robin pass. `serviceSab` short-circuits the response-push on parked. Listener `close_socket` drains any parked acceptor with `-EBADF`; parker `proc_exit` clears the listener's `parked_acceptor` slot via a linear scan over `IpcTable::sockets` (`Kernel::proc_exit` → `IpcTable::clear_parked_acceptor_for_pid`). `drain_closed_object_side_effects` (exposed for tests that bypass `release_object` via `FdTable::close`) reconciles orphan sockets against live fd tables and runs the same wake side-effects. One-parker-per-listener invariant in v1: a second blocking accept while one is parked returns `-EAGAIN` regardless of flags, documented as a deferred POSIX-divergence lift — v1 display-server needs only one acceptor at a time. **Zero userland diff in 2a**: `encodeRequest` zero-fills the unused args window, so the existing display-server shim (args[0..4] = listener_fd, rest zero) resolves to `flags=0` = blocking on the first call. display-server's `MAX_POLLS` inner busy-poll still runs but succeeds on iteration 0 — dead code that slice 2b deletes cleanly. display-server now parks indefinitely on iteration 2 of its outer loop (kernel blocks on the empty listener backlog with no 3rd client under Playwright's two-client setup) — the long-running-server shape the arc is building toward; slice 2b's signal-driven exit will restore clean shutdown. `dist/assets/kernel.wasm` rebuilt, `dist/assets/kernel-worker.js` rebundled (esbuild picked up the new JS-side exports). No user-wasm rebuild needed. Playwright's terminal observable signal shifted from `"display-server fb blit ok"` (no longer prints — blocks before reaching the println!) to `"display-server served client 1"` (second served-client line, implies every earlier line landed); the DOM and console-ordering assertions updated accordingly. The `user-wasm-runtime.test.ts:2118` `init (std) spawns hello-std AND display-server AND display-client-demo` test now pins display-server exit 12 (not 17): the sequential `runAllSpawns` harness leaves spawned pids in Ready state (it doesn't call `markRunning` the way production `kernel-worker-entry` does), so `park_on_accept`'s `Ready → BlockedOnIpc` transition fails with `KernelError::NoSuchPid` → shim surfaces `-ESRCH` → display-server's "rc != 0 && rc != -EAGAIN" arm hits exit 12. Production (Playwright) marks spawned pids Running, blocking accept parks cleanly, peer's connect wakes it. Constitution check PASS on all ten principles; Principle V **strengthens** (parked Worker is genuinely idle vs busy-polling — isolation is load-bearing across the SAB wake boundary), Principle IX **improves** (10000-iteration busy-poll replaced by 0 CPU in the parked Worker + microsecond round-robin wake latency). Deviation introduced: one-parker-per-listener invariant. Deviation closed: `contracts/syscalls.md:203-210`'s `flags` parameter. **Architectural milestone**: the kernel now has its first real blocking syscall. Every prior "blocking syscall" case (fd_read on empty pipe, proc_wait on running child) returned `-EAGAIN` and expected userland to busy-poll; slice 2a establishes the park/wake + deferred-response pattern (Socket.parked_acceptor slot + Kernel.pending_wakes queue + ServiceOutcome::Parked + kernel_take_next_wake_for_pid export + drainWakesForPid JS wiring) that future blocking-syscall work extends. `BlockedOnIpc` state transitions at `crates/kernel/src/proc/mod.rs:66` — predicted by `data-model.md:48-82` since v1 — are load-bearing for the first time. Next single-slice runway candidates: (slice 2b of the arc) display-server migration — drop `MAX_POLLS` inner busy-poll and the `MAX_CLIENTS = 4` outer ceiling; add signal-driven exit via fd 3 (SignalChannel) between accepts, which requires a SIGTERM-interrupts-parked-accept semantic (kernel wakes the parked acceptor with `-EINTR` + Ready transition when `proc_kill(SIGTERM)` targets a blocked-on-ipc pid); restores the Playwright `fb blit ok` terminal assertion; (slice 2c) init `proc_wait` supervision loop; (fallback) parked `fd_read` on a pipe, applying the same pattern to `Pipe::waiting_readers` / `waiting_writers` already-existing parker Vecs in `crates/kernel/src/ipc/pipe.rs`; (fallback) runAllSpawns harness calls `markRunning` on spawned children so vitest can exercise the full parked-accept+wake path without Playwright (would remove the exit-12-on-ESRCH divergence between vitest and Playwright).

After the **ipc_accept blocking slice 2b kernel EINTR + display-server MAX_POLLS drop slice**: workspace **1698 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1197 → 1200 +3, TS-unit 495 → 496 +1, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 774b6da. Narrowed second sub-slice in the "long-running display-server accept loop" arc (see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-19-ipc-accept-blocking-slice-2b-design.md`). Kernel lands the SIGTERM-interrupts-parked-accept semantic in full — `IpcTable::take_parked_acceptor_for_pid` (mirror of 2a's clear_parked_acceptor, returning req_id for the wake response), `Kernel::interrupt_parked_accept` (composes take + pending_wakes.push + Ready transition + block_reason clear), `handle_proc_kill` Signal::Term branch invoking the new method after inbox delivery, and a surgical SIGKILL-arm clear_parked_acceptor_for_pid call in `cleanup_proc` (the SIGKILL path bypassed proc_exit and left orphan parker slots — now pinned by a new kernel test). Display-server's `ipc_accept` inner busy-poll (`MAX_POLLS = 10_000`) deletes: slice 2a made the call blocking, so every inner-loop iteration succeeded on pass 0 — dead code. Replaced by a single blocking call; any negative rc is fatal (exit 12), which preserves slice 1's "non-EAGAIN error" semantic for vitest `runAllSpawns` (where `park_on_accept`'s `Ready → BlockedOnIpc` transition fails with `KernelError::NoSuchPid` and the shim surfaces `-ESRCH`). Outer `MAX_CLIENTS = 4` loop and all other exit codes unchanged from slice 1 — signal-driven exit + unbounded outer loop deferred to slice 2c. Kernel tests (+3 in `crates/kernel/tests/sys.rs`): `sigterm_interrupts_parked_accept_with_eintr` (SIGTERM on a parked pid → pending_wakes gets `Response::err(req_id, EINTR)`, pid transitions Ready, listener's parked_acceptor clears, SignalInbox still holds Term), `signum_zero_probe_does_not_wake_parked_accept` (POSIX existence probe is no-op on park — pinned decision from design §3.1), `sigkill_on_parked_accept_exits_without_eintr_wake` (SIGKILL path synchronously exits without queuing EINTR, parker slot swept by the new cleanup_proc fix). TS dispatcher test (+1 in `web/tests/unit/kernel-wasm-host.test.ts`): `SIGTERM wakes parked acceptor with EINTR; take-wake delivers error response` — real kernel.wasm via KernelWasmHost mirrors kernel-isolation test 1 at the dispatch layer. No Platform / Filesystem / Vfs API / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal variant additions; no Kernel public-method signature changes beyond the new `interrupt_parked_accept`; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (slice 2a's `flags: u16` in args[4..6] is the v1 shape). Deviation from design: the prior attempt at bundling init's `proc_kill(ds_pid, 15)` + display-server signal-driven exit + Playwright `fb blit ok` restoration exposed a concurrent-Worker timing race — init's proc_kill fires before display-server's Worker starts `_start`, delivering SIGTERM to display-server's inbox before any client is served. display-server's pre-accept drain then exits 0 without serving any client, breaking Playwright. Fix requires init to wait for clients to exit before signalling — that's proc_wait, which is slice 2c scope. The init coordination + unbounded outer loop + signal-driven exit all re-scope to slice 2c. Constitution check PASS on all ten principles; Principle IX **improves** (10k-iteration busy-poll replaced by 0 CPU in the parked Worker), Principle V **strengthens** (parked Worker is genuinely idle vs busy-polling — isolation is load-bearing across the SAB wake boundary for the second time). Kernel.wasm rebuilt, user-worker.js rebundled. Next single-slice runway candidates: (slice 2c of the arc) init `proc_wait` supervision loop + delayed SIGTERM to display-server + display-server unbounded outer loop + signal-driven exit via fd 3 polling + Playwright `fb blit ok` restoration — restores every observable deferred from this narrower 2b slice; (fallback) parked `fd_read` on a pipe, applying the 2a park-and-wake pattern to `Pipe::waiting_readers` / `waiting_writers` already-existing parker Vecs in `crates/kernel/src/ipc/pipe.rs`; (fallback) runAllSpawns harness calls `markRunning` on spawned children so vitest can exercise the full parked-accept+wake path without the `-ESRCH`-to-exit-17 defensive branch.

After the **proc_wait blocking kernel park/wake slice (2c.1)**: workspace **1709 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1200 → 1208 +8, TS-unit 496 → 499 +3, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 76e54e3. Third slice in the kernel-park/wake arc (sibling of 2a/2b — see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-20-proc-wait-blocking-slice-2c1-design.md`). Closes the spec-vs-impl drift at `specs/001-browser-os-v1/contracts/syscalls.md` §3.2: `proc_wait`'s *"blocks until a matching child transitions to Zombie"* semantics now match the implementation (pre-2c.1 the kernel comment *"v1 always returns non-blocking — the caller retries"* was the documented drift). Composes slice 2a's `ServiceOutcome::{Done, Parked}` + `Kernel.pending_wakes` + `kernel_take_next_wake_for_pid` machinery against the second blockable kernel primitive. New `Kernel.parked_waiters: BTreeMap<Pid, WaitParker>` keyed by parent pid; `Kernel::park_on_wait` parks a parent on a blocking `proc_wait` when a live matching child exists but no zombie; `Kernel::wake_parked_waiter_for_child(child_pid, ppid, status)` helper fires the reap + wake sequence from both `Kernel::proc_exit` and `Kernel::proc_kill`'s `Signal::Kill` arm (covers voluntary + SIGKILL child-exit paths); `Kernel::interrupt_parked_wait(pid) -> bool` mirrors slice 2b's `interrupt_parked_accept` and is called alongside it in `handle_proc_kill`'s `Signal::Term` arm. v1 invariant: one parker per parent (second blocking `proc_wait` while parked returns `-EAGAIN` regardless of WNOHANG) — mirror of 2a's one-parker-per-listener. `WaitTarget::Any` matches every child; `Specific(pid)` matches only that child (a child exiting with a mismatched target becomes a regular zombie for a later non-blocking wait to reap). `pending_wakes` tuple shape extended from `(Pid, Response)` to `(Pid, Response, WakeHeap)` where `WakeHeap = Option<PendingHeap { heap_ptr, bytes }>`; every 2a/2b producer pushes `None`; 2c.1's parked-wait wake pushes `Some` with the 4-byte reaped-child-pid when the parker's `heap_len >= 4`. **Drainer extension** (open question §8 resolution): `kernel_take_next_wake_for_pid` now copies heap bytes into `HEAP_SCRATCH[0..extra_len]` + captures the user's SAB heap_ptr in a new `RESP_HEAP_PTR` slot readable via new `kernel_resp_heap_ptr` export; TS `drainWakesForPid` copies `response.extraLen` bytes from kernel HEAP_SCRATCH to the user SAB's heap scratch at the recorded heap_ptr. Synchronous-reap and parked-wake paths now produce responses with identical `(value = packed_status, extra_len = 4, heap[0..4] = child_pid)` shape — userland is agnostic to which path fired. `BlockedOnWait` + `BlockReason::Wait { pid }` (spec-blessed since ratification at `crates/kernel/src/proc/mod.rs:66`, unused until this slice) are now load-bearing. **Zero userland diff in 2c.1**: `hello-wait-noop`'s `proc_wait(-1, 0, 0)` still hits the `NoChildren → ECHILD` arm (the binary has no children); init stays fire-and-forget + single `proc_kill(display-server, SIGTERM)`; display-server unchanged; no other binary calls `proc_wait`. Slice 2c.2 is the next runway: init migrates to a blocking supervision loop (`loop { match proc_wait(Any, 0) { pid => println!("init reaped pid={}") } }`), display-server drops `MAX_CLIENTS = 4` entirely with an unbounded outer loop, Playwright restores the `"display-server fb blit ok"` observable + adds `"init reaped pid=<M>"` observables. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle V **strengthens** (parked Worker genuinely idle vs busy-poll — same pattern 2a/2b landed for ipc_accept, extended to proc_wait), Principle IX **improves** (neutral under 2c.1 because userland is unchanged; strictly when 2c.2 migrates, init's supervisor CPU drops from busy-poll-every-SIGCHLD to parked-until-child-exits, same magnitude as 2a's display-server CPU reduction). Deviation introduced: one-waiter-per-parent invariant. Deviation closed: `contracts/syscalls.md` §3.2's blocking semantics now match impl. **Architectural milestone**: every blockable-in-spec kernel primitive (ipc_accept + proc_wait) now has real park/wake. The remaining busy-polled primitives (`fd_read` on empty pipe/socket/signal-channel, `proc_check_signal` on empty inbox) can adopt the same pattern — add a parker slot on the underlying object, route wake-on-readiness through `pending_wakes`, reuse `kernel_take_next_wake_for_pid` + `drainWakesForPid`. Next single-slice runway candidates: (slice 2c.2) userland migration — init supervisor loop + display-server unbounded accept + `"fb blit ok"` restoration; (fallback) parked `fd_read` on pipe with `Pipe::waiting_readers` / `waiting_writers` — the Vec parker slots already exist at `crates/kernel/src/ipc/pipe.rs`, 2c.1's drainer-extra_len path covers the N-byte readback they'd need.

After the **T114 toolkit App::connect facade slice**: workspace **1712 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1208 → 1211 +3, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit ce27f0c. First slice against the Phase 2 Toolkit block (T114–T120): extracts the application-level `App<C: Connection>` facade at `crates/toolkit/src/app.rs` over the wire-protocol `Client` that has lived at `crates/toolkit/src/protocol.rs` since the toolkit-protocol-client deviation landed. `App::connect(connection)` drives the handshake every v1 app needs in a single call — `display.get_registry → drain registry.global events → registry.bind(pmd_compositor, pmd_shm, pmd_xdg_shell)` — so the window wrapper (T115), widgets (T116), layout (T117), and the draw-buffer commit loop (T119) can layer above `App` without re-walking the bind sequence. Public surface: `App::connect(connection) -> Result<Self, ClientError>`; `App::compositor() / shm() / xdg_shell() -> ObjectId` accessors; `App::dispatch() -> Result<Vec<ClientEventWithPayload>, ClientError>` runs one event-drain cycle (flush outbound, pull one `recv` chunk, parse events); `App::client() / client_mut() / into_client()` escape hatches for callers that need direct protocol access. Supporting additions in `protocol.rs`: `Connection` trait grows a default-empty `fn recv(&mut self) -> Vec<u8>` method (backwards-compatible — the outbound-only `MemoryConnection` used by `tests/{protocol,loopback}.rs` inherits the default; bidirectional test harnesses and the real `ipc::Pipe`-backed `display_connect` fd transport override); new `ClientError::MissingGlobal(&'static str)` variant surfaces a missing-required-global handshake failure while preserving the outer enum's `Copy` bound. Tests (`crates/toolkit/tests/app.rs`, +3): `app_connect_binds_required_globals` (mock pre-queues three `registry.global` events covering the required trio; `App::connect` succeeds; accessors return distinct non-zero ids bound to the right interfaces in the underlying `Client`'s object table); `app_connect_fails_when_compositor_missing` (mock omits compositor → `App::connect` returns `ClientError::MissingGlobal("pmd_compositor")`); `app_dispatch_delivers_events` (post-bootstrap, seed a synthetic `registry.global_remove(2)` through the app's own connection, dispatch returns it in a one-element vec, a second dispatch returns empty rather than blocking). Note on naming: spec §10 names the interface `pmd_xdg_wm_base`; the codebase renamed it to `pmd_xdg_shell` (see `crates/display-proto/src/objects.rs`), so the `App::xdg_shell()` accessor matches the in-code vocabulary and the doc comment calls out the rename. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the `App` facade only consumes `protocol.rs` + `display-proto` public API and could be swapped out without touching either; Principle VII (protocol over API) strengthens because the facade explicitly routes every bind through `Client::registry_bind` + `Client::get_registry`, i.e. speaks the wire protocol rather than calling back-channel helpers. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T115 `Window` wrapper over `pmd_xdg_toplevel` — `App::connect` produced the `xdg_shell` id this slice, T115 builds the `Window` struct that calls `xdg_shell.get_xdg_surface → xdg_surface.get_toplevel → toplevel.set_title` behind a title/size/close-callback API; (b) T119 draw-buffer + commit loop — consumes `App::shm` + `App::compositor` to allocate a SAB-backed `pmd_shm_pool`, create a `pmd_buffer`, attach to a surface, and commit on `frame` callback ticks; (c) the toolkit-free-client fixture (T113) could migrate onto `App::connect` instead of walking the bind sequence by hand, collapsing `crates/toolkit-free-client/src/lib.rs` from 276 lines to a ~30-line `App::connect` + follow-up wrapper (would be followed by renaming the crate and repointing the Principle VII conformance assertion at "the toolkit-free-client uses only `display-proto` + `toolkit::App`" — an intentional weakening from "never touches toolkit at all" since `App` itself is a facade over `display-proto`); (d) slice 2c.2's deferred init coordination work (none at the toolkit layer, but the kernel-side park/wake machinery is stable enough that the next blockable primitive — `fd_read` on pipe — can now be attacked).

After the **T095 init proc_wait supervision loop + T110 display-server signal-driven exit slice (2c.2)**: workspace **1709 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1208 → 1208, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit a13fca0. Closing slice in the "long-running display-server accept loop" arc (see T095 + T110 partial notes). Init migrates from fire-and-forget launcher to a blocking `proc_wait(-1, 0, &status)` supervision loop: four spawns (hello-std + display-server + 2× display-client-demo) → reap loop printing `init reaped child pid={N}` per hit → after both display-client-demo pids have been reaped, `proc_kill(ds_pid, 15)` + `init sent SIGTERM to display-server pid={ds_pid}` → remaining reaps (server + hello-std, in whatever order they Zombie) → `init exiting`. Graceful fallback for vitest `runAllSpawns` (spawned pids stay Ready so `park_on_wait` hits the Running→Blocked* illegal-transition path and returns `-ESRCH`): init prints `init proc_wait returned errno={N}; exiting with {remaining} children unreaped` + falls through to `init exiting`. Display-server drops `MAX_CLIENTS = 4` entirely — `'outer: loop` unbounded; each iteration starts with a non-blocking `fd_read` on fd 3 (auto-installed SignalChannel) draining up to four queued signals, SIGTERM (signum 15) → `break 'outer`; blocking `ipc_accept`'s `-EINTR` (slice 2b interrupt path) re-polls fd 3 + breaks on confirmed SIGTERM; any other negative rc is fatal (exit 12). After the outer loop breaks prints `display-server fb blit ok` + exits 0 — the Playwright observable restored. Three JS-side substrate fixes the arc depended on: (1) `kernel-wasm-host.ts` dispatch loop uses RES_HEAD delta across `drainWakesForPid + serviceSab` as the authoritative "response pushed?" signal so `Atomics.notify(USER_WAIT_SLOT)` fires only when something landed in the user's ring — parked syscalls no longer spuriously wake callers into empty rings, drained wakes (parent reaping child) DO fire notify when the request ring is otherwise empty; (2) `kernel-worker-entry.ts` `proc:sab` handler now calls `realKernel.markRunning(msg.pid)` after `pidMap.set` so spawned pids are Running by the time their blocking syscalls land (kernel's `park_on_wait` / `park_on_accept` require `Running → Blocked*`, fail from Ready with `NoSuchPid → -ESRCH`); (3) `user-wasm-runtime.ts` `_start`-returns-cleanly path now dispatches `PROC_EXIT(0)` via SabBackend so Rust `std` binaries that return normally from `main` (which DOES NOT emit `__wasi_proc_exit` in wasm32-wasip1 — std just returns from `_start`) still transition the kernel-side proc entry to Zombie; without this fix, parked parents' `proc_wait` blocked forever because no child ever transitioned to Zombie. Kernel `cargo test --workspace` unchanged (Rust 1208): no kernel code touched, just userland + JS glue. Vitest user-wasm-runtime composition test assertions updated for init's new output shape under sequential `runAllSpawns` (lines 10 → 11 for the new errno-annotated exit line; `display-server` exit 12 preserved; clients still exit 10 against torn-down listener). Playwright test strengthens with restored `display-server fb blit ok` + new `init sent SIGTERM to display-server pid=\d+` + `init reaped child pid=\d+` assertions, plus ordering-chain `client-sent → server-served → init-SIGTERM → fb-blit-ok` pinned. `kernel.wasm` untouched; `kernel-worker.js` + `user-worker.js` rebundled; `init.wasm` + `display-server.wasm` rebuilt. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle V **strengthens** (the three JS-side fixes remove a latent busy-loop where workers could wake into empty rings and a latent park-forever where a clean-exit child left its parent's proc_wait stranded — both were correctness-in-expectation gaps until the arc closed end-to-end under Playwright), Principle IX **strictly improves** (init's supervisor CPU drops from "would have busy-polled via proc_wait(WNOHANG)" to "parked until child exits" — materialises the 2c.1-promised reduction). Deviation introduced: none. Deviation closed: the spec-promised `display-server fb blit ok` trailing print under clean shutdown is observable again. **Architectural milestone**: first end-to-end demonstration of a long-running server in the workspace — display-server binds `/run/display`, serves every client that connects, exits cleanly on SIGTERM delivered by a supervisor. The four-piece choreography (init supervisor + server + two clients) exercises every blocking-syscall + wake path the v1 kernel has (park_on_accept, park_on_wait, SIGTERM delivery to BlockedOnIpc, wake_parked_waiter_for_child on child-Zombie transition). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) parked `fd_read` on pipe with `Pipe::waiting_readers` / `waiting_writers` — the Vec parker slots already exist at `crates/kernel/src/ipc/pipe.rs`; (b) init `/etc/init.conf` parsing + bundled-service spawn table — blocked on T074's variable-length manifest arm; (c) shell respawn policy; (d) `preferences.toml` dynamic env-var injection (T183/T184).

After the **T115 toolkit Window facade slice**: workspace **1716 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1211 → 1215 +4, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 5ce8ade. Second slice against the Phase 2 Toolkit block (T114–T120): layers a `Window<'a, C: Connection>` facade at `crates/toolkit/src/window.rs` on top of the T114 `App` facade. `Window::new(&mut app)` allocates the surface + xdg_toplevel object ids and sends the `compositor.create_surface` + `xdg_shell.get_toplevel` request sequence via the borrowed `App`'s `Client`; callers follow up with `set_title` / `set_app_id` (send + cache locally) and `commit` to trigger the server's first `configure` event. Public API: `Window::new / set_title / set_app_id / commit / dispatch / is_configured / configured_size / close_requested / surface / xdg_surface / xdg_toplevel / title / app_id / app / app_mut`. `dispatch` delegates to `App::dispatch` then post-processes each event: `xdg_toplevel::configure(serial, width, height)` updates `configured_size` + flips `is_configured=true` + emits `ack_configure(serial)` outbound (not passed through to caller); `xdg_toplevel::close` flips `close_requested=true` (not passed through); all other events pass through in the returned vec for the app-level logic that lands with frame callbacks + input events in future slices. **Protocol extensions** (minimal, required by the tests — `XDG_TOPLEVEL_EVENTS` was empty prior to this slice): `crates/display-proto/src/objects.rs` grows `XDG_TOPLEVEL_EVENTS` with `configure` (opcode 1) + `close` (opcode 2) and `XDG_TOPLEVEL_REQUESTS` with `ack_configure` (opcode 4); `events.rs` gains typed `XdgToplevelConfigure { serial, width, height }` (merges spec §11's `pmd_xdg_surface.configure(serial)` and §12's `pmd_xdg_toplevel.configure(w, h, states)` into one event because the v1 codebase collapses `pmd_xdg_surface` into `pmd_xdg_toplevel` — see `client.rs`'s single `xdg_shell.get_toplevel(new_id, surface)` that skips the spec's intermediate `get_xdg_surface`) and `XdgToplevelClose` (empty payload) decoders/encoders; `requests.rs` gains `XdgToplevelAckConfigure { serial }` decoder — spec §11's `xdg_surface.ack_configure` lands on xdg_toplevel in the collapsed shape. `crates/toolkit/src/protocol.rs` gains `Client::xdg_toplevel_ack_configure(toplevel_id, serial)` helper. `lib.rs` re-exports `Window`. The `Window::xdg_surface()` accessor aliases `Window::surface()` in v1 — no separate `get_xdg_surface` request is on the wire; the field is kept for spec-alignment and documented in the module doc. Test coverage (`crates/toolkit/tests/window.rs`, +4): `window_new_sends_create_surface_sequence` (mock; `Window::new` emits compositor.create_surface + xdg_shell.get_toplevel with the Window's allocated surface + toplevel ids; initial state `is_configured=false`, `configured_size=(0,0)`, `close_requested=false`); `window_set_title_sends_set_title_request` (`set_title("Hello")` emits xdg_toplevel.set_title + `title()` returns "Hello"); `window_dispatch_handles_configure` (mock injects configure(1, 800, 600); dispatch returns empty passthrough, `is_configured=true`, `configured_size=(800, 600)`, outbound carries ack_configure(1)); `window_dispatch_records_close` (mock injects close; dispatch returns empty passthrough, `close_requested=true`, no outbound reply, toplevel still in the client object table). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no wire-format changes on previously-shipped opcodes (only new `XDG_TOPLEVEL_{EVENTS,REQUESTS}` entries); no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `Window` is a facade over `App`+`protocol::Client`+`display-proto` and is independently replaceable; Principle VII (protocol over API) strengthens — every `Window` method routes through wire-protocol requests (create_surface, get_toplevel, set_title, set_app_id, ack_configure) rather than back-channel helpers, and the merged configure event decoder lives in `display-proto` so the server-side collapse is mirrored in the typed codec. Deviation introduced: `pmd_xdg_surface` is not a distinct interface — `pmd_xdg_toplevel`'s opcode tables + typed structs absorb both the §11 xdg_surface ops (configure event + ack_configure request) and the §12 xdg_toplevel ops (set_title, set_app_id, close event); this was already true for requests, extended to events in this slice. The deviation is documented in the `XDG_TOPLEVEL_*` tables in `display-proto/src/objects.rs`, in the `XdgToplevelConfigure` / `XdgToplevelAckConfigure` doc comments, and in the `Window` module-level doc. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T116 `Widget` trait + primitives (`Label`, `Button`, `TextInput`, `List`, `Container`) — `WindowFrame` (title bar chrome) is the scheduled first widget, consuming `Window::configured_size` + `toolkit::draw::Canvas` to paint chrome around the client area; (b) T119 SAB buffer + commit loop — consumes `App::shm` + `App::compositor` + `Window::surface` to allocate a `pmd_shm_pool`, create a `pmd_buffer`, attach + commit on `frame` callback ticks; (c) T113 toolkit-free-client migration onto `App::connect` + `Window` — would collapse `crates/toolkit-free-client/src/lib.rs` from 276 lines to ~60 while keeping the Principle VII conformance assertion intact; (d) undo the `pmd_xdg_surface` collapse — add the interface + requests/events + the intermediate `get_xdg_surface` step in display-server's `client.rs` + toolkit's `protocol.rs` + `Window::new`, splitting the merged `XdgToplevelConfigure` + `XdgToplevelAckConfigure` back into their spec-assigned `XdgSurface*` homes; would be a multi-slice arc and the v1-collapsed shape is adequate for every existing downstream so the split can wait until a v1.1 conformance pass.

After the **T119 toolkit BufferPool double-buffered shm + attach/commit slice**: workspace **1721 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1215 → 1220 +5, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 671590b. Third slice against the Phase 2 Toolkit block (T114–T120): layers a `BufferPool` double-buffered shm-pool facade at `crates/toolkit/src/draw/buffer.rs` on top of the T114 `App` + T115 `Window` facades. `BufferPool::new(&mut app, width, height)` sends `pmd_shm.create_pool(new_id=pool, size = 2 * w * h * 4)` once and then two `pmd_shm_pool.create_buffer(new_id=buf_n, offset, w, h, stride = w*4, format = ARGB8888)` requests at offsets `0` and `w * h * 4`, pre-allocating the two ping-pong buffers ready for paint. Public API: `new`, `width / height / stride / size / pool_id / buffer_id(idx) / back_index / is_in_use(idx) / buffer_offset(idx) / back_buffer` accessors; `acquire_back_canvas(&mut self) -> Option<Canvas<'_>>` hands out a borrowing Canvas view onto the back buffer (returns `None` when the buffer is awaiting `buffer.release` back-pressure — forward-compatible with a real frame loop); `commit_and_swap(&mut window)` sends `surface.attach(back_buffer, 0, 0)` + `surface.damage(0, 0, w, h)` + `surface.commit()` then flips `back_index` and marks the just-attached buffer in-use; `handle_release(buffer_id)` flips the per-buffer in-use flag when the server emits `pmd_buffer.release` (returns `false` for unrelated ids so the caller can route elsewhere); minimal frame-callback helpers `request_frame(&mut window)` / `handle_frame_done(callback_id)` / `pending_frames()` track outstanding `surface.frame(new_id)` calls without auto-driving paint (that's a future slice). **Canvas lifetime adaptation** (required because `acquire_back_canvas` returns a Canvas that borrows the pool's `pixels` vec): `Canvas` gains a lifetime parameter `Canvas<'a>` with internal `CanvasStorage` enum. `Canvas::new(w, h) -> Canvas<'static>` keeps the owning variant (backed by `CanvasStorage::Owned(Vec<u8>)`) so every existing call site — 18 draw tests, 10 widget_label tests, 2 integration-tests — compiles with inferred `'static` lifetime unchanged; new `Canvas::from_slice(&mut [u8], w, h) -> Canvas<'a>` returns a borrowing variant (backed by `CanvasStorage::Borrowed(&mut [u8])`) used by `BufferPool::acquire_back_canvas`. All existing draw primitives (`clear`, `set_pixel`, `fill_rect`, `stroke_rect`, `draw_text`, `pixel`, `pixels` / `pixels_mut`) move into `impl<'a> Canvas<'a>`; `into_pixels` stays in `impl Canvas<'static>` since only the owning variant has a `Vec<u8>` to give up — the borrowed variant would panic with `unreachable!` on that path but is statically prevented from reaching it by the `Canvas<'static>` impl-block constraint. **Deviations from the slice brief**: (a) `commit_and_swap` and `request_frame` take `&mut Window<'_, C>` instead of the brief's `(&mut App, &Window)` pair — Window already owns a `&mut App`, so the two-arg shape is unborrowable by the caller (double mutable borrow); a single `&mut Window` is the unique clean shape, and the pool calls `window.app_mut().client_mut()` internally; (b) the SAB fd is not transmitted — `pmd_shm.create_pool` carries only the size, consistent with the T100 note in `protocol.rs` that the native-host test path trusts the size and a future slice wires the aux-fd channel; (c) frame-callback helpers don't bind a `pmd_callback` interface (there's no `Interface::Callback` variant in `display-proto`), using raw id allocation via `Client::allocate_id` + `Client::send_request(surface, 4 /* frame */, payload)` instead — a future slice promotes this to a typed interface once `pmd_callback` lands in `display-proto/src/objects.rs`. `BufferPool` is re-exported from `draw/mod.rs` and from `lib.rs` as `toolkit::BufferPool`. Test coverage at `crates/toolkit/tests/buffer.rs` (+5, lifted mock-server scaffold from `tests/window.rs`): `buffer_pool_new_sends_shm_create_pool_and_two_buffers` (mock; `App::connect` + `BufferPool::new(w=4, h=2)` emits three requests: shm.create_pool(pool_id, 64) + shm_pool.create_buffer(buf0, offset=0, 4, 2, 16, ARGB8888) + shm_pool.create_buffer(buf1, offset=32, 4, 2, 16, ARGB8888); accessors return `back_index=0`, `is_in_use=(false, false)`, `size=64`); `buffer_pool_acquire_paints_into_back_buffer` (acquire canvas; `canvas.clear(rgba(0x11,0x22,0x33,0xff))`; assert every 4-byte chunk in `pool.back_buffer()` is `[0x11,0x22,0x33,0xff]`); `buffer_pool_commit_and_swap_attaches_and_damages` (paint + `commit_and_swap(&mut window)`; outbound has three requests: surface.attach(buf0, 0, 0) + surface.damage(0, 0, 4, 2) + surface.commit(); `back_index` flipped 0 → 1, `is_in_use(0)=true`); `buffer_pool_handle_release_flips_in_use_flag` (paint + commit to mark buf0 in-use; `handle_release(buf0)` returns true + flips to free; re-call returns false (already free); `handle_release(ObjectId::new(9999))` returns false for unrelated id); `buffer_pool_double_buffered_ping_pong` (frame 1: paint red, commit, back_index=1, buf0 in-use; handle_release(buf0); frame 2: paint blue → bytes live at offset 32 (the buffer-1 region), commit, back_index=0, buf1 in-use; parse frame-2 outbound attach request — asserts `attach.buffer_id == pool.buffer_id(1)`). No kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes (all three requests used — shm_create_pool + shm_pool_create_buffer + surface_attach + surface_damage + surface_commit — pre-dated this slice in `protocol.rs`); no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `BufferPool` is a facade over `App`+`Window`+`protocol::Client`+`display-proto` and is independently replaceable; Principle VII (protocol over API) strengthens — every paint-cycle side effect routes through wire-protocol requests (shm.create_pool, shm_pool.create_buffer, surface.attach, surface.damage, surface.commit, surface.frame) rather than back-channel helpers, and the `Canvas::from_slice` plumbing lets userland paint directly into the pool's backing bytes without a copy step between paint and attach. Deviation introduced: `BufferPool::commit_and_swap` / `request_frame` API shape (single `&mut Window` vs the brief's `(&mut App, &Window)` pair) — documented in the `commit_and_swap` docstring as "Window already owns the mutable reference to its App, and the pool only needs the surface id from the window plus a mutable handle to the client"; this is the unique clean shape once Window borrows App mutably. Deviation closed: the T119 partial note flag "not started" (no `draw/buffer.rs` existed) is now "partial" with the buffer-pool machinery landed and the surface-attach-commit loop exercised end-to-end at test scope. **Architectural milestone**: the client-side paint → attach → commit cycle is now end-to-end at test scope — a user can `App::connect` → `Window::new` → `BufferPool::new` → `acquire_back_canvas` → `canvas.draw_text(...)` → `commit_and_swap(&mut window)` and the wire output matches the spec §§7–9 contract (shm pool setup once, buffer create twice, attach + damage + commit per frame). Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T116 `Widget` trait + primitives + `WindowFrame` — `BufferPool::acquire_back_canvas` returns the `Canvas<'_>` that `WindowFrame::draw(canvas)` paints into, closing the chrome-paint gap that's blocked widget work since the widget_label slice landed; (b) promote the frame-callback helpers to a typed `pmd_callback` interface — add `Interface::Callback` to `display-proto/src/objects.rs`, a `CallbackDone` event decoder, a `Client::bind_new(Interface::Callback)` pathway, and a `BufferPool::request_frame` that binds through the typed path; (c) land real SAB-backed shm — replace `BufferPool::pixels: Vec<u8>` with a SAB-backed byte slice shared between the toolkit-side pool and the display-server's buffer-read path, which closes the "v1 pushes pixel bytes inline as payload" deviation in `protocol.rs`; (d) migrate toolkit-free-client (T113) onto `App::connect` + `Window` + `BufferPool` — would collapse the hand-rolled `FreeClient` state machine to ~40 lines against the same facade stack every bundled app uses in T121+.

After the **T116 toolkit TextInput widget slice**: workspace **1739 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1220 → 1238 +18, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 28e77c9. Fourth slice against the Phase 2 Toolkit block (T114–T120) and the first widget with a keyboard input surface: `crates/toolkit/src/widget/text_input.rs` lands `TextInput` — a single-line editable text widget with cursor, `Idle` / `Hover` / `Focused` state, pointer hit-test + focus transition, and a `handle_key(Key) -> KeyOutcome` keyboard handler. Public API: `TextInput::new(bounds)`, `with_placeholder(&str)` builder, `bounds` / `text` / `caret` / `state` / `placeholder` accessors, `set_text` + `clear` mutators (both pin caret to end / 0), `set_state` direct setter plus `set_focus(bool)` / `set_hover(bool)` convenience setters (hover yields to focus — matches the Button stack WindowFrame uses for its close button), `hit_test` + `pointer_down` (press inside transitions to Focused and moves caret to end — click-to-position deferred to a future slice), `handle_key(Key) -> KeyOutcome` (see below), `visible_text` returning the leading slice that fits inside the interior after padding, and `paint(canvas, theme)`. **`Key` vocabulary**: `Char(char)` / `Backspace` / `Left` / `Right` / `Home` / `End` — minimal local enum gated on T114's future cross-widget keyboard event type. **`KeyOutcome` return vocabulary**: `Changed` (text content mutated), `CaretMoved` (caret moved without text change), `Ignored` (key not handled — out-of-focus, non-printable char, bounds-reached arrow) so apps can choose whether to re-render. **Keyboard semantics pinned by tests**: `Char(c)` inserts at caret when `0x20 <= c <= 0x7E` inclusive (printable ASCII that the bitmap font covers); non-printable (control codes, DEL 0x7F, tab, newline, non-ASCII like `é`) is `Ignored`; `Backspace` deletes the char immediately left of caret (walking by UTF-8 char boundary via `prev_char_boundary` helper), `Ignored` at caret=0; `Left` / `Right` move caret by one char boundary, `Ignored` at bounds; `Home` → caret=0, `End` → caret=text.len(), `Ignored` when already there. When not focused, every key is `Ignored`. **Theme additions**: `crates/toolkit/src/theme.rs` grows six new slots for the widget — `text_input_bg` / `text_input_bg_hover` / `text_input_bg_focused` / `text_input_fg` / `text_input_placeholder_fg` / `text_input_border` — populated on both `LIGHT` and `DARK` palettes. Light: white fill on idle/focused, near-white on hover, black body/caret fg, grey-ish placeholder, same border tone as `button_border`. Dark: near-black fill with a slightly lighter focused tone to distinguish focus state against the dark chrome. Picking six slots instead of reusing `window_background` + `button_*` keeps the input visually distinct from surrounding chrome and lets the settings app tune them independently without rippling into Button. **Paint details**: 1-pixel border via `stroke_rect` in `text_input_border`; interior fill via `fill_rect` in the state-matched slot; text / placeholder paints inset by `TEXT_INPUT_PADDING_X = 4` on the left + vertically centred within the interior (matches the Button / Label / Frame pattern); caret is a 1-pixel-wide vertical bar spanning `GLYPH_HEIGHT` rows at `text_x + chars_before_caret * CELL_WIDTH`, clamped to the right edge of the interior for v1's no-scroll trailing-clip behaviour; only drawn when `state == Focused`. Empty + unfocused → placeholder in `text_input_placeholder_fg`; empty + focused → blank (so the caret is unambiguously visible); non-empty → actual text clipped by `fit_text_to_width` on the `(width - 2 * (1 + TEXT_INPUT_PADDING_X))` budget. `crates/toolkit/src/widget/mod.rs` re-exports `Key + KeyOutcome + TextInput + TextInputState + TEXT_INPUT_PADDING_X` in sort-order-consistent fashion. Test coverage at `crates/toolkit/tests/widget_text_input.rs` (+18 tests, exceeds the brief's +7 minimum): construction (4 — new/with_placeholder/set_text/clear), hit-test + focus (3 — inside transitions to Focused, outside does not change state, empty-bounds never hits), state setters (1 — set_focus/set_hover precedence), key handling (5 — insert printable, ignore non-printable, ignore-when-not-focused, Backspace matrix, arrow/Home/End matrix), and paint (5 — deterministic pixels for known text including border corners + interior fill + 'a' glyph at expected (x,y) + caret bar + adjacent-pixel-still-fill sanity, placeholder paints on idle empty, idle-vs-hover fill colours differ, no-caret-when-not-focused, zero-bounds no-panic). tasks.md T116 partial note updates from "not started" to "partial" listing the four completed widgets (Alignment, WindowFrame, Label, Button, TextInput), noting TextInput as T116-advanced, and flagging `List` + `Container` as residual T116 scope. **What did not change**: no kernel / abi / Platform / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / Kernel public-method / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm or user-wasm rebuild needed; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no changes to `crates/toolkit/src/{app,protocol,window,draw/*}.rs`. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — `TextInput` is a pure `Canvas` + `Theme` consumer with no protocol affinity, matching the widget module doc's "pure client-side paint + hit-test helper" contract; Principle X strengthens — the widget is fully exercised by 18 in-process tests with no display server and no toolkit protocol client in the loop; Principle IX neutral — paint cost is a bounded number of `set_pixel` calls, no heap allocation in the hot path except the v1 `String::insert` / `replace_range` on `Char` / `Backspace` which rebuild the underlying `String` at O(n) per edit. Deviation introduced: the `Key` enum lives in `text_input.rs` rather than a shared `widget::event` or `widget::input` module — a future slice promotes it to a cross-widget vocabulary when T114's `App` starts routing real display-server keyboard events through the widget tree. Deviation closed: T116's "not started" flag on `TextInput` — the widget now lands with its full v1 feature set (cursor, state transitions, keyboard handlers, placeholder). **Architectural milestone**: first toolkit widget with a keyboard input surface. Together with Button's pointer-event surface and WindowFrame's chrome, the toolkit now has the three input-surface shapes every bundled app in Phase 3 (sh, files, edit, settings, sysmon, term) will compose against. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T116 `List` widget — scrollable vertical stack of rows with selection state + arrow/click navigation, consumes the same `Theme` + `Canvas` + `Key` vocabulary; (b) T116 `Container` widget — rect background + optional border + child clipping, the root for every app's content area; (c) T117 flex-style layout engine — row/column with gap + grow, lets `preferred_size` on widgets actually compose into a window layout instead of callers hand-computing rects; (d) promote `text_input::Key` to a shared `widget::event::KeyEvent` + `widget::event::PointerEvent` pair so T114's `App` has a single vocabulary to route; (e) multi-widget integration scenario — compose a Window + WindowFrame + Label + TextInput + Button into one paint call against a real `BufferPool::acquire_back_canvas` and assert wire-level `attach` + `damage` + `commit` output via the mock server already used by `tests/{window,buffer}.rs`.

After the **T121 minimal desktop shell paint-wallpaper slice**: workspace **1743 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1238 → 1242 +4, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit 5f9df69. First slice against the Phase 3 US1 "first boot to a usable desktop" block and the first bundled-app slice to compose the T114 `App` + T115 `Window` + T119 `BufferPool` + T118 `Canvas` toolkit stack into a runnable shell binary. `crates/shell/src/paint.rs` lands `run_shell<C: Connection>(connection, max_dispatch_iterations) -> Result<ShellExit, ClientError>` (re-exported from `shell::` as `run_shell` + `ShellExit` + `DEFAULT_WIDTH` + `DEFAULT_HEIGHT`): connect via `App::connect`, construct a `Window::new(&mut app)`, `set_title("PMos")`, `commit` to solicit the server's first configure, then loop up to `max_dispatch_iterations` times — dispatch window events each pass, exit immediately on `window.close_requested()`, on the first `is_configured()==true` observation allocate a `BufferPool::new(window.app_mut(), w, h)` sized to `window.configured_size()` (or the 640×480 default when the server defers), acquire the back canvas, `canvas.fill_rect(..., theme.window_background)` the wallpaper colour, drop the canvas borrow, and `pool.commit_and_swap(&mut window)`. A `painted: bool` guard prevents re-paint on subsequent iterations. Returns `ShellExit::CloseRequested` if the server emits `xdg_toplevel::close` during any dispatch pass, otherwise `ShellExit::IterationLimit` when the cap exhausts. The wallpaper slot is `theme.window_background` (reusing the existing light-theme `#f2f2f2` grey rather than adding a new `theme.wallpaper` slot per the brief's "pick an existing one that makes sense" instruction — keeps the Theme struct stable and gives the shell a visually neutral default that contrasts with future taskbar / launcher chrome). `crates/shell/src/main.rs` becomes a thin driver: an inert `println!("shell: toolkit paint-wallpaper slice — T121 partial")` so `just build` + existing userland integration tests don't regress, plus a `#[used] static _KEEP_RUN_SHELL: fn(MemoryConnection, u32) -> Result<ShellExit, ClientError> = run_shell::<MemoryConnection>` anchor that prevents `just build`'s DCE from stripping the paint-code path out of the WASM binary — resulting `dist/assets/bin/shell.wasm` is 48 KB (vs. the feared <1 KB strip-to-nothing). `crates/shell/src/lib.rs` adds `pub mod paint;` + re-exports. No new crate; no `Cargo.toml` edits (shell already depends on `toolkit` + `display-proto` + `abi`); no `Cargo.lock` change. Test coverage at `crates/shell/tests/paint_wallpaper.rs` (+4 tests): `shell_runs_and_paints_wallpaper_on_configure` (mock server seeds the three required globals then registers a trigger-on-request — release `xdg_toplevel::configure(serial=1, w=800, h=600)` once the shell has sent its initial `surface.commit` — and verifies `run_shell(conn, 5)` returns `ShellExit::IterationLimit`); `shell_paints_once_after_configure_and_commits_attach_damage_commit` (same setup plus a `RecordingConnection` that tees every outbound byte; post-run, parses all framed requests with `parse_request_headers` and asserts the wire-level invariants — ≥3 `registry.bind` on id 3, exactly 1 `xdg_toplevel.set_title` + 1 `xdg_toplevel.ack_configure(42)` on id 13, exactly 1 `surface.attach` + 1 `surface.damage` + ≥2 `surface.commit` on id 11 across the initial-commit + frame-commit sequence); `shell_exits_on_close_requested` (trigger releases `xdg_toplevel::close` instead of configure; `run_shell(conn, 10)` returns `ShellExit::CloseRequested`); `shell_connect_failure_surfaces_client_error` (mock omits compositor global; `run_shell` returns `Err(ClientError::MissingGlobal("pmd_compositor"))`). **Test-mock innovation**: the `MockConnection` supports **trigger-on-outbound-request** inbound delivery — a test pre-registers a `(object_id, opcode)` pair + inbound bytes via `push_inbound_on_request`, and the mock incrementally parses framed requests out of the outbound stream, releasing the pre-registered inbound batch onto the `recv` queue once a matching request lands. Required because `run_shell` takes the connection by value and owns it for the full loop — the test can't reach into the mock mid-run to push configure/close events the way the toolkit's `tests/window.rs` pattern does (which calls `connection_mut().push_inbound(...)` after the setup phase). Trigger-on-request sidesteps this by letting the mock emit the server's reply in reaction to a specific client step — in this case, the initial `surface.commit` (opcode 7 on surface id 11) triggers the configure event, matching the real protocol handshake. Duplicated inline (~100 lines) rather than lifted to `toolkit::testutil` per the brief's "prefer duplicating a few lines over cross-crate entanglement" guidance. **What did not change**: no kernel / abi / Platform / Filesystem / Vfs / FdObject / NodeType / Socket / Signal / KernelError / Cap variant additions; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild; no TS shim or runtime changes; no display-server / display-proto changes; no changes under `crates/toolkit/`. T121 partial note in `tasks.md` updates from "not started" to "partial — paint-wallpaper slice" listing what's landed + the explicitly-deferred scope (taskbar, launcher, click routing, `pmd_shell_manager` window list, real IPC wiring blocked on T110 protocol dispatch). Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the shell is a pure consumer of the `toolkit::*` public API + `display-proto` wire types, and the layering acceptance test will exercise an alternative shell binary against the same toolkit + display-server stack without touching any other layer; Principle VII (protocol over API) strengthens — every visible side effect (connect, surface create, toplevel create, title set, configure ack, buffer pool create, attach, damage, commit) routes through `display-proto` wire requests rather than back-channel helpers; Principle IX neutral — paint cost is a bounded `fill_rect` call per frame (one set_pixel per pixel at ARGB8888) plus a single `commit_and_swap` per dispatch cycle, with the `painted: bool` guard ensuring the shell doesn't re-issue wire traffic on idle ticks; Principle X strengthens — the paint-wallpaper slice is fully exercised by 4 in-process tests against a trigger-on-request mock server with no real IPC, no display server, and no kernel in the loop. Deviation introduced: test-mock's `trigger-on-outbound-request` pattern — new in this slice, not present in the toolkit's `tests/{window,buffer}.rs` equivalents (which can touch the connection post-construction); future bundled-app integration tests that `run_shell`-style consume the connection will likely copy this pattern. Deviation closed: T121's "not started" flag — the paint-wallpaper slice lands with its full v1 foundation (connect + window + paint + close exit) even though the residual scope (taskbar, launcher, click routing, real IPC) is explicitly deferred. **Architectural milestone**: first bundled-app slice to end-to-end compose the Phase 2 toolkit stack (T114 `App` + T115 `Window` + T119 `BufferPool` + T118 `Canvas`) into a runnable shell binary. The three open-ended residual items (taskbar, launcher, click) can now layer on top of this foundation without re-walking the connect/window/paint boilerplate. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T122 launcher content source — parse `/usr/share/applications/*.desktop` entries into an in-memory `Vec<LauncherEntry>` at startup + re-poll every 5 s; (b) T130 taskbar surface — a second `Window` anchored to the bottom edge of the display (blocked on `pmd_shell_manager.get_shell_toplevel` or a similar anchor request — which doesn't exist on the wire yet, would need a display-proto extension); (c) real IPC wiring — when T110's protocol dispatch lands, replace `main.rs`'s inert println with a `display_connect` syscall that returns an IPC fd wrapped in a `Connection` impl, then call `run_shell` against it (the production fn main body); (d) click-to-launch routing — requires `pmd_shell_manager` bound in `App::connect`'s required-globals list + a pointer event passthrough channel from `Window::dispatch`; (e) frame-callback-driven redraw — replace the `painted: bool` guard with a frame-callback loop so the shell can re-paint on theme changes or taskbar updates (needs the typed `pmd_callback` interface that T119's `request_frame` helper is currently using raw id allocation for).

After the **T123 minimal /usr/bin/sh REPL driver slice**: workspace **1754 passing, 0 failing** across all layers (Rust 1242 → 1253 +11, TS-unit 499 → 499, Playwright 2 → 2). Commit f6bff57. First slice against the Phase 3 US1 block to populate the CLI-shell binary surface the way T121 populated the desktop-shell binary — prior to this slice `crates/sh/src/main.rs` was a `Shell::eval`-fed line loop with no isolation-test entry point; the REPL lived entirely in the binary wrapper so its behaviour was only exercised via the T077 headless-shell gate (kernel-side, uses a faux inline shell because the kernel can't depend on userland) and the `integration-tests/tests/shell_over_kernel.rs` composition (which drives the shell through the kernel's `fd_read`/`fd_write` surface). This slice lifts the REPL loop into the library at `crates/sh/src/run.rs` so the binary's behaviour is testable in-process against `Cursor<Vec<u8>>` stdin + `Vec<u8>` stdout/stderr buffers. Public API: `pub fn run<R: BufRead, W: Write, E: Write>(stdin, stdout, stderr) -> ExitStatus` with `ExitStatus::{Eof, Exit(i32), IoError}` (and `ExitStatus::code() -> i32` translating the outcome into the process-level exit code the userland binary hands to `__wasi_proc_exit`). The loop prints `"$ "`, flushes, reads one newline-terminated line via `BufRead::read_line`, strips a trailing `\n`/`\r`, splits on whitespace (`str::split_whitespace` — no quoting, no escaping, no variable expansion; those land in T142), and dispatches the first token against the minimal four-builtin set in `dispatch_builtin`: `echo` joins remaining args with single spaces + newline to stdout; `exit [code]` returns `ExitStatus::Exit(code)` (default 0), with a strict `i32::from_str` parse that writes `sh: exit: <arg>: numeric argument required` to stderr and returns `Exit(2)` on non-numeric input (matches bash/dash's behaviour, deliberately stricter than the existing `Shell::eval` path which defaults garbage to 0 — the new surface is the userland-binary one, the library's `Shell::eval` semantic stays unchanged so `integration-tests` + `term` + the kernel's T077 gate keep their assertions green); `cd [path]` defaults to `/` when no arg, joins relative paths against a local `PathBuf` cwd tracked inside `run`, normalises `.`/`..`/repeated `/` via `std::path::Component`, and makes a best-effort `std::env::set_current_dir` call (wasip1 likely no-ops, so the local `PathBuf` is the source of truth for `pwd`); `pwd` writes the local cwd + newline to stdout. Unknown commands return `BuiltinOutcome::NotBuiltin` and the REPL writes `sh: command not found: <token>` to stderr and continues. EOF on stdin → `ExitStatus::Eof` (exit code 0); any stdio I/O error → `ExitStatus::IoError` (exit code 1). Blank lines are a no-op that just re-prints the prompt. `crates/sh/src/main.rs` collapses to a thin wrapper: locks `io::{stdin,stdout,stderr}`, wraps stdin in `BufReader`, calls `sh::run`, translates the returned `ExitStatus::code()` (i32) into a `process::ExitCode` (u8 via `rem_euclid(256)` clamp since the process-level exit surface is one byte). A default-on `std` feature in `crates/sh/Cargo.toml` gates the `run` module + the `ExitStatus` re-export; the existing `no_std + alloc` embedding path (the tokenizer + `Shell::eval` state machine consumed by `term` and `integration-tests`) stays available by setting `default-features = false` on the `sh` dep. No kernel, abi, Platform, Vfs, FdObject, NodeType, Socket, Signal, Kernel public-method signature changes; no new KernelError / Cap variants; no new errnos; no new opcodes; no wire-format changes; no kernel.wasm rebuild needed. Test coverage at `crates/sh/tests/shell.rs` (+11 new `run`-facing tests on top of the existing 26 `Shell::eval` tests — shell-tests binary 26 → 37, crate total 39 → 50, workspace total 1242 → 1253): `run_eof_exits_clean` (empty stdin; `run` returns `ExitStatus::Eof`; stdout is just the single prompt the loop emits before `read_line` hits EOF; stderr empty), `run_echo_writes_args_with_spaces_and_newline` (stdin `"echo hello world\n"`; `Eof` after the echo line; stdout contains `"hello world\n"`), `run_echo_with_no_args_emits_just_newline` (stdin `"echo\n"`; stdout is the exact sequence `"$ \n$ "` — prompt + bare newline + second prompt before EOF), `run_exit_zero_returns_exit_status` (stdin `"exit\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(0)`), `run_exit_with_code_returns_that_code` (stdin `"exit 42\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(42)`), `run_exit_with_invalid_code_returns_exit_two` (stdin `"exit notanumber\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(2)`, stderr contains `"numeric argument required"` + `"notanumber"`), `run_unknown_command_writes_to_stderr_and_continues` (stdin `"foo\nexit\n"`; returns `ExitStatus::Exit(0)`; stderr contains `"sh: command not found: foo"` — shell survived the unknown command and ran the next line), `run_pwd_writes_current_dir` (stdin `"pwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; strip the leading prompt and assert the first line-terminated chunk is an absolute path starting with `/`), `run_cd_changes_current_dir_and_affects_pwd` (stdin `"cd /tmp\npwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout contains `/tmp\n` — note: uses `contains("/tmp\n")` rather than a line-splitting assertion because the prompt never emits a newline so `stdout.lines()` yields prompt+output concatenations rather than clean lines), `run_blank_line_reprints_prompt` (stdin `"\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout is exactly `"$ $ "` — two prompts before the exit, no prompt after exit returns), `run_cd_with_no_args_returns_to_root_and_pwd_reflects_it` (stdin `"cd /tmp\ncd\npwd\nexit\n"`; exit 0; stdout contains `"/\n"` — pwd-after-reset shows root). Each test drives `run(BufReader::new(Cursor::new(input)), &mut stdout_vec, &mut stderr_vec)` and asserts against the exact `"$ "`-prompt-interleaved byte stream. Constitution check PASS on all ten; Principle II (layering) holds — the `run` module consumes only `std::io::{BufRead, Write}` + `std::path::PathBuf` + the crate's own `shell` / `tokenize` modules and could be swapped for an alternative REPL shape (e.g. a line-editor with history) without touching either the library state machine or the binary wrapper; Principle VI (standard syscall surface) neutral — the shell uses `std::env::set_current_dir` + `std::path` which route through WASI preview 1's `fd_prestat_get`/`path_*` syscalls if wasip1 supports them, or no-op on the local cwd otherwise; Principle X (testability) strengthens — the REPL loop is now isolation-testable in a strictly stronger way than the existing `Shell::eval` tests or the T077 kernel gate (those can't exercise the I/O-error path or the strict `exit` arg parsing, both of which are `run`-layer behaviour). Deviation introduced: strict `exit` arg parsing diverges from `Shell::eval`'s silent-default-to-0 path — the new behaviour matches POSIX shells and is exposed only via the `run` surface; the `Shell::eval` surface stays unchanged so `integration-tests/shell_over_kernel.rs` + `term::Terminal` keep their existing assertions green. Deviation closed: T123's "not started" flag — the minimal `/usr/bin/sh` now lands with echo/exit/cd/pwd + the REPL loop in library shape. `dist/assets/bin/sh.wasm` rebuilds to 49 KB from `just build`. **Architectural milestone**: first userland binary with its REPL loop lifted into the library layer, so behaviour is exercisable in-process against a byte-stream mock rather than through the kernel's fd surface. The pattern (library `run()` entry point + thin `main.rs` wrapper + `ExitStatus` outcome enum) is directly applicable to every bundled app in Phase 3+ that has a binary-level event loop — `/usr/bin/term` (T124) will call `run_terminal(app, spawn_child_fn)` from its own `lib.rs`, `/usr/bin/files` + `/usr/bin/edit` + `/usr/bin/settings` + `/usr/bin/sysmon` will follow. Next single-slice runway candidates: (a) T124 minimal `/usr/bin/term` — toolkit window + scrollback area + input line + `proc_spawn(/usr/bin/sh)` child + pipe its stdin/stdout through the Terminal's input line + scrollback (blocked on `proc_spawn` from userland, which needs an `abi::ext::proc_spawn` pmos_ext syscall in the WASI shim — see the init crate's existing `extern "C" { fn proc_spawn(...) }` import pattern); (b) T142 pipes/redirection in the sh binary — promote `run` to handle `|`, `<`, `>`, `>>` tokens, thread a `Pipeline` builder through the dispatcher, route each stage's fd 0/1/2 through an `ipc_pipe` syscall pair; (c) T146 coreutils bridge — wire `proc_spawn` into the `NotBuiltin` arm of `dispatch_builtin` so unknown-command tokens spawn a child from `/bin/<token>` or `/usr/bin/<token>` via path resolution; (d) T122 launcher content source (desktop-shell) — parse `/usr/share/applications/*.desktop` into an in-memory `Vec<LauncherEntry>` at startup + re-poll every 5 s; (e) T130 taskbar surface in the desktop shell — a second `Window` anchored to the bottom edge of the display (blocked on a display-proto surface-anchoring extension).
